# Carry-over backlog

Items this repo owes itself. An entry lives here when it is real, measured, and **not the
subject of any live plan** — the state that previously had no home, so it survived only by
being written into a plan about something else and vanished when that plan was discharged.

**This is the DISTRIBUTION's backlog, and it is not a push-candidate ledger.** A consumer's
`_bmad-output/ai-dlc-update/push-candidate-ledger.md` tracks what that consumer wants pushed
UPSTREAM to ai-dlc, and its receipts resolve against a pull's `theirs` ref with the verbs
`theirs_has` / `theirs_lacks`. This file tracks what ai-dlc owes ITSELF, its receipts resolve
against this working tree, and its verbs are `sh` / `has` / `lacks`. The two grammars are
mutually unreadable by each other's engine on purpose. Entry ids are `BL-`, never `PC-`.

**Read by** `scripts/backlog-reverify.sh`, which executes each entry's `verify:` receipt and
emits a status. **Rotated by** `scripts/backlog-rotate.sh`, which moves closed entries to
`docs/backlog.archive.md` — it moves, it never deletes. Neither ships; both are
distribution-only, as `core/fixtures/plan-shape/.dist-only` already is.

## Receipts

```
verify: sh <one-liner>              exit 0 = the fix is present -> CLOSE-CANDIDATE
                                    exit 9 = the receipt cannot measure its subject -> NEEDS-REVIEW
                                    any other non-zero = still reproduces -> STILL-LIVE
verify: has   <repo-rel-path> "<substr>"    close when the file CONTAINS the substring
verify: lacks <repo-rel-path> "<substr>"    close when the file LACKS it
verify: manual                      no mechanical predicate by design -> HAND-REVIEW
```

**Prefer `sh`.** The tree is right here and executable, which the consumer's ledger cannot
assume of the ref it greps. A receipt runs from the repo root with stdin closed, so it names
any input it reads as a file. A behavioural predicate asserts the defect itself and cannot be
anchored on prose the author invented to describe a wanted fix.

**An `sh` receipt that reads a tracked file asserts its anchor first.** Before it tests the defect,
it asserts on that same path something that exits 9 if absent: `grep -q '<identifier>' <path> ||
exit 9`, or the brace-group, `if !` or non-empty-extract forms of the same guard. This covers
`grep`, `awk`, `sed`, `cat`, `! grep`, a working-tree `git grep` and `git show HEAD:<path>`. A rename
inside the file then reads as exit 9 (NEEDS-REVIEW) instead of as a quiet exit 1. `[ -f <path> ]`
alone is not an anchor. The anchor pattern carries a real identifier of 4 or more characters, does
not match line 1 or a shebang, and the guard exits exactly 9. The anchor must survive the fix,
because R8 wants a closed receipt to exit exactly 0, and an anchor the fix removes fails the close
permanently. An irrelevant but real identifier is left to review. This is enforced by R10 of
`scripts/validate-backlog-receipts.sh` (ceiling 0, `--max-unanchored`). Only history reads at a
non-HEAD revision are exempt.

**THIS FILE'S `sh` POLARITY IS THE OPPOSITE OF THE CONSUMER LEDGER'S, AND THE TWO ARE WRITTEN
IN THE SAME SESSIONS.** Here, `scripts/backlog-reverify.sh:334-335` reads **exit 0 as "the fix
is present"** and non-zero as "still reproduces". In a consumer's push-candidate ledger,
`core/skills/ai-dlc-update/reconcile/ledger-reverify.sh:2929` reads it the other way — **exit 0
means the entry STILL REPRODUCES**, and non-zero proposes CLOSE-CANDIDATE. Carrying this file's
rule into a consumer receipt writes a predicate that proposes closing a LIVE defect, which is
the one direction that loses data permanently. Check which file your receipt lands in before
you fix its polarity, and read the emitter rather than either header.

**In a consumer receipt, guard the unresolvable subject too.** A RENAMED subject also exits
non-zero there, so a relocation reads as an absorption that never happened; `[ -n "$s" ] ||
exit 127` makes it NEEDS-REVIEW instead. This file's engine needs no such guard, because its
non-zero direction is the one that keeps the entry open.

**When you must use `has`/`lacks`, anchor on a token the fix CANNOT BE WRITTEN WITHOUT** — a
flag, a path, a function name — never a phrase describing the fix. The consumer's engine
detects that error by reading a third ref; this one has no third ref to read, so the rule is
enforced by the author and by review, not by the tool. `core/fixtures/ledger-reverify-unfalsifiable/README.md`
is the measurement: 13 entries on the reference consumer carried predicates that could never
have gone green, and would have reported "still open" forever.

**A closed entry is annotated in place and left for rotation**, in the form
`**LANDED (v<version>, verified <sha>).**` — the annotation FORM is what the rotator keys on,
never the word anywhere in prose, because an entry that merely discusses landing something is
not a closed entry.

## Pipeline-efficiency ranked set (efficiency ranks 1–14)

Filed from [`docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s318.md`](analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s318.md),
section **"Ranked defects"**. The rank is that section's order and each entry names its row as
`[efficiency rank N]`. Costs are API-list-equivalent over graph sprints 317 and 318, in-session
only (handoff gaps are excluded by operator ruling). Shared cost is counted once, under the higher
rank. Defects first exposed while the local `qwen38flash-mlx` model led are pipeline fixes toward
the operator's local-lead goal, never findings against the model.

**Measuring the change.** A receipt closes an entry on the tree. Whether the fix moved anything is
measured afterwards on consumer transcripts, re-running `pipeline-efficiency-s317-s318/scripts/` over
the sprints after the fix lands. graph is the reference, not the only population:
`magic-bonus-calc-engine` also runs ai-dlc (0.780.0) and has its own transcripts. Measure on every
consumer that has run the fixed release, and name the population beside each figure.

## BL-538 — the lead takes a full turn for every agent notification while an armed join already covers it

**PARTIAL (v0.794.0, verified 8aab009d) — the prose half shipped; the hook and the measurement are OPEN.** rule-28 states the lead's response to a notification a live beat covers (no status line, no re-check) and the two that are not covered. Correction at v0.796.0: 0.794.0 said "arming the beat ships with the wait release"; that release's rule-28 paragraph arms the serial remediator's join with `--complete-repair`, which is BL-541's subject, and carries nothing for this entry. Still owed: the optional hook that injects the response when the notified agent's deliverable is inside a live beat's path set, and the per-wake count re-measured on the next consumer sprint.

**DEFECT (efficiency).** In graph s319 lead session `62eadbbe-59c3-436d-bf7c-931ebc09ecba`, measured at 22:47Z: 109 `<task-notification>` wakes, of which 61 ended with no tool call and only a restatement that the join was still armed ("X delivered … the join beat is still armed, so I'm waiting"). Each such turn re-reads the lead's whole context. That context ran 250–320K tokens (the sensor fired yellow at 252,985 and the snapshot records red readings of 317,305 and 324,185), and by 21:37Z the session had already spent 61 M tokens of lead cache read over 272 API calls. The waits it restates are `wait-for-deliverable.sh --complete` beats over the whole round, which return on their own. Core says what a notification MEANS for the cap-release rule (`rule-28.md:195`) but nowhere what the lead should DO with one that arrives while a beat covers that agent's deliverable. The analysis this backlog is filed from ranks lead waiting among the largest costs (`docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s318.md`, headline and "Relation to the v0.380.0 investigation").

Owed: a stated lead response to a notification for an agent whose deliverable an armed beat covers: no per-agent status line, no re-check, end the turn. Optionally, a hook that injects that instruction when the notified agent's deliverable is inside `.beat-inflight`'s path set. Measure it by re-running the per-wake count on the next consumer sprint.

**Second reading (f7 measured; transcript-only, not re-derived here).** In graph's s319 lead session `c61a91eb`, 20 of 29 task-notification wakes ended with no tool call. Method, f7's heuristic: a wake counts as ending with no tool call when no `tool_use` block appears before the next user line. This is NOT the same series as the filing's 61 of 109, which is a different session (`62eadbbe`), a different count of wakes, and was classed by whether the turn only restated that the join was armed; the two ratios (20/29 and 61/109) are two readings and are not a trend. Both say most wakes under an armed beat end without acting.

**Sharper reading (f7 measured; transcript-only, not re-derived here).** In graph's s319 lead session `c61a91eb`, on 0.828.0, one 25-agent SM seat ran under a single armed join (task `brrt6rqjh`). Between 14:40:41Z and 14:48:20Z (transcript lines 2702-2838) the lead took 14 consecutive wakes whose entire output was "Covered by the armed join; no action." Each was a full turn over a context of about 318k tokens or more, past the RED threshold. By f7's heuristic, the same method as the paragraph above, idle wakes went from 20 of 37 to 37 of 54: 17 in about 8 minutes on one round. The round plans 4 seats of about 25 agents each, so about 70 such turns are expected per round; that figure is f7's projection, not a count. Reading: the 0.794.0 prose half shortened each turn's output but cannot remove the turns; only the hook half can.

**Evidence (HANDOFF, 0.876.0).** The precondition for the hook half is in: the architect, analyst and ux deliverables now carry every item their return carried (`## Architect handoff`, `## Gaps`, `## Design decisions`), and SubagentStop keeps each final message in `_bmad-output/.subagent-final/<agent_id>.txt`. No hook is installed and this entry stays open.

**Evidence (HOOKWAKE).** The hook half ships as a UserPromptSubmit hook, `core/hooks/ai-dlc-hookwake.sh`, that BLOCKS a subagent hand-back while that agent's wait beat is outstanding. It joins on the `.wait-beats/.beat-argv-<pid>` record `core/scripts/wait-for-deliverable.sh` writes at arm time, with the start time read through the shared `core/scripts/wfd-start-of.sh`, and `core/fixtures/hookwake` drives it in both layouts. That is a different shape from the "hook that injects that instruction" this entry names: it refuses the wake rather than telling the lead how to answer it. The measurement half, a re-taken wake count on a consumer lead session, is still OPEN, so the entry stays PARTIAL.

**Progress (batch 240).** The flip half needs a redesign before it returns to the operator: `wait-for-deliverable.sh` records dispatch-to-path pairs at arm time, J4 becomes per-agent, C5 gets a guaranteed carrier, and the design is checked against a captured real UserPromptSubmit payload.

**Progress (batch 241; the entry stays OPEN, `verify: manual` unchanged, no LANDED line).** The hook half is retired by operator ruling 2026-10-23, and `core/hooks/ai-dlc-hookwake.sh` and its fixture are removed in this release. Retiring the hook does not touch this entry's subject, the full lead turn per notification that an armed beat already covers, so that defect and its per-wake re-measure on a consumer lead session are still owed. The "Evidence (HOOKWAKE)" and batch-240 paragraphs above describe a mechanism that no longer ships.

**Third reading (batch 243, c5; transcript-only; the entry stays PARTIAL).** The s320 lead work spans four transcripts under `~/.claude/projects/-Users-n8-git-graph/`: `719a49a0` (the opening session, 133 dispatches), `d71fcbce` (a short resume, 4), `d22c7889` (the main s320 lead, 539) and `04c0da6d` (the latest resume, 35). They were identified as the non-sidechain sessions whose first human turn is `/ai-dlc`. `888e1365` (the s319 lead) and `913d04cc` were rejected for carrying almost no s320 mentions, and `fa7ce946` for being an `/ai-dlc-update` session. Under f7's heuristic (a wake ends with no tool call when no `tool_use` block appears before the next user line), the wakes and the no-tool-call wakes per transcript are 125/91, 7/1, 482/355 and 34/25, which sum to 472 of 648 (73%). The same counter reproduces the s319 `c61a91eb` window of 37 of 54 exactly (up to transcript line 2838) but does NOT reproduce the window of 20 of 29 (its nearest reading is 23 of 40 at line 2702). The 73% figure is therefore a FLOOR OF UNKNOWN ACCURACY, not an exact count. The wake definition (a non-sidechain user line carrying the task-notification tag that is not a `tool_result`) is this counter's own, and a raw tag count disagrees with it: 2482 occurrences against 482 parsed wakes in `d22c7889`, because the tag also appears in queued and quoted lines. About 112 of the 472 idle wakes explicitly restate that an armed join or beat covers them; that number is a TEXT-REGEX APPROXIMATION, not a classification of each turn. One example is `d22c7889` line 716: "Covered by the armed join; nothing to do until it reports." The mechanism that would remove these turns has no design since the hook was retired, so the entry stays PARTIAL and its verify line is unchanged.

Decision rule: the step-value ruling, "A step with no measured value goes" (`.claude/rules/operator-rulings.md`), cited per BL-817.

**Progress (batch b248; measured by the batch b248 lead, ai-dlc-f8; the entry stays PARTIAL).** The round-dispatcher design is a small subagent that owns each wide round and returns to the lead once; the operator chose to probe it before building. Probe 1 (Claude Code 2.1.316): a subagent spawned 6 background agents, all 6 completion notifications reached that subagent and 0 reached the parent session, so notifications route to the spawning subagent. Probe 2 (headless `claude -p`, haiku): with CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_CONCURRENT_SUBAGENTS=2, the child's dispatcher took slot 1 and its first nested spawn took slot 2, and the 2nd and 3rd nested spawns were REFUSED, not queued, with `Concurrent subagent limit reached. You can run 2 subagents at once. Do not retry. ...`; the control at cap 10 accepted all 4 spawns. So the cap counts every live subagent at any nesting depth, and the top-level session takes no slot. Consequence for the design: a dispatcher holds one slot for its whole round, so it reduces the lead's wakes but not its concurrency; it must size its waves to the cap minus every other live subagent, and an over-cap spawn fails hard, so its "Do not retry" text must be handled as a refusal, not as a failed agent. The raw evidence is two stream-json scratch files, `cap2.jsonl` and `cap10.jsonl`, which are not committed, so these figures cannot be re-derived from the tree.

**Progress (batch b249, c5, 0.1000.0; the entry stays PARTIAL until its outcome line).** Re-measure on s321, counted by f7's heuristic (a wake counts as ending with no tool call when no `tool_use` block appears before the next user line) over four graph lead transcripts, `2c84f6b7`, `59a855bd`, `d9d2b30c` and `38d6a63b`, all entrypoint `cli` on Claude Code 2.1.312 to 2.1.317: 270 task-notification wakes, of which 150 were idle, 56%. The control is that the same counter, run on the s319 `c61a91eb` window, reproduces 37 idle of 54 exactly. Split by kind with the contract adversary's classifier, 197 wakes were agent exits and 153 of them idle, while 81 were background-command completions and only 2 of them idle, so the idle turns are almost entirely agent-exit notifications. Two 28-agent rounds took 20 and 16 idle wakes in their windows; those windows run to the next round and therefore over-count. Correction to the batch-b248 paragraph's probe 1: on Claude Code 2.1.317 a child's completion notification reaches its spawner only while the spawner is running. A spawner that ends its turn with live background children sends its parent an interim notification ("... is waiting on its own background work", status `completed`) and is re-woken once per child, while a spawner held inside a single foreground call until every child finished woke its parent exactly once. Measured interactively with 3 children, the background-beat variant woke the top session 2 times and the foreground variant once, and a headless run agrees. Nested agents' meta files land flat in the top session's `subagents/` directory with `parentAgentId` and `spawnDepth: 2`, and share the top session's `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`; a child given a `name` becomes an in-process teammate at `spawnDepth` 1. This release ships the round-dispatcher: Rule 28 "Split dispatch", the 28(a) carve-out, the `_dispatch-protocol.md` "Round-dispatcher brief" section, the handoff guard counting a dispatcher's members, and the `foreground-budget-deny` fixture pins that allow (a subagent's foreground `AI_DLC_WAIT_BEAT_SECS=540 ... wait-for-deliverable.sh` call at `timeout: 600000` is allowed, a poll-shaped twin is denied, the same call from the lead is denied). Its scope is the adversarial-pass, elicitation and party-seat rounds; review/QA, repair, gate-adjudicator and dev rounds stay lead-dispatched. The raw probe transcripts are scratch and uncommitted, so the probe figures above cannot be re-derived from the tree. Under the batch-147 ruling no model replay closes a prompt fix; the close is the structural trace plus the pin fixture named by the entry's `verify:` line, and the per-wake count on the next consumer sprint is the outcome measurement, recorded when taken and not a close condition. The entry is tagged `**DEFECT (efficiency).**`, so under BL-817 it closes only with that `Outcome measured:` line, taken on the first graph sprint that runs this release; until then it stays PARTIAL. NOTE: the dispatcher runs on the lead's model, holding member brief paths and spending its round inside foreground waits; a team-roles binding (role file, aiDlcRoles row, definition) is the remedy if its cost shows up.

**Provisional reading (batch b255, F2; s322 is INCOMPLETE, about 3 of 8 stories; the entry stays PARTIAL, `verify: manual` unchanged, no LANDED line; RE-TAKE ON s322 COMPLETE).** The counter was rebuilt from this entry's own definition and committed as `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s322/scripts/idle_wakes.py` (stdlib only, read only on the transcripts), invoked as `python3 -I docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s322/scripts/idle_wakes.py [--upto LINE] <transcript>...`. A wake is a non-sidechain user line carrying the `<task-notification>` tag that is not a `tool_result`; it is idle when no `tool_use` block appears before the next user line. Control, same script: graph s319 lead `c61a91eb`, `--upto 2838`, reads 37 idle of 54 wakes (the recorded 37/54 exactly), and `--upto 2702` reads 23 of 40 (the third reading's recorded nearest figure). Reading, the six graph lead transcripts whose sessions are s322 (all entrypoint `cli`, 2026-11-09 to 2026-11-11, first s322 mention at line 131 or 22; `6c14d949`, `80c96d42`, `9a646a3b`, `fdc4249a`, `70fd1d6d`, `6249c621`): wakes/idle 137/34, 5/0, 162/64, 60/6, 49/17, 64/8, which sum to 129 idle of 477 wakes (27%), against s321's 150 of 270 (56%). Agent-exit wakes alone: 115 idle of 274 (42%), against s321's 153 of 197 (78%); background-command wakes 14 idle of 203. The direction is the one the entry predicts (fewer idle lead turns per notification), and s322 ran after the 0.1000.0 round-dispatcher shipped (graph reconciled 0.1028.0 on 2026-11-09 and 0.1036.0 and 0.1044.0 afterwards; nested `spawnDepth: 2` agents exist in `6c14d949` (52) and `9a646a3b` (40) and in none of the others). It does NOT close the entry, for three reasons. (1) The measure is a per-wake share over a sprint's rounds, and s322 has run only its early steps, so its mix of wide party and adversary rounds is not the s321 mix it is compared with; the share can move either way once the later rounds run. (2) The two transcripts that used dispatchers read 98 idle of 299 (33%) and the four that did not read 31 of 178 (17%), so this reading does not isolate the dispatcher as the cause, and 129 idle full-context turns remain. (3) A close-form `Outcome measured:` line needs lead and subagent seat figures, token-equivalent, API and gap-inclusive, from `seats.py` over the COMPLETE sprint; those are not taken here. Not measured: the seat cost of the s322 lead transcripts (available, deferred to the complete sprint); the sprint's per-round split (available). The identification of the six transcripts is by s322 mentions and `/ai-dlc` first turn; `6c14d949` opens at the end of s321 carry-over and so may carry a few s321 wakes.

**Progress (batch b249, c5, party rounds; operator ruling 2026-11-03 "Include in c5 (Recommended)").** Sharded party rounds are now per-seat role-bound `Agent` spawns that a round-dispatcher may run, while an unsharded round stays on the `/bmad-party-mode` Skill, and Rule 20 gains shape (v). The record of a sharded seat is the dispatch guard's spawn-ledger row, written by the hook, plus the seat's `--mark` line; seats write no provenance block, Check 17 runs no arm on a seat file, and a block stamped on afterwards is a forged record. `join-remediator-shards.sh` refuses a sharded seat file that has no role-bound row of that sprint, role and shard. It judges only when the installed `_gate-procedures.md` carries the new record sentence and otherwise prints `SEAT-ROWS: SKIP`, because the engine reaches a consumer at pull step 3 and the rulebook only at the reconcile merge. Ground truth from graph, measured read only: s321's 56 seat files carry 0 party provenance blocks, against 164 in s318 and 130 in s320, and all 56 seat spawns have role-bound, contract-cited ledger rows; the s321 architecture party round had 28 seats. s318's carry-over gate failed Check 17 on 40 blockless seat files, after which a remediator stamped 40 blocks with `tool_use_id: NOT_ACCESSIBLE`. Measured with the shipping function on copies, the false-positive set with the boundary in place is 0 refused in s318, s320 and s321, because graph's installed rulebook lacks the sentence. With the boundary forced on, it is 0 in s321 (56 files), 87 of 295 in s318 and 152 of 280 in s320; those are pre-ruling rounds (the Skill path, or per-group `cross/<K> g1/<G>` shards) that the boundary exists to skip. NOTE: A party round whose findings need no repair is never joined, so its seats are never checked. NOTE: Spawn-ledger rows carry no step, so a seat also matches rows from another round of the same sprint. K is therefore taken as the shard count common to every row-bearing seat of the round, the smallest when several are shared. A round sharing none is refused, naming its Ks. The literal reading, refuse when rows disagree on K, was rejected because it refused both of graph's s321 party rounds, whose union of row Ks is {6, 13}. A row from another round with the same role, ordinal and K can still cover a hand-written seat. NOTE: A round is judged only if its latest matched spawn-ledger row (or, without one, its seat files' newest add commits) is at or after the oldest commit whose `git log -S` adds the seat-row sentence to the installed `_gate-procedures.md`. An earlier round prints `SEAT-ROWS: SKIP legacy-round <step>`, and an unreadable date skips with its reason. A seat-ledger row older than the install dates its round but never covers a seat or names a K. A round with no row at or after the install is dated by its seat files when every seat file is committed and clean, each by its newest add commit, so a seat deleted and re-added after the install is judged: it is legacy only if all of them predate the install, and otherwise it is judged. The seat list is the scan's own sharded-seat parse, so an unsharded `<step>-<role>.md` or another step's seat beside the round does not enter it. A round with any uncommitted seat, or any seat that is modified, staged or untracked in one `git status --porcelain` over its seats, prints `SEAT-ROWS: SKIP round-undatable <step> <n> seats` and is judged once its seats are committed. A round is therefore judged whenever any of its matched rows is at or after the install, which closes the case where a new round in the same sprint matches an older round's rows. Measured on copies of graph's s318 records with a simulated install commit dated after 2026-10-14T16:04:44Z, which is when graph commit 28a15fce4 first added all 295 s318 seat files: `requirements-party-repair` (36 seat refusals at e49446c61) and `stories-party-repair-p1-d3` (19) skip as legacy rounds with rc 0 there. Graph itself has no install commit today, so on graph the join prints the rulebook-sentence `SEAT-ROWS: SKIP` line instead. s321 architecture joins its 28 seats with 0 refusals. NOTE: `sprint-review.md` says its party round is always sharded and has no line for a one-story or SERIAL map, unlike `carry-over-evaluation.md`. NOTE: The resident Rule 20 stub in `SKILL.md` names the sub-skills' `mode: subagent` provenance and does not mention shape (v). The full Rule 20 text it loads does. NOTE: `receipt-bl538.sh` creates a `mktemp -d` and does not remove it. (`receipt-bl538.sh` was deleted in 0.1019.0; the `verify:` line now runs the fixture.) NOTE: A round in which every seat bypassed the dispatch guard and every seat file is still uncommitted at join time is skipped as undatable, not refused. NOTE: A re-run that overwrites committed pre-install seat files and then COMMITS the overwrite, with no post-install row, still reads `legacy-round`, because the seats are dated by their add commits and a committed modification is not an add.

**Progress (batch b249, c5, seat file on the ledger row; lead ruling).** This closes a defect in the seat-row join described above. Spawn-ledger rows carry no step, so re-joining an earlier step of a sprint that straddles the install matched a later step's post-install rows, judged the earlier round and refused its seats. It was measured on copies of graph's s321 ledger with the install dated 2026-11-01: `requirements-party-repair` gave rc 2 with 14 seat refusals, where it had read legacy before the per-round change. The dispatch guard now writes `mark_file` onto each spawn-ledger row: the last `party-mode/s<N>/<basename>` tail in the brief's single `--mark` invocation, stopping at a blank or a quote, so a blank in the consumer's root cannot cut it, or the path as written when it carries no such tail. It is written only when exactly one `--mark` invocation is present, and it is not proof that the file exists. The seat-row join matches a row carrying it ONLY to the seat file whose path ends in that `party-mode/s<N>/<basename>` tail. That row never enters step, ordinal or K inference, and it covers its seat only when its role matches, it is role-bound, and it is stamped at or after the install. A pre-install field-row dates its seat and covers nothing. One pair of surrounding quotes is stripped. A `mark_file` with no usable tail names no seat file and is read as a field-less row, and it infers only for seats that no usable-tail row names. Rows from an older guard keep the field-less inference. Measured read only on graph's lead transcripts, across 140 party-seat spawns (s321 28+28, s320 70, s319 14): the brief's `--mark` path, reduced to its `party-mode/s<N>/<basename>` tail, equals the file the seat wrote in 140 of 140, and the negative control (the next seat's writes) is 0 of 140. 112 of the 140 paths are absolute, which is why the match is by tail and never by root. The guard was driven base against tip over 5309 real graph Agent payloads: decisions are identical on 5309 of 5309, rows are identical on 5288 of 5288 once `mark_file` is set aside, `mark` is kept on all 1344 rows that carried it, and no fork is added. On graph's real ledger, which has 0 `mark_file` rows, all 43 field-less join runs read identically before and after. With the architecture rows given `mark_file`, the DEFECT-1 world reads `SKIP legacy-round requirements`, rc 0. 0 of 1969 real `--mark` briefs quote their path, and 182 of 182 real seat-tail rows carry the seat's own role. NOTE: a field-row is matched on its seat basename alone, so a post-install field-row from an earlier run of the same step covers the re-run's same-named seat at any K, and a round whose seats are all field seats has no K to disagree on. NOTE: the tail's `s<N>` decides the sprint, and the row's `sprint` field is not read for a field-row; on the real ledger they agree on 184 of 184. A delta adversary measured the first guard on a consumer root containing a blank: `mark_file` read `/Users/n8/My`, a tail-less row, and on the s321 copy the requirements round refused 14 seats; with the tail extracted it reads `round-undatable` or `legacy-round`, rc 0, and an unquoted, single-quoted and double-quoted path all record the bare tail.

## BL-583 — the push suite's non-mutant poles have not been judged on value, and no per-gate wall clock or dollar figure has been measured

**NOTE (efficiency).** Re-tiered from DEFECT: Phase 2 (v0.842.0) retired every mutation battery, so the remaining owed work (judging the non-mutant poles, a per-gate wall clock and dollars) is measurement, not a defect. Operator-raised, 2026-10-16 ~04:30 EDT: "gating a release is so expensive, like stupidly expensive to run the push suite". Filed by peer session ai-dlc-68 (listed as ai-dlc-4e [e15800]). Assigned to ai-dlc-a6: phase 1 is a read-only census of where kill sets overlap, phase 2 is confirmation in the lead's slots after H-b. ai-dlc-bb's world-cache release already targets the `review-shard-merge-mutants` pole, so this entry does not count that saving twice. Fixture COST is now BL-878's subject; this entry keeps two things: whether each non-mutant pole catches a defect class no other unit catches (the value judgment), and the per-gate wall clock and dollars from a real release push.

Measured from `.git/ai-dlc-fixture-durations` in the main checkout, a LOADED-cost record that each push rewrites:
- Filer, at 04:29 EDT (288 rows): sum 28783 s (~8.0 h; ~40 min per full run on a pool of 12); 41 mutant rows at 12398 s, 43%; 12 of the top 15 rows mutant batteries at 583-737 s, the three non-mutant rows `snapshot-archive-rotate` 717 s, `suite-pole-guard` 493 s and `readset-skip-d` 410 s (corrected from 13 after lead ai-dlc-9c re-derived it); 130 rows at 60 s or more, 71 at 120 s or more, 82 under 10 s.
- Intake, re-derived at 06:18 EDT (288 rows, the record rewritten since): sum 27473 s; 41 mutant rows at 11346 s, 41%; 10 of the top 15 are mutant batteries (`derivation-capture-mutants` 737, `review-shard-merge-mutants` and `-b`/`-c` 728-729, `adversarial-shard-merge-mutants` 694, `remediator-shard-join-mutants` and `-b`/`-c`/`-d` 583-589, `updater-session-signals-mutants` 367), and the five non-mutant rows are `suite-pole-guard` 493, `readset-skip-d` 453, `ledger-reverify` 409, `-b` 392 and `-d` 362. Fixture directories: 290 (`/usr/bin/find core/fixtures -mindepth 1 -maxdepth 1 -type d`).
The two readings differ because the record moves with every push. The shape holds across both: the mutant batteries dominate the top of the rank.

Owed, under the step-value ruling in `.claude/rules/operator-rulings.md` ("A step with no measured value goes") applied to fixtures — a fixture or mutant shard stays only on evidence, a named defect class it catches that no other unit catches; without that the batch retires or merges it, and it is not put to the operator as a choice:
1. Rank by cost first: the mutant batteries, then the non-mutant poles.
2. For each sharded battery, measure whether its mutants kill anything the base fixture's arms do not; shards of one battery that kill the same set are redundant.
3. State the gate wall clock removed per release and the dollars beside it.

Caveat: the record is a loaded-cost set from recent runs, not a solo timing, and a content-keyed push runs only part of it. The per-gate figure needs the gate log of a real release push (0.794.0 onward). Related: BL-558, BL-592, CLAUDE.md "A wall clock is a defect until it is justified".

Scheduled in batch 235 for a later release (scope ruling: the boundary is the release).

**Partial, v0.810.0.** Item (a), the sandbox re-trace of 13 of the 14 stale fixtures, landed in intake 2 (`52555359`). Items (b) and (c) land here: `fold-architect-ledger-join-mutants` runs C0 and the M6 premise in shard `a` only, bound by a J2 arm, and `derivation-capture-mutants` is four pool units with a derived deal and coverage join (J0, J1, J1b) and no redundant control. Open: the remaining phase-2 items (gate-adjudication M0 in one shard, the readset-skip-digest and advisor-gate-deny controls in one shard each, the ledger-reverify-shard control) and the per-gate wall clock and dollars, which need the gate log of a real release push.

**Gate-log evidence (f7 measured, graph session `f78aa565`; transcript-only, not re-derived here).** The graph push of the reconcile commit took 17.6 minutes in pre-push with 139 of 224 fixtures selected. That is a real release-class push on a consumer tree and a first data point toward the per-gate wall clock this entry owes; it is a consumer's selected subset, not this repo's full-suite figure, and the two must not be compared.

**Item (b), batch 236.** `gate-adjudication-mutants` (M0) and `readset-skip-digest-mutants` (unmutated and impossible-anchor controls) now run their controls in shard `a` only, bound by a J2 once-only arm with a two-direction self-probe, and a D14 arm every shard runs holds each shard's `inputs.decl`/`tools.decl` equal to `a`'s apart from the driver lines (also added to `fold-architect-ledger-join-mutants`). `advisor-gate-deny-mutants` keeps its per-shard control because its shards' declarations differ (`-d` adds `od` and `tee`; `-b`/`-e` declare sibling directories whole), and `ledger-reverify-shard-mutants` keeps its C-a and C-b controls because it is one unit whose kill list is 3 in `a` and 1 in `b`, so `b`'s control is the only one for its mutants. Item (b)'s receipt (inert: its subject batteries `gate-adjudication-mutants` and `readset-skip-digest-mutants` were retired in v0.842.0, and run in a scratch copy it exits 9; it is kept as history and is not a live `verify:` line): `for f in gate-adjudication-mutants readset-skip-digest-mutants; do case $f in gate*) r='/^# M0 /,/^# m1 /' ;; *) r='/^NCTL=0$/,/^# J2: both/' ;; esac; t=$(mktemp) || exit 9; sed -n "${r}p" core/fixtures/$f/run.sh | sed '$d' > $t; [ -s $t ] || exit 9; S=$(grep -m1 '^SHARDS=' core/fixtures/$f/run.sh | cut -d'"' -f2); [ -n "$S" ] || exit 9; first=${S%% *}; for s in $S; do c=$(GROUP=$s C=0; score() { C=$((C+1)); }; dg_all() { C=$((C+1)); }; broken() { :; }; ok() { :; }; bad() { :; }; DG_WORLDS=; POOL=/dev/null; FX=x; HOOK=h; ROOT=r; . $t >/dev/null 2>&1; echo $C); w=0; [ "$s" = "$first" ] && w=1; [ "$c" = "$w" ] || { rm -f $t; exit 1; }; done; rm -f $t; done`. The entry stays open for the per-gate wall clock and dollars.

**Phase 2, v0.842.0.** Every mutation battery is retired: 56 units in 24 batteries, removed because none has a catch on record. A census of every record on `origin/main` that names a battery found only drift reds (re-anchored mutants, re-declared kill sets, scorer timeouts, the battery's own bugs) and one red that was not the battery's: BL-181, a subject locale bug the shipping fixture `gate-remediation-deny` also caught. In the main checkout's loaded-cost record the 56 units were 30380 of 54724 fixture-seconds (56%), and the pole moves from `adversarial-shard-merge-mutants` at 2214 s to `document-partition` at 732 s. The CHANGELOG for 0.842.0 carries the per-battery table. Items (b) and (c) above described those batteries' controls and shards; they no longer exist. Open: the non-mutant poles (`document-partition`, `enforcement-map-sites`, `apply-restamp-worklist-c` and the rest of the top of the record) have not been judged on value, and no per-gate wall clock or dollar figure has been measured.

**Evidence: the read-set fan-out of a runner change.** On a consumer's 0.830.0 to 0.834.0 reconcile push (graph transcript 5d4fbc59), the first push took about 98 minutes and ran 128 of 224 fixtures. 122 of them were woken because the pull changed runner and tooling files: `hermetic-run.sh`, `.githooks/pre-push`, `apply.sh` and `derive-fixture-readsets.sh`. The re-push took about 6 minutes and ran 42 of 224. Load was 77 to 85, so these are loaded figures. No dollar figure was measured. This is BL-583's subject, not BL-694's.

**Progress (batch 240).** Re-scoped to the poles not covered by BL-699 or BL-755 (BL-755 is archived, in `docs/backlog.archive.md`).

**Progress (batch 256, M6; the entry stays open, `verify: manual` unchanged, no LANDED line).** Both owed figures are measured from recorded logs, with no timing run, in [`docs/analysis/push-gate-cost-and-pole-value-b256.md`](analysis/push-gate-cost-and-pole-value-b256.md). No phase header in the surviving logs carries a duration, so the whole push is the per-gate figure. Dollars: the pushing session's spend inside its push window, deduped by `message.id`, median $0.167, max $2.37, sum $17.61. That is session spend, not a gate cost. Of the 10 FAIL lines in the 8 red pushes, 2 were real catches (`validator-arm-selection`, `handoff-record-carries-return`). 7 branch-delete pushes ran the whole hook, 144 to 258 s from start to the suite-key HIT for the 6 whose start m6-583a's report gives; filed as BL-1010. Retiring any pole is a separate release with its own contract, so this entry stays open.

Population: the 32 push logs of this repo in the batch-257 lead scratchpads that print a whole-suite read-set line (2026-11-13T09:51 to 2026-11-15T00:49), 2070 dispatched unit runs, priced at the main checkout's `.git/ai-dlc-fixture-durations` (a loaded record) at 97320 fixture-seconds. 321 runs had no cost row and count 0, so the total is a floor. Built here, counts from one counted run each (C16; before is the committed decl at d619fe62a, after is this unit's tree f6f44898a at `AI_DLC_FIXTURE_JOBS=12`; the `readset-skip` rows are re-counted on the combined B12 tree):

## BL-584 — two fixtures cannot be traced and have no `inputs.decl`, so they run on every push

**PARTIAL (v0.1146.0) — the map-write guard (option 2 of "Refuted premise" below) ships; owed (3), the sender behind a high-load drop, stays open, so this entry carries no `**LANDED` line.** `readset_write_map` in `core/scripts/derive-fixture-readsets.sh` no longer writes the `# OMITTED by the last run (always run):` line, so a run whose omitted set differs from the last one, a clean run included, no longer changes the tracked map on that line. The omission is still reported on the run's stdout (`OMITTED and therefore always run:`), and an omitted fixture still keeps no rows, which is what makes the hook run it. The committed map's own line (`fixture-git-env-seam`) is deleted in the same change. Only fixture code read the line: `git grep -F "OMITTED by the last run"` over `.githooks`, `scripts` and `core` hit the emitter and three `core/fixtures/readset-skip/run.sh` lines, all in the `tracer` unit (shard c), against 3 files for the map's `# GENERATED` header line in the same tree. Those arms (OMITTED --list, the two `--all` worlds, IDENTITY) now require 0 `# OMITTED` lines in the map and 1 stdout report naming the omitted fixture.

**PARTIAL (batch 257, wave 5) — in one window at push load, no outside Sandbox report fell within ±100 ms of any drop. The window's own volume is the leading cause, but it is unproven. (3) stays open, and this entry carries no `**LANDED` line.** One box-wide `log stream --style compact --predicate 'sender == "Sandbox"'` ran across B11's push (main checkout at d594c01f2), from 15:46:05Z to 15:46:35Z, at 1-minute load 25.6-28.2. It was started with no sudo and stopped by its recorded pid. Beside it, `--list "layer-contract-conformance-b" --tracer sandbox` ran clamped for 24 s, with trace rc 0. The liveness control passed: the seeded deny and the seeded allow-with-report each appear twice, once at start and once at stop. The stream held 76,168 report lines, 75,851 of them under the trace root. lcc-b's window logged 52 `dropped during` notices, in the 100 ms buckets 10:46:26.5 and 10:46:27.0-27.3 local. The box stream logged 46 in the same buckets except 27.0. In each drop bucket lcc-b's own reports ran 2,727-3,825 per 100 ms, against 0 from outside senders; the neighbouring buckets held at most 3 outside reports. Over the window, outside senders made 317 lines in total, and the largest outside burst, `spotlightknowledged.updater`'s 220 lines at 10:46:32.0, came after the trace and logged no drop. A Sandbox report is never persisted: `log show` over the same minutes returns none, not even the two seeded reports a live stream did capture, while the unfiltered `log show` window returns 20,087 lines. So no window can be attributed after the fact; only a live box-wide stream can name a sender. The method has a gap: the stream's predicate sees only Sandbox-sender messages, and the loss is upstream of the stream client (BL-1068), on a path that other log traffic shares. So a non-Sandbox burst at a drop is invisible to this capture. Owed for (3): a live capture across a push at load 27-135 that counts non-Sandbox log lines per 100 ms beside the Sandbox stream, from an unfiltered stream.

**Refuted premise, with the guard it needs.** `--list` WRITES the tracked `.ai-dlc-fixture-readsets.tsv` even when it traced nothing. `readset_write_map` runs unconditionally at the end of the run (`core/scripts/derive-fixture-readsets.sh:2259`), and its header line `# OMITTED by the last run (always run): <fx>` (`:1867`) changes whenever the omitted set changes. So a run that maps 0 fixtures still dirtied the main checkout, by that one comment line, for 21 s during B11's gate; the HEAD blob was restored with `git show HEAD:`. The write has a second shape, which 95's C13 trace at d619fe62a found: a one-fixture `--list` run that omits nothing DELETES the line. That drops every fixture the line named, not only the one traced, because the line is printed only when this run's `$OMITTED` is non-empty (`:1867`). So the line records the last run's omissions and never the merged map's. Any run whose omitted set differs from the one the line already names changes the file, whether it traced its fixtures cleanly or omitted them. Proposed guard, two options. Option 1 makes a run that maps no fixture (`MAPPED` is 0) leave the map byte-identical. It closes shape 1 only: 95's clean one-fixture run had `MAPPED` 1, and `readset_write_map` runs regardless, so shape 2 still deletes the line. Option 2 covers both shapes: take the OMITTED line out of the committed file entirely, or derive it from the merged map rather than from this run's `$OMITTED`. The guard to build is option 2.

**PARTIAL (batch 257, wave 3) — owed (3) stays open: the sender behind a high-load burst is still unnamed, so this entry carries no `**LANDED` line.** One more loss was located, and it is the window's own. In a `--list --tracer sandbox` run at 30a78d884 (load 19-28; BL-1068 has the figures), fixture-git-env-seam's 61 drop notices all fall inside its own two sequential bsdtar bursts (filed as BL-1117). The same run traced layer-contract-conformance-b and ledger-status-vocabulary with 0 notices. So a window can lose reports to its own volume, but the hermetic-runner drops recorded above, at about 100 own reports a second, remain unexplained. The box-wide `sender == "Sandbox"` capture then ran across B9's push (0.1133.0) and caught no loss to attribute. It ran from 2026-11-15T12:47:50Z to 13:07:17Z, at load 5 at the start and 10-14 at the stop: one `log stream` with no sudo, stopped by its recorded pid. A seeded deny and a seeded allow-with-report appear in the stream at both start and stop, so the predicate was live for the whole window. It read 6,480 lines with 0 "dropped" notices. Its largest burst was about 2,875 lines in the 13:05Z minute; over the whole window, Python accounts for 2,084 lines and cp for 590, and system daemons account for the rest. The `#discards` rows in `.git/ai-dlc-fixture-readsets.local` are byte-identical at start and stop (66 rows), although the file was rewritten at 13:06Z. So no traced window lost a report during this push. A push at load 10-14 does not reproduce the loss, which was recorded at load 27-135. Owed (3) still needs a capture across a push at that load.

**PARTIAL (v0.1132.0) — owed (3) is diagnosed for hermetic-runner. Its drops are not its own volume. The burst source behind them is unidentified, so (3) stays open and this entry carries no `**LANDED` line.** hermetic-runner traced with `--list --tracer sandbox` (BL-1068's measurement) gave 0 drop notices, 0 canary paths and 30 paths unclamped in an 84 s window at load 50, peaking at 1075 of its own reports a second. Under the background clamp at load 100-135 it gave 67 notices and a 12-path canary over a 41-minute window, with its own reports peaking at 116 a second. A window carrying about a hundred reports a second does not overflow on its own volume. The loss in it is other senders' reports, which every Sandbox report shares the path with: `hermetic-run.sh` profiles carry no `(with report)`, but the kernel logs sandbox denials on the same channel. A 10 s box-wide `sender == "Sandbox"` sample at load 61 read 4 messages, so the burst was not caught live. The BL-1068 clamp applies to the plain emitter only; `--local-map` and `--in-pool`, where these drops were recorded, stay unclamped. A longer window there would hold the per-uid slot longer and widen the exposure. Owed: name the sender behind a high-load burst. A `sender == "Sandbox"` stream left running across a loaded push, sampled when a traced window logs a drop, catches it.

**PARTIAL (v0.806.0, verified 581ca289) — owed (1) shipped for `fold-architect-ledger-join` only; owed (1) for `hermetic-runner` shipped in v0.832.0 (verified 67bdbb7a), and owed (3) is OPEN.** `fold-architect-ledger-join` carries `inputs.decl` and `tools.decl` and runs under `hermetic-run.sh` (72 ok, `required_missing=0`); its 46 traced rows are removed. `hermetic-runner` reads its subject files from copies made once at startup. That cut its trace window about 6x (17691 lines to 2902, loaded) but did NOT stop the drops: one 6-fixture `--tracer sandbox` call dropped 3 reports in its window, and three `--local-map` reps dropped 1, 2 and 7, at load 27-43 (0 of 3 clean). The premise that the drops come from re-opening four tree files hundreds of times is REFUTED; the drop cause is undiagnosed. `hermetic-runner`'s 17 rows and digest are removed, so it runs on every push (fail-closed) until the drops are fixed. The earlier count of 27 held fixtures is 5: 27 have `#discards` at n≥3 in `.git/ai-dlc-fixture-readsets.local`, and only 5 are keyed on the current runner and deriver.

**PARTIAL (batch 240) — owed (2) is met; owed (3) stays OPEN, so this entry carries no `**LANDED` line.** Of the 37 `core/fixtures/*/run.sh` directories that had no `inputs.decl`, 34 now carry `inputs.decl` and `tools.decl`, each proven by `core/scripts/hermetic-run.sh`. The other 2 carry a dedicated `.undeclarable` marker, whose first line states why no one declaration holds in both layouts: `hook-registration-join` and `retro-compliance-workflow`. (This sentence first named a third, `self-update-join-gate`; it now carries `inputs.decl` and `.dist-only`, and its `.undeclarable` was deleted in 233794d68.) The marker replaces the README sentence that (2) named, because 10 of the 37 already had a README for other purposes. Invariant I74(e) in `scripts/validate-enforcement-map.sh` binds it: every `run.sh` directory carries exactly one of `inputs.decl` or a `.undeclarable` with a non-empty first line. The arm's self-probe runs under mktemp before the corpus and fires in both directions.

Census, scored under `bash -c 'set -uo pipefail; …'`: 261 `run.sh` directories, 259 declared, 2 undeclarable, 0 offenders, exit 0. A second spelling (a `find -name run.sh` walk with `head -1`) also exits 0. Three seeded regressions each exit 1: a directory with neither file, an empty `.undeclarable`, and a directory with both. The receipt is `verify: manual`, because a census over the marker would read CLOSE-CANDIDATE while (3), the trace-stream drop cause, is still owed.

**PARTIAL (batch 241) — owed (3) is diagnosed and its cross-deriver cause is fixed; a solo drop at high load is unexplained, so (3) stays open and this entry carries no `**LANDED` line.** The cause is box-wide Sandbox report volume. Every `--local-map` and `--in-pool` deriver (`core/scripts/derive-fixture-readsets.sh`) adds to one shared pool of kernel Sandbox reports. A heavy fixture's window therefore drives "dropped during" notices into every window that overlaps it, and each of those windows discards its fixture on the drop-notice guard or the loss canary. The forced reproduction traced subject `small`, which issues exactly 40 tagged reads, beside a neighbour deriver tracing `big`, which runs 25 passes of `xargs -P 8 cat` over 3000 files. Without a slot, the subject's windows read notices/delivered/canary of 56/37/3, 62/35/5 and 51/33/7. With a per-uid window slot in both derivers they read 0/40/0 three times, while `big` still issued 59-61k reports per window. The fix is that slot. It is a per-uid mkdir lock at `/private/tmp/ai-dlc-readset-window-<uid>`, taken just before a window's sentinel writes and released just after its stream is stopped, under `--local-map` and `--in-pool` only. It is the BL-375 shape: it changes the schedule and not the work. Its pid file is written by the hook's own `readset_lock_write` and judged by its `readset_lock_stale`. The deriver sources both from a new `READSET_LOCK` span in both pre-push hooks. A waiter breaks a stale slot, writes a heartbeat line to its pool log every 100 polls (about 20s) so `POOL_STALL` does not refuse it, and runs the stale test inside its wait loop. The slot is re-entrant by holder pid. A nested deriver skips the wait only when the `READSET_SLOT_HOLDER` marker it inherited names this slot and the pid that the slot's pid file records as live. A stale marker or a foreign one waits. `core/fixtures/readset-skip`'s `slot` unit drives all of this through `core/fixtures/readset-skip/slot-world.sh` in a stub-stream world, and six mutants each fail exactly their own scenarios.

What (3) still owes is one observation: `small` traced alone at load 88, with no other `log stream` attached, gave 4 notices and lost 1 path of 40, and the slot does not explain that.

The cost of serialising windows is UNMEASURED. The bound below is derived from the code and was not timed. On one push, the windows that the pool and the detached trace open together number at most the `.trace` list (a fixture the pool traced leaves that list at `.githooks/pre-push:1251-1252`). That list holds only fixtures with no `inputs.decl` (`:1224`, and the `.mapped.all` join at `:960`), so the window count is derived by `for d in core/fixtures/*/; do [ -f "$d/run.sh" ] && [ ! -f "$d/inputs.decl" ] && echo "$d"; done | wc -l`. Each window holds the slot for, at most, the whole-tree `reset_atimes` (one `find | xargs touch` over the trace tree, which runs after the acquire), plus the settle loop (100 x 0.2s), plus the fixture's own run, plus the flush loop (100 x 0.2s). So a deriver waits at most the sum, over the windows queued ahead of it, of (40s plus that window's `reset_atimes` plus its fixture run). Windows from other sessions on the same uid add to the queue, because the slot is box-wide by design. In the detached trace this waiting is absorbed by `readset_trace_ceiling`: a wait long enough to reach the ceiling is killed, and the fixture stays unmapped, which is fail-closed. Under `--in-pool` the waiter heartbeats into its pool log, so `POOL_STALL` (`.githooks/pre-push:498`, 600 still polls) does not refuse the waiter. A HUNG `--in-pool` holder is itself refused by `POOL_STALL`: its group is killed, so its EXIT trap does not run and its slot is left behind, and the waiter then breaks that stale slot and takes it. Only when the holder is not under the pool (a detached `--local-map` trace) does the waiter wait until that holder's `readset_trace_ceiling`.

A slot that cannot be taken no longer wedges the wait loop. A path that is not a directory, a missing or unwritable parent, a stale directory whose `rmdir` fails, and a fifth stale break in one acquire each print one `slot: UNSLOTTED` line naming the slot and the reason, and that window runs unslotted. This fails open on overlap only: the drop-notice guard and the loss canary still discard an overlapped window.

The evidence that drives the slot is `bash core/fixtures/readset-skip/slot-world.sh core/scripts/derive-fixture-readsets.sh .githooks/pre-push`. It exits 0 when every scenario passes, 1 when one fails, and 9 when the world cannot be built. Its scenarios cover the window order (each window's stream stops before the next holder's starts), nesting, stale and foreign markers, the stale break, the heartbeat, the unslotted plain mode, the four fail-open cases and an old-empty-slot control. `core/fixtures/readset-skip`'s `slot` unit runs it against the shipped deriver and against one mutant per property. It is not this entry's receipt: the receipt is `verify: manual`, because a live receipt would read CLOSE-CANDIDATE while (3)'s solo high-load drop remainder is open.

**Absence-tolerant `!` lines, held by sentinel.** For each of the following, dropping the line from the declaration leaves `hermetic-run.sh` at rc 0, because the fixture tolerates the file's absence once it leaves the sandbox. The `!` sentinel is what binds each one while it is declared, and that is the shape the `!` grammar exists for, so none of them is a dead line: `context-mode-protect` `core/hooks/ai-dlc-context-provenance.sh`; `context-provenance` `core/hooks/ai-dlc-window.sh`; `context-sensor` `core/hooks/ai-dlc-context-provenance.sh`; `core-script-boundary` `core/hooks/ai-dlc-context-provenance.sh`; `cycle-commits-enforce` `core/fixtures/lib/preamble.sh` and `core/scripts/validate-mandatory-rules.sh`; and `derivation-capture` `core/scripts/partition-document.sh`, `core/scripts/validate-adversarial-convergence.sh`, `core/scripts/validate-artifact-budget.sh`, `core/scripts/wait-for-deliverable.sh` and `core/scripts/wfd-start-of.sh`.

**DEFECT (efficiency).** Operator-raised, 2026-10-16 ~04:40 EDT: "what about the fixtures whose readsets cant be mapped? i do not believe you cant figure out a way to refactor those so they dont need to be run every time." Filed by peer session ai-dlc-68 (listed as ai-dlc-4e [e15800]).

Intake re-derived: 86 `#discards` rows, 27 of them at 3 or more; `fold-architect-ledger-join` and `hermetic-runner` both have a `run.sh` and no `inputs.decl`. Per the filer those two are the only units that truly run on every push (173 s and 53 s recorded). Discard reasons across all rows, per the filer: 72 "the stream dropped reports", 9 "VERDICT: the sandboxed run's verdict lines differ", 2 "LOSS CANARY", 2 "TRIP".

Owed:
1. `fold-architect-ledger-join` and `hermetic-runner` get an `inputs.decl` and `tools.decl`, so `hermetic-run.sh` runs them hermetically and the hook keys them like the other 62; the sandbox proves the declaration complete.
2. A fixture that cannot be declared because it reads the whole tree by design says so in one README sentence, and is scored under BL-583's value test.
3. Find and fix why the trace stream drops reports (72 rows); while it does, every new undeclared fixture lands in the run-every-push set.

Caveat: in a linked worktree the local map is a different file (the BL-558 symptom); the fixtures unmapped there are re-measured once (1) lands. Related: BL-558, BL-583.

Scheduled in batch 235 for a later release (scope ruling: the boundary is the release).

The sandbox runs `env -i PATH="$HR_BIN"` (`core/scripts/hermetic-run.sh:608`) and the header at `:569` states "THE FIXTURE'S PATH IS THE FARM ALONE, so an undeclared tool is `command not found` rather than silently run", so removing a tool line makes a fixture that uses that tool fail. Of the 5 `inputs.decl` lines, 4 are mutated by `core/fixtures/hermetic-runner/run.sh` (DC1 `core/scripts/hermetic-run.sh`, DC2 `core/scripts/core-paths.sh`, DC3 `core/fixtures/lib/`, DC4 `.githooks/pre-push`), and one cannot be caught. ## BL-806 — a lead can write an estimated, future-dated time into `pipeline-snapshot.md`, and a resuming session reads it as fact

**DEFECT.** Filed at the batch-242 close from the graph watch (ai-dlc-fa, poll 30, 03:42 EDT 2026-10-26). At 07:39:12Z graph's s320 lead ran a `sed` that wrote `| 18/19 delivered at 07:50Z (still out: cross-1 only …` into `_bmad-output/pipeline-snapshot.md`. That stamp is 11 minutes after the moment it was written. The row's previous value read `16/19 delivered at 07:35Z`, so the time was estimated, not taken from `date`. `core/skills/ai-dlc/SKILL.md:52` makes a resuming session read that file in full before any pipeline action, so a wrong time there becomes a resuming lead's premise. No outcome was affected this time.

Owed: a check, not an instruction, because the instruction to take times from `date` exists in this repo's own rules and was not followed. Either the snapshot writer stamps times itself, or a hook or validator refuses a snapshot time later than the write's own clock. Measure the false-positive set first: a deliberately future time (a stated deadline) must be spelled differently from a delivery stamp.

**Progress (batch 243; the entry stays PARTIAL, `verify: manual` unchanged, no LANDED line).** Part (a) shipped; part (b) is owed.

**(a) shipped.** Deny B in `core/hooks/ai-dlc-foreground-budget.sh` (`elif ... then "DENY B"` at `:248`, header `# DENY B, A SHELL WRITE OF THE SNAPSHOT` at `:81-122`) refuses a Bash call that names `pipeline-snapshot.md` (as a path ending there; `pipeline-snapshot-history.md` appends stay allowed) and carries a write shape: `sed`/`perl` with `-i`/`--in-place`, a `>`/`>>` redirect onto it, `tee [-a]`, `cp|mv|install|ln` with it as the last operand, `git checkout|restore` of it, or an interpreter write (`open(…,'w'|'a'|'x'|'+')`, `.write_text(`, `.write_bytes(`, `writeFileSync`) on the literal path or on a variable bound to it earlier in the command. Reads stay allowed (`cat`, `grep`, `sed -n`, `<`, `python3 open(p)`, `cp` from it, `git add/commit/show/diff`). It is an early branch decided before every scope gate and fail-open, so no timeout, `agent_id` or missing detector exempts it. The remedy is the Edit or Write tool, so `ai-dlc-derivation-capture.sh` judges the written stamps; that hook's own header (`core/hooks/ai-dlc-derivation-capture.sh:134`, "reaches no PostToolUse hook") says a Bash `sed -i` on the snapshot reaches no PostToolUse hook, which is how the motivating `07:50Z` row went unjudged.

Measurement 0a, driving the shipped hook over graph's transcripts: 4,617 Bash calls name the snapshot and 1,912 are denied (1,905 by Deny B and 7 by Deny A, which fires on the waiter shape in the same corpus; 1,873 distinct). Of the 1,905 Deny B records (1,866 distinct), an independent second reading tags 1,740 by write shape: interpreter open-w 1,630, `sed -i` 121, cp/mv 10, tee 7, `git restore` 3 and redirect 2. These are tag counts, and a record can carry more than one, so they sum to 1,773. The other 165 records (164 distinct) carry no tag from that reading. A quote-aware scan of each `sed -i` invocation's file operand resolves all 164 distinct records to the snapshot, so they are `sed -i` writes that the reading's regex did not tokenise. The false-positive set is empty by an independent second reading. The fail-closed alternative (deny any command naming the snapshot unless it starts with a whitelisted read verb) was measured on the same corpus and rejected: it denied 1,465 commands that are not writes. Over `core/` and `templates/` (`core/fixtures` excluded) there are 517 payloads and exactly 1 deny, the validator's own self-probe body at `core/scripts/validate-artifact-budget.sh:1951` (`} > "$LPROBE_D/state/pipeline-snapshot.md"`), which is script body and not a command an agent issues. No core script takes the snapshot path as an argument and writes it.

(a)'s receipt, QUOTED and not installed (the entry's receipt line stays manual): drive the shipped hook with `{"tool_name":"Bash","tool_input":{"command":"sed -i '' 's/a/b/' _bmad-output/pipeline-snapshot.md"}}` and require a deny naming Edit; require `sed -n 1p _bmad-output/pipeline-snapshot.md` and a `>>` onto `pipeline-snapshot-history.md` to be allowed; the `sed -i.bak` spelling must also deny. It has not been scored here; the fixture arms of the hook change carry the scored form.

**(b) owed, and blocked on the CLOCK and two GRAMMAR gaps.** The clock: history carries only commit time (below). The grammar: a range end whose start carries no `Z` is missed, and a date-only stamp is skipped by the date rule, so its bare time is dated a day late (both in the census lower bound below). No arm ships until the re-filed evidence below exists. **Ship criterion: an arm ships only with a derived false-positive set that is EMPTY or ENUMERATED.** The N ≥ 300 exit condition below is the condition for RE-MEASURING, not a ship standard; a re-measurement that leaves an unenumerated false positive ships nothing. BL-912 does not subsume (b): `snapshot-update.sh` calling `--stamps-only` carries the same bare `HH:MMZ` gap.
- Census (batch 253, graph's committed history, file:// clone, read-only). Population: `git log --all --no-merges -- _bmad-output/pipeline-snapshot.md`, 1,190 commits; the HEAD-simplified history holds only 565, which the first b253 measurement took and was wrong. 11,859 distinct added lines, each deduped to its introducing commit. Rule: a bare `HH:MM(:SS)?Z` is dated from the nearest preceding dated stamp in its line (else from now's date), with previous-day rollover only and the `CLOCK CORRECTION` `to date -u:` token list exempt; it fires when more than 600 s ahead of now. Stamp-led Recent Activity (2,499 lines): 113 fires on 75 distinct lines with now = the parent commit's time (upper bound). Every other line (9,360): 78 fires on 63 distinct lines (upper). Of the 191 upper-bound fires, 116 carry a token that resolves at or before the line's own leading stamp. Under the commit's own clock 182 of the 191 stop firing (191 − 9). Lower bound, with now = the commit's own time: 9 fires on 9 distinct lines (2 stamp-led Recent Activity, 7 other), in 4 categories. 3 are range ends the grammar misses when the start carries no `Z` (`15:40-22:30Z`, `13:30→14:00Z`, `14:40→16:10Z`). 3 are lines with no dated stamp, whose bare time is dated from the commit clock. 1 is a date-only stamp (`- 2026-10-12 …`) that the date rule skips, so its `08:40Z` is dated a day late. 2 lead with a stamp that is itself ahead of the commit, which the existing FUTURE arm already fires on. The motivating `| 18/19 delivered at 07:50Z` row was never committed (`git log --all -S'delivered at 07:35Z'` = 0 against a positive control). Its text is a TABLE CELL, and five `snapshot-update.sh` ops write such text with rc 0: `--row-set <a> deliverable`, `--line-replace 'In-Flight Teammates'`, `--set in_flight_sub_step`, `--activity` with no leading stamp, and `--open-item`. Any future arm's population includes all five.
- Measurement 1 (batch 243), superseded by the census above, because its population was the HEAD-simplified history: 568 revisions of `_bmad-output/pipeline-snapshot.md`, now = the commit's `%ct`. 999 bare tokens, 292 fire (more than 600 s ahead) in 73 revisions. A 12 h previous-day rule removes 137 of them. The remainder is 184 `~` approximations, 35 range ends, 1 stray `T` and 72 other in 22 distinct texts, all false positives (times on another date, quoted past times); 0 true positives. Controls: the HEAD path exists (`cat-file` 0, a missing path 128); `99:99Z` gets 0 hits; the shipping dated-stamp arm on the same population finds 71 revisions and 342 FUTURE findings.
- Measurement 2 (batch 243), hook input (graph transcripts; that corpus is gone, see below): 5,236 jsonl files, 351,365 tool_uses, 5,695 touching the snapshot (3,552 Edit, 9 Write, 2,134 Bash). The motivating write is a Bash `sed` at 2026-10-26T07:39:12Z in `d22c7889…jsonl`, with `07:50Z` 648 s ahead; it is invisible to the write-time hook, and Deny B now refuses it. Edit payloads: 163 token fires without a rollover rule, 133 with. After narrowing (`~`, range ends, CLOCK CORRECTION/NOTE lines, 12 h rollover, backticked) about 55 fire, about 51 true positives and about 4 false positives, BY HAND CLASSIFICATION, not a derived count. An FP class that cannot be enumerated remains: a quoted earlier time under 12 h old (`08:57:12Z` quoted at 00:38). Controls: `99:99Z` 0; the motivating row matches. Eight CLOCK CORRECTION rewrites by graph leads show the true-positive class is real.
- Why history cannot decide it: commit time is the only clock history carries. The parent's time over-counts by hours and the commit's own time under-counts. The true write clock was the transcripts. Of measurement 2's 5,236 jsonl, 356 are reachable now (`find /Users/n8/.claude/projects/-Users-n8-git-graph -name '*.jsonl' | wc -l`), and the oldest record is 2026-11-09T00:00Z. `d22c7889*` is absent under `$HOME` and `/private/tmp` (`/usr/bin/find`; the control, a present file's 8-character prefix under `$HOME`, returns 4). Measurement 2's text above is now the only instance of its evidence, and its by-hand counts cannot be re-derived.
- Re-filed evidence requirement. Since BL-912 (0.1012.0) every snapshot write is a `snapshot-update.sh` Bash tool_use, and that record's own `timestamp` is a true write clock. Measured in batch 253 over the 356 reachable files (records with `"type":"assistant"`, Bash `tool_use` blocks whose `input.command` invokes `snapshot-update.sh` with a write op, keyed by tool_use `id`, joined to their `tool_result` by `tool_use_id`). **Op-detection rule: an invocation is a write when any write-op flag appears anywhere in the invocation, not only the first flag after `snapshot-update.sh`** (a leading `--snapshot <path>` puts the op second). Cutoff: tool_use `timestamp` ≤ 2026-11-10T12:41:59.999Z. Result: 273 write invocations, 2026-11-09T00:21Z to 2026-11-10T12:41Z; 252 returned without `is_error`, and 39 of those carry at least one bare `HH:MM(:SS)?Z` in the command text, an upper bound on writes adding one, since old-text and key arguments count too. A first-flag-only reading of the same population found 262, 242 and 37; it missed 11 writes and found none the any-flag reading lacks. Control: `99:99Z` matches 0. Sufficiency, in writes: zero fires in N bare-bearing writes bounds the FP rate below about 3/N at 95%, so excluding a 1% FP rate needs N ≥ 300 (the point where a 1% rate predicts 3 fires). Today 39 × 1% predicts 0.39 fires, below one, so a zero now refutes nothing. At the measured 39/252, 300 bare-bearing writes means 300 × 252 / 39 = 1,938.5, so ≥ 1,939 rc-0 `snapshot-update.sh` writes. The reachable transcripts span about 37 hours and held 252 such writes. `cleanupPeriodDays` is 365 in `~/.claude/settings.json`, and whether the window is rolling was not established. **Exit condition: re-measure when the post-BL-912 rc-0 `snapshot-update.sh` write population, ACCUMULATED across successive captures of graph's transcripts and deduped by tool_use `id`, holds ≥ 300 writes whose command text carries a bare token, counted by the strict grammar stated in the batch-256 Progress paragraph below; until then the check waits.** Meeting it starts a re-measurement; it does not ship an arm, which still needs the ship criterion above. The measurement then runs on that population with now = the tool_use `timestamp` and the census rule above, and counts four arms separately: stamp-led Recent Activity; the five table and key ops; the range-end-without-`Z` miss; and the `CLOCK CORRECTION` exemption.
- Design: the earlier "warn-only" line was the batch-243 builder's plan, introduced in 1326b45f5, not an operator ruling. Its "like `check_malformed_stamps`" does not hold, because that arm refuses (exit 1) in the same `--stamps-only` block (`core/scripts/validate-artifact-budget.sh:2024`). Whether to warn or refuse is decided when an arm is built on the re-filed evidence, not before.

**Progress (batch 256, M6; the entry stays PARTIAL, `verify: manual` unchanged, no LANDED line).** The exit condition is counted and not met, so the wait is confirmed and no arm is built.

The grammar, restated; it replaces the counting rule of the re-filed evidence bullet above:
- Write op: a whole-token flag from the case arms of `core/scripts/snapshot-update.sh` (`--snapshot)` at `:258` through `--init)` at `:291`) other than `--snapshot`. That is 19 flags: `--activity`, `--open-item`, `--row-add`, `--close-item`, `--row-delete`, `--open-item-file`, `--row-status`, `--row-set`, `--set`, `--key-delete`, `--set-block`, `--section-add`, `--section-move-to-history`, `--row-replace`, `--line-add`, `--line-replace`, `--line-delete`, `--move-to-history`, `--init`. Whole-token matters because `--open-item` is a prefix of `--open-item-file`, `--set` of `--set-block`, and `--activity` of `--activity-add`.
- Invocation: from `snapshot-update.sh` to the end of its command segment.
- Bare token: `(?<![0-9T:])\d\d:\d\d(:\d\d)?Z`, so the time inside a full ISO stamp does not count.
- Lower bound: graph's install of 0.1012.0, 2026-11-05T23:58:28Z, from graph's history.
- The deciding quantity is rc-0 writes carrying a bare token, against 300.

Reading, from the bl806 hand's corpus taken 2026-11-12T18:12:16Z: 6315 jsonl files under `~/.claude/projects/-Users-n8-git-graph`, newest write 2026-11-12T12:48:49Z. After the install there are 1646 writes, 1560 of them rc-0, and **209** of those carry a strict bare token, below 300. Over 6.52 days that is 32.1 a day; the 91 remaining project to about 2026-11-15T08:54Z. Controls in the same run: `99:99Z` matches 0, and the output's control count of rc-0 writes, labelled `ctl snapshot-update.sh`, reads 1560, equal to the rc-0 figure. The loose regex `\d\d:\d\d(:\d\d)?Z`, which also counts the time inside an ISO stamp, reads 722 on the same writes. That is why the grammar is pinned before the count is read. Source: the hand's scratch output `bl806.Pyct/contract2.out` in session 66c124f2's scratchpad, a perishable scratch path.

The recorded 273 / 252 / 39 reproduces exactly. The batch-253 counter, `tipadv-fix.py` (recovered from its transcript, tool_use `toolu_01J7qwB7fFbGDNUWsveu5Rrm`, which printed "fixed writes 273 ok 252 ok_bare 39" at 2026-11-10T13:02:56Z), was re-run with its grammar verbatim on the 356 files the entry measured, at the entry's cutoff (tool_use `timestamp` ≤ 2026-11-10T12:41:59.999Z). It reads 273 writes, 252 rc-0, 39 bare-bearing, and its first-flag reading 262 / 242 / 37, as recorded. Controls in the same run: `99:99Z` 0, and 0 tool_use ids seen in more than one record. Source: that run's output, `hrec.u3j0/repro.out` in session 66c124f2's scratchpad, perishable. Its grammar has three flaws, each checked against `snapshot-update.sh` and against the counter's own gate and `WRITE_OPS`:
- Its invocation gate, `snapshot-update\.sh\s+--`, drops a backslash-continued call, because `\s+` cannot match the `\` before the newline. Removing only the gate, same run, reads 344 / 314 / 64: the gate dropped 71 writes, 25 of them bare-bearing.
- Its write-op list names `--activity-add`, `--section-add-line` and `--row-del`. None of the three is a case arm: `--activity-add` appears only in the comment at `:298`, and the other two appear nowhere in the file.
- The list omits three real arms, `--init` (`:291`), and `--section-add` and `--section-move-to-history` (`:283`). Swapping in the 19 real flags, same run, reads 272 / 252 / 39.

The strict grammar above reads 331 / 303 / 59 on the same 356 files at the same cutoff, and 318 / 291 / 57 when only the first flag is read (`bl806.Pyct/contract2.out`, perishable). The deciding number is 209.
