## BL-397 — a story reaches gate-1 without the evidence sections, and reaches QA without a full-collection run with counts

**DEFECT (efficiency).** Closed in 0.674.0 on a tree receipt, reopened by BL-817. Its decision rule is the step-value ruling, "A step with no measured value goes" (`.claude/rules/operator-rulings.md`).

**DEFECT.** Filed by the consumer as PC-S315-DEV-AND-QA-ROLE-CHECKLISTS-LET-A-STORY-REACH-GATE-1-WITHOUT-THE-EVIDENCE-SECTIONS-AND-THE-FULL-SUITE.

**Claims, enumerated.** (1) No story file carried `Production Integrity Tests`, `Smoke Test Updates`
and, for renames, `Rename Verification` at its first submission: the filing says 0 of 5. (2) Story
1.5 reached QA without the dev having run the full suite and failed gate-2 on a baseline-fixture
test. (3) Check 21 failed pass 1 with 0 of 31 strategy test ids cited in any Dev Agent Record.
(4) Code review has no baseline-fixture sweep when a diff changes a handler's result shape.

**Premise, re-derived at `origin/main` 144c41b8.** The story template is BMAD's, and no ai-dlc
step tells the lead to add the evidence headings: `Strategy Test IDs` occurs in 0 files under `core/`
(control: `Production Integrity Tests` occurs in 4). `dev.md` already requires a canonical
full-collection run, but only inside `## Workflow Per Task` item 15. The dev side requires no counts: `deselect`
occurs 0 times in `dev.md` outside that section and once inside it. The phrase is the `--deselect`
flag, not a count. `code-reviewer.md` contains `baseline-fixture` 0 times (control: `fixture` 9).
At the consumer's final S315 tip, 5 of 5 stories carry the Production Integrity Tests and Smoke
Test Updates headings and 2 of 5 carry Rename Verification, because rework added them. The
first-submission state the filing counts was not walked. That walk was available but not taken,
because this session's harness refuses git commands aimed at the consumer tree. Claim (1) is an
unmeasured claim, not an unmeasurable one. Claim (2) is the seats' reading and was not
re-derived.

**Consumer overrides, read-only, `graph/.claude/skills/ai-dlc/overrides/`.** 10 override files
(control: 10 of 10 carry `shadows:`). The consumer shadows `team-roles/dev.md#Workflow Per Task` and
`team-roles/qa.md#Validation Checklist` wholesale (`team-roles__dev__bug-class-sweep.md`,
`team-roles__qa__bug-class-sweep.md`). Nothing shadows `code-reviewer.md`, `stories-test-strategy.md`
or Check 21. Any upstream text inside those two sections never reaches this consumer's dev or QA
until the consumer re-adopts the overrides against the new base. That is why the new requirements
sit in sibling sections.

**The fix.** `stories-test-strategy.md` Pre-Flight Checklist item (c) requires four headings in
every story file. They are `## Production Integrity Tests`, `## Smoke Test Updates`,
`## Rename Verification` and `## Strategy Test IDs`, the last a table with a closed `Kind`
vocabulary. `dev.md` gains `## QA Handoff Evidence`: the full-collection invocation, its working
directory and collected/passed/failed/deselected/skipped/xfailed counts before the handoff
message, the four sections filled, and every table row resolved. `qa.md` gains
`## Handoff Evidence Precondition`, a REJECT before any checklist item when that evidence is
absent. `code-reviewer.md` Field Verification item 5 adds the baseline-fixture sweep, with a
control, as an Important finding. Check 21 names the table as its citation set (BL-398).

**Fixture.** `story-evidence-scaffold` (ships) has section-keyed arms. The dev and qa arms first
stub the two shadowed sections, as the loader does, so text moved into them is a finding. It has
twelve copy mutants, each killed by its own arm only, plus a control and a cwd probe. Bound to
Check 21 in `enforcement-map.yaml`. The fixture is keyed on its `inputs.decl`, so no read-set trace is owed
(`core/scripts/derive-fixture-readsets.sh:367-368`).

**Receipt, scored.** Tip 1 (no fixture). Fix 0. Wrong fix A exits 1: it folds the dev text into
the shadowed `## Workflow Per Task`, which a whole-file grep accepts. Wrong fix B exits 1: it puts
the qa precondition inside the shadowed `## Validation Checklist`. Exit 9 means a subject role
file is gone or the fixture reports FIXTURE BROKEN.

## BL-398 — a strategy case letter is not bound to a test until sprint-review, and a re-point story lists its consumers from memory

**DEFECT (efficiency).** Closed in 0.674.0 on a tree receipt, reopened by BL-817. Its decision rule is the step-value ruling, "A step with no measured value goes" (`.claude/rules/operator-rulings.md`).

**DEFECT.** Filed by the consumer as PC-S315-STORIES-TEST-STRATEGY-CASE-LETTERS-AND-RE-POINT-CONSUMERS-ARE-NOT-BOUND-TO-A-TEST-AT-AUTHORING.

**Claims, enumerated.** (1) Nothing at authoring or story close resolves a strategy case letter
to a test name: planned case `(g)` of `1.4-UNIT-002` had no test until sprint-review. (2) Nothing
requires a re-point story to enumerate every consumer of the constant by `git grep`, with a
control. The filing's instance is one constant left un-normalized for a sprint.

**Premise, re-derived at `origin/main` 144c41b8.** `stories-test-strategy.md` names no strategy case
letters. Its one `lowercase letter` hit, line 156, is the AC-ordinal grammar (control:
`Test Strategy` occurs 4 times). Check 21 resolved "every test the strategy names" against "a Dev Agent
Record" citation with no prescribed shape. A DAR prose mention therefore counted, and a lettered
case inside one strategy row was never its own unit. **Measured on the consumer, read-only:**
S315's strategy row for `1.4-UNIT-002` lists cases (a) to (g). The story's Check 21 repair section
maps 6 of the 7 to nodes in `server/tests/test_s315_4_base_binding.py` and states (g) has none. A
per-letter check over that population returns 6 resolved and 1 unresolved: the true (g), with 0
false positives. **N=1 strategy row**, so the zero is a floor, not a rate. That figure was taken
over the repair section's PROSE mapping, and it scores the per-letter resolution logic only. The
shipped Check 21 wording does not accept that prose as a citation. On S315 as it stands, with no
story carrying the table, the check FAILS on shape and never reaches the letters. The same story carries
rows no collectable node can satisfy: `1.4-OPS-001`/`-004` are deploy-time and `1.4-UNIT-005` is a
shell predicate. The table's `Kind` column exists so those rows do not fail a node check.

**Consumer overrides.** No override shadows `stories-test-strategy.md` or Check 21. The consumer
overrides `steps/gate-validation.md#Check 20` and `#Check 5` only, so this reaches the consumer on
its next pull with no re-adoption.

**The fix.** Pre-Flight item (c)'s `## Strategy Test IDs` table has one row per strategy id and
per lettered case, with `Kind` ∈ {`test`, `predicate`, `deploy-time`}. Step 5 item 3 writes the
rows and requires the strategy's letter count to equal the row count. Check 21 names that table
as its citation set and says a DAR prose mention is not a citation. It fails a letter with no row,
a `test` row whose node is not on disk, and an empty `Resolves to`, and reports a strategy with
no id table as a stated SKIP, never a PASS. **Re-point consumers:** `## Rename Verification` now
requires `git grep` of the old and new names, every hit dispositioned, with a control. This half
is prose plus fixture only, and there is no mechanical check. A re-point story's consumer set has
no machine-readable declaration to join against, so its false-positive set cannot be measured.

**Fixture.** `story-evidence-scaffold` (shared with BL-397): the per-letter binding, the closed
vocabulary, the citation set, the unrowed-letter arm, the SKIP-not-PASS arm and an unisolatable
Check 21. Each has a mutant killed by its own arm only.

**Receipt, scored.** Tip 1. Fix 0. Wrong fix A exits 1: Check 21 keeps accepting a DAR naming a
test as a citation. Wrong fix B exits 1: a table-less strategy reads PASS and Step 5 writes "rows
as needed".

Closed in 0.674.0 (verified f7eec6f5) on a tree receipt, reopened by BL-817.

Held note (batch 177): shipped the `## Strategy Test IDs` table with per-letter rows and a closed
Kind vocabulary, Step 5's binding, Check 21 reading that table with a stated SKIP, the
`## Rename Verification` git-grep enumeration, and fixture `story-evidence-scaffold`.

Former receipt (the mechanism, not the outcome): sh F=core/fixtures/story-evidence-scaffold/run.sh; [ -f core/skills/ai-dlc/steps/gate-validation.md ] && [ -f core/skills/ai-dlc/steps/stories-test-strategy.md ] || exit 9; [ -f "$F" ] || exit 1; o="$(bash "$F" 2>&1)"; [ $? -eq 2 ] && exit 9; for m in no-letter-binding open-kind dar-prose-citation letter-unchecked skip-as-pass no-check-21; do grep -qF "MUTATION '$m' killed by its own arm only" <<<"$o" || exit 1; done; grep -qx 'story-evidence-scaffold: PASS' <<<"$o"

A finding id is series, pass, shard and `F-<n>`, with a keyless heading taking its ordinal. A finding is counted iff it is MAJOR+, no finding of a later pass references its id, and a repaired record keyed to it has every remediator check it records, bound to the record's own `edit:` target, still holding on the shipped blob, with no such record failing. Per pass, MAJOR+, caught later, repair present, counted: stories p1 11, 3, 8, 6; stories p2 3, 0, 2, 2; test-strategy p1 18, 0, 3, 3; test-strategy p2 1, 0, 1, 1; test-strategy p3 0. **Value FOUND: 12 counted findings, at least 1 by the rule.** These are findings, not distinct defects (test-strategy p1 5#F-4 and cross#F-2 both name 1.5-AC8), and the count is a floor. 15 test-strategy p1 MAJORs read UNVERIFIABLE, not ABSENT. `test-strategy-repair-p1.md`'s 21 repaired records all name a section shard under `shards/test-strategy-repair-p1/sections/` as their edit target, and the 8 of them that carry a recognised check grep a story, so none of them checks the shipped strategy (value.txt prints this coverage per repair file). The record and finding severities agree on 122 of 134 joined records, and 13 of 13 on keyless findings; a probe that shifts every keyless finding one ordinal up mismatches 2, and a disagreeing record cannot supply a repair. Edit-target probe: test-strategy p2 3#F-1 reads PRESENT unbound and UNVERIFIABLE bound. Presence self-probe: an absent literal fails as a hit-check and holds as a miss-check. Five unresolved `F-<n>` mentions were settled by hand: three point at earlier elicitation records, and two are stories p2 1#F-1's own "not a re-litigation of the F-4 disposition". The population's limits: "later round" means the step's later adversarial passes only, so the implementation-phase reviews and gate checks are outside the join, and presence is a replay of the remediator's own literal checks. **s321's adversarial-pass value** is `docs/analysis/step-value-stories-adversarial-s321.md`, covering the stories passes p1 to p3 only, with figures quoted from that file. ## BL-508 — a repair pass writes the defects the next adversary pass finds

**DEFECT (efficiency).** Closed in 0.786.0 on a tree receipt, reopened by BL-817. Its decision rule is the step-value ruling, "A step with no measured value goes" (`.claude/rules/operator-rulings.md`).

**DEFECT. [efficiency rank 2] about $105 across both sprints.** In every planning series read
(carry-over, requirements, architecture) in both sprints, each MAJOR on pass 2 or 3 sat in text the
previous repair wrote or half-fixed: 19 traced, 16 read in full, one true re-find. In stories the
adversary labelled repair-introduced majors s317 p1 0/11, p2 2/9, p3 2/4 and s318 p1 16/30, p2 8/19,
p3 1/2. Typical forms: one copy fixed and its sibling left, line cites shifted, a false count written
by the repair. Costed as planning third passes that ran only because every pass-2 MAJOR was repair-made
($39 s318 + $2 s317), plus the stories repair-introduced share of later-pass adversary and
remediator spend (4/13 × $97 ≈ $30 s317, 9/21 × $80 ≈ $34 s318). The remediator's drop from
high to medium effort at the s317/s318 boundary coincides with the rise; that is confounded and is
graph's setting, not core's. Subsumes the earlier "adversary series length" finding.

Efficacy, re-read for the fix: about 10 of about 33 sampled repair-introduced MAJORs are a sibling
left unfixed or a moved cite; the rest are underived claims the existing evidence contract already
forbids. The fix reaches that third, so the $105 is not what it recovers. The fix: every `repaired`
entry with a real edit carries `- siblings:`. A files-mode shard writes a fence grepping the
replaced text over ONLY its own files (a concurrent shard fixing a copy elsewhere would turn a
wider fence stale) plus a control count of the new text, and names an out-of-shard sibling on an
unfenced `escalated` entry. A section or subject shard writes `n/a (section shard; re-checked by
the serial remediator after assembly)`, because assembly deletes the section file its control
names. An insertion-only edit writes `n/a (insertion)`, a deletion-only edit `n/a (deletion)`. The serial
remediator runs the cross-file grep over the assembled files (`s<N>/stories s<N>/epics` after a
files-mode join, never the slot, `shards/` or a record) and re-checks moved cites, after the join.
Every repaired edit owes that grep, so the join prints `SERIAL-REQUIRED: <n>` (repaired entries
with a real edit) beside `ESCALATED-COUNT`, and the procedure dispatches the serial remediator in
every mode when either is above 0. Before this it ran only for an escalation or a multi-file
finding, and in graph s318 `stories-repair-p3` and `stories-elicitation-repair-p1` no serial
remediator ran. `join-remediator-shards.sh`
refuses a missing field in every parts mode, total omission included: the era is the install,
since parts are written fresh by agents reading the installed role file. Only `--sources`, which
checks a record that may span a pull, keeps a PENDING line, binding from the record's first
carrier onward. 0 of graph's 202 s317/s318 shard parts carry the field. The arm's own awk, run
over scratch copies of them, flags 936 entries in 193 parts, with the same run's set-aside count of
1 as its control. Those parts are never re-joined, though: 20 of their 23 dirs already hold a joined record, which a join
refuses to overwrite; the other 3 belong to closed sprints; and no shipped program calls the join.
The field is read in its four forms and no other: a presence reader passed `- siblings: checked`,
and a reader keyed on the word passed any entry whose prose mentions a sibling. The fence is a
`text`-labelled one, a point-in-time record that `validate-artifact-derivations.sh` and the capture
hook never replay: as a `derived` fence its control went STALE the first time a later pass rewrote the
text it counts, and the slot-wide re-run exits 1, "send back to the remediator" (graph's s318 slot
already reads 3316 stale of 4475). The join counts the control once, in parts mode, and refuses a
fence whose text is not in the file it names.

Cost, measured by re-running the join over graph's eight files-mode s317/s318 stories repair
rounds, with a compliant `siblings:` line added to every non-escalated entry: SERIAL-REQUIRED reads
71, 185, 23, 11, 4, 31 and 24 on the seven that join (s317 elicitation, party, adversarial p1, p2;
s318 elicitation, stories p1, p2). That is 349 repaired edits whose cross-file sibling grep the
serial remediator now owes, against the earlier dispatch rule, under which s318 stories-repair-p3
and stories-elicitation-repair-p1 ran no serial remediator. The eighth round, s318 party, refuses on
the known sm-05 set-aside (BL-519), its one true positive; there are 0 false refusals.

Residue (operator ruling: BL-508 closes on the after-join cite re-check, with this recorded): the
`grep -rn -F` line's output is recorded at the edit, never checked at write time (the capture hook
replays only `derived` fences) and never re-run; whether the grepped text is
really the replaced text (the write ledger carries no old_string); a files-mode shard writing the
section-shard form skips the fence, because the four forms are not gated by mode; a `--sources`
record omitting the field everywhere reads PENDING; and a substantive mis-repair whose prose and
pin agree.

## BL-512 — gate passes are discarded for lead paperwork, most often a Check 22 waiver

**DEFECT (efficiency).** Closed in 0.784.0 on a tree receipt, reopened by BL-817. Its decision rule is the step-value ruling, "A step with no measured value goes" (`.claude/rules/operator-rulings.md`).

**DEFECT. [efficiency rank 6] $16, 132 adjudicator-minutes.** Seven gate nonces were discarded for
lead-caused reasons across both sprints, 3 of them the same Check 22 waiver with no Rule 28
exception, under both the Sonnet and the local qwen lead at about 0.08 per active hour. Check 22
fired correctly each time; the waste is the discarded pass, not an escape. Two further discards
(Check 1 verdict form, Check 5 drift) are inferred, not stated as lead error.

The count did not reproduce as stated. Re-derived from graph's lead transcripts, the measured Check 22
discards are three nonces: s317 `implementation-20261010T230257Z` (a tier-pin waiver naming "82 dev
and 42 code-reviewer rows" and no spawn), and s317 `124524Z` and s318 `011534Z` (waivers naming
every `invalid-exception` spawn and citing no Rule 28 exception, or only "the four"). All four lead
`OVERRIDDEN` writes in s317-s318 were Bash `cat >> … <<EOF` appends, so no Edit/Write matcher sees
them. The check runs at dispatch: `ai-dlc-dispatch-guard.sh` refuses an implementation-gate
`gate-adjudicator` dispatch (Check 22 is in that manifest row only) when a current-sprint `OVERRIDDEN`
entry that dispositions Check 22 names no spawn of the sprint (catches 230257Z), or when a shardable
`invalid-exception` ledger row is not named by an `OVERRIDDEN` entry that remediates it as Check 22
clearing arm 4 accepts, by a sharded re-dispatch or a cited exception (1)-(4) (catches 124524Z and
011534Z). The Remediation field is assembled across graph's wrapped continuation lines before it is
read, and a negation counts only in the clause before the `re-dispatch` claim (or before a cited
exception), so "re-dispatched sharded, not serially" covers and "exception (3) does not apply" does
not. A refused dispatch writes no spawn-ledger row. Over graph's pending-archive.md + pending.md,
S298-S318, clause 1 denies nothing, and clause 2 denies only S316/S317 rows that arm S3 fails under
the 0.783.0 validator, which counts party seats as shardable (`party_seats`). Graph ran S317 under a
validator with no `party_seats`, so a party-seat `invalid-exception` row passed Check 22 there and is
denied here (the tea row `toolu_012cdPrfgjVUGRG1X3sAcT1g`, measured). Corrected at the batch-230 close:
the guard judges only the snapshot's current `sprint_id`, so a prior sprint's rows never refuse a later
sprint's adjudicator. What refuses one after the pull is the current sprint's own shardable
`invalid-exception` rows with no remediation (graph: 3, 11 and 3 in S316, S317 and S318); the waiver
shape is action 7 of `docs/plans/graph-pull-0781-to-0787.md`. The tip
adversary's replay over graph's S317 nonces: 20 denied, 19 of them recorded as Check 22 PASSED
under graph's own validator.

A nonce counts as discarded when it did not pass and a later nonce replaced it. Not passing means no merged verdict, a FAIL row in its verdict or parts, or a FAILED row in its gate-log entry. ## BL-514 — subagents poll with bare `sleep`, and no guard sees a subagent's wait

**DEFECT (efficiency).** Closed in 0.782.0 on a tree receipt, reopened by BL-817. Its decision rule is the step-value ruling, "A step with no measured value goes" (`.claude/rules/operator-rulings.md`).

**DEFECT. [efficiency rank 8] 5.4-12.4 h of subagent wall clock.** 228 subagent Bash calls whose first
token is `sleep` ran 12.4 h, about 196 s each; the narrow count (commands beginning `sleep`, `until`
or `while`) is 125 calls, 5.4 h, mostly qa and code-reviewer. `ai-dlc-foreground-budget.sh` exempts
any call carrying an `agent_id` by design, and `validate-steering-budget.sh` drops `isSidechain`
records, so neither can see it. Measured: a lead call with `timeout: 900000` is denied, a subagent's
`sleep 200` with the same timeout is not.

Premise corrected on re-measurement: the 228 first-token `sleep` calls / 12.4 h do not reproduce.
There are 80 first-word subagent sleeps (57 refused by the harness, 23 ran, 0.03 h). The cost is
foreground sleeping LOOPS, usually behind a prefix such as `S=/x;` or `cd x &&`, so the arm matches
a sleeping loop or a `sleep` of 30 s or more at any command position. Replaying graph's subagent
Bash calls through the fixed hook (budget 120 s) denies 198: 152 loops and 46 long sleeps, 17.0 h of
tool_use-to-tool_result wall clock.

Closed in 0.782.0 (verified 6fe903b6) on a tree receipt, reopened by BL-817.

Former receipt (the mechanism, not the outcome): sh bash docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s318/probes/bl-514-subagent-poll.sh

Re-audit (BL-817): graph s317+s318 against s319, s320, `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s320/scripts/` (extract.py, roll.py as committed at fc71a7b10 on main; the step rows of steps.csv per story); per story, the whole pipeline bucket: before (s317+s318 pooled, 34 stories) 5.52 h gap-inclusive, token-equivalent $119.9 (API $114.5), 228.3M tokens; after s319 (20 stories) 5.50 h gap-inclusive (gap 2.76 h), token-equivalent $111.7 (API $111.7), 255.3M tokens; s320 (13 stories) 5.79 h gap-inclusive (gap 4.50 h), token-equivalent $80.6 (API $80.6), 142.6M tokens. Lead-seat local-model share of calls in this step group: s317 44%, s318 0%, s319 0%, s320 0%; subagent seats ran hosted models only (opus, sonnet, haiku). The step did not fall. The subject (bare `sleep` polls) has no step of its own; the whole-pipeline figure is stated as the population it would move.

**Progress (batch b251, s321 subagent polls).** The mechanism fires on the consumer. Its outcome is not derivable, so this entry stays PARTIAL. Measured read-only over graph's subagent transcripts `~/.claude/projects/-Users-n8-git-graph/*/subagents/*.jsonl`. The window runs from the s320/s321 edge, 2026-10-30T00:10:03Z, to the corpus end at 2026-11-06T09:18:20Z, so s321 was still open: graph HEAD is 1ff134602, at 2026-11-06T04:04:38-05:00. 813 of the 5181 subagent transcripts have records in that window.

Classification rule. A subagent Bash call is a POLL when the hook's own predicate accepts its command. That predicate is the four jq defs `cmdpos`, `loopend`, `loopsleep` and `longsleep`, lifted verbatim from graph's installed `.claude/hooks/ai-dlc-foreground-budget.sh`: a sleeping `until`/`while`/`for` loop, or a `sleep` of 30 s or more, at any command position. The installed hook is byte-equal to `core/hooks/ai-dlc-foreground-budget.sh` at base (`cmp` exit 0, md5 17e1b07a77ba9e08124a62e32ef4fea7 both). The four defs hash alike at graph 72e1cd749, which installed the hook before s321, and at 124cdd80c, the 0.1019.0 reconcile (md5 20f61d71a8a24eb210414596070424f9 both). A poll is DENIED when its tool_result carries the hook's subagent-poll reason, `this subagent's FOREGROUND Bash call is a poll`. A poll that was not denied RAN, and is bucketed as backgrounded, as declaring no timeout, as declaring a timeout of 120 s or less, or as foreground over the 120 s budget.

Counts in s321: 19 polls. 6 were DENIED, 11 RAN backgrounded and 2 RAN with no timeout. None RAN in the foreground over budget, and 0 hours of foreground poll wall clock were spent. 18 of the 19 polls come from subagents of one lead session, 0ec8b044, and 1 from 189712ad. Every non-poll subagent call whose command mentions `sleep` comes to 28 calls and 0.03 h. The deny reason occurs in 6 records in the window, and every one is a poll's result, so no non-poll call was denied. Controls in the same run: 30035 subagent Bash calls in the window (the known-present count), and 0 lines carrying the token `QZXJ-BL514-NEVER-7731`. That token was verified absent from the graph project directory beforehand (`grep -rl` over it lists 0 files). Discriminating control: the same classifier over the pre-mechanism window, 2026-09-26T00:35:00Z to 2026-10-15T03:10:01Z, finds 212 polls. 0 were denied, 140 RAN in the foreground over budget for 13.73 h, 42 RAN backgrounded, 23 declared no timeout and 7 declared 120 s or less. So the classifier can fire, and the foreground-over-budget class went from 140 calls to 0. That b251 run was taken by a scratch Python reader, `bl514.py`, which was never committed and exists nowhere now; the committed classifier is `docs/analysis/subagent-poll-bl514/classify.sh` (see the b253 paragraph below).

Why this does not close. The figures measure the mechanism, not the outcome. A denied call and a backgrounded call both return their tool_result at once, so 0 h of foreground wait is true whether or not any agent waited less. The record shows a wait moved rather than removed: the poll denied at 2026-11-06T06:01:11Z was re-issued backgrounded 3 s later as the same `until grep -q -E 'RECORDED-RESULT|REFUSED' ...` loop. BL-817's close needs per-story machine hours and tokens of this entry's own step, by a measuring script in the tree. BL-514 has no step of its own, because the cost is subagent wait inside every step. s321 is not closed, and no committed script separates a poll's wait from the agent wait it was moved into. The no-step branch would declare an exemption, not measure one. Still owed: the change in subagent wait per story on a closed sprint, by a committed script, or the step-value evidence for what that wait buys.

**Progress (batch b253, the committed classifier).** The classifier is committed at `docs/analysis/subagent-poll-bl514/classify.sh`. It lifts the four jq defs (`cmdpos`, `loopend`, `loopsleep`, `longsleep`) from the hook at run time, and the deny-reason substring too, so it cannot drift from the hook it classifies. Its self-probe pins both the classifier's bucketing and the lifted rule's shapes against synthetic records, so a hook whose rule changes makes the classifier refuse until the probe is re-examined; it runs before the corpus is read. Its buckets are DENIED, HARNESS-BLOCKED, RAN-bg, RAN-notimeout, RAN-le-budget and RAN-fg-over-budget, plus NONPOLL-SLEEP-BLOCKED and NONPOLL-SLEEP-RAN for `sleep` calls the hook does not call polls. HARNESS-BLOCKED exists because a poll the harness itself refuses (`<tool_use_error>Blocked:` with `is_error`, returned within milliseconds) is not a poll that ran: a live run at 2026-11-10T12:25:03Z, before the bucket existed, had put two such calls in the wrong buckets: a `sleep 240` with timeout 300000 and an `is_error` result 5 ms later in RAN-fg-over-budget, and a second blocked poll with timeout 110000 in RAN-le-budget. (b) Idling is derivable from the records; definitions are in `docs/analysis/subagent-poll-bl514/README.md`. Idle: no tool_use other than one naming the launch's own task id between the launch and its `queued_command` task-notification. Not-idle: any other tool_use in that interval. Chain: a launcher exit followed by a Monitor, the later completion ending it. Unjoined: no task id, or no completion recorded.

| Window / population | idle | not-idle | unjoined | chain |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| s321 RAN-bg polls (54) | 10 | 29 | 14 | 1, not-idle |
| s321 post-DENIED backgrounded (25) | 3 | 18 | 4 | 0 |
| s321 all backgrounded (63) | 12 | 32 | 18 | 1 |
| pre RAN-bg (42) | 2 | 32 | 8 | 0 |
| pre post-control (27) | 4 | 16 | 7 | 0 |
| pre all (64) | 5 | 46 | 13 | 0 |

"All backgrounded" (63 in s321, 64 pre) is the union by (file, id) of the RAN-bg polls and the post-DENIED (or post-control) launches (`launchset($RB + $D.launches + $C.launches)`, `unique_by([.file, .id])`). "After DENIED (25 launches: 23 Bash, 2 Monitor)" and the join's "16 of 23 DENIED" count different things off the same rule: `follow($row)` selects, per DENIED row, every backgrounded Bash or Monitor call in the same file with `.id != $row.id` and `result ts <= call ts <= result ts + $span` (join.sh:157-158); `any_bg` counts the DENIED ROWS with at least one such candidate (16), while the 25 is the candidate LAUNCHES, flattened by `$J | map(select(...)) ... .cand[]` (join.sh:166) and deduplicated by (file, id) in `launchset` (join.sh:202). One row can carry several candidates, so 16 rows yield 25 launches.

Limits: The idle rule does not count TaskOutput or BashOutput calls on the launch's own task as waiting: their `ref` is empty (join.sh:97-103), so they fall in `$other` and would read not-idle; the corpus files named in `join.detail` carry 0 of either.  the records hold no model-thinking spans, so deliberation between tool calls reads as idle. Why the unjoined launches lack completions is not recorded and was not tested.

**PARTIAL (v0.1146.0) — the subagent gap is classified; most of it is agents parked between rounds with none of their own background work outstanding.**

New script: `docs/analysis/subagent-poll-bl514/gapclass.py <EXTRACT_OUT>`. It sits beside `gap.py`, and `gap.py` is unchanged.
- It classifies every subagent gap of an extract, meaning every interval over 300 s between two records, by the state when the gap opened. Its docstring defines the eight classes: open-model-request, open-tool:poll, open-tool:other, own-background-wait, parked-bg:poll, parked-bg:other, parked-until-message and other.
- A parked gap (the agent ended its turn, and a message ended the gap) is parked-bg when one of the agent's own background launches was still outstanding. A launch is a Bash call with `run_in_background`, a Monitor call, or a foreground Bash call the harness moved to the background on timeout. It is outstanding from its result until a task-notification names its task id or tool_use id, the same join as `join.sh`. A launch whose result names no task id stays outstanding, keyed on its tool_use id alone.
- Both poll classes use the hook's own `loopsleep or longsleep`, lifted by `lift.sh`.
- Its self-probe runs before the corpus is read: one synthetic transcript holding one gap of each class, plus a 200 s interval that must stay active. Among its six parked gaps are an id-less launch outstanding and a harness-moved launch outstanding. The probe also pins the launch counts.
- `--detail FILE` writes one row per gap, with the agent's last text before the gap and the line that closed it.
- It refuses unless, for every sub transcript, its summed gap equals the summed `gap` of that transcript's `segs.jsonl` rows, to the millisecond.

## BL-541 — the serial remediator's join is a beat on a record that already exists and is appended to, so it returns on every append and never on completion

Closed in 0.828.0 on a tree receipt, reopened by BL-817. Its decision rule is the step-value ruling, "A step with no measured value goes" (`.claude/rules/operator-rulings.md`).

Closed in 0.828.0 (verified a777c5b4) on a tree receipt, reopened by BL-817. The lease-race half: the beat takes `.beat-inflight` and its EXIT trap directly after the state-dir mkdir, before `--dispatch` resolution, so a beat blocked on the spawn ledger is seen by the stop hook (section 20; receipt below).

**PARTIAL (v0.796.0, verified e3073787; v0.815.0, verified a88f88d4) — the completion signal and its join step shipped; the stop-hook lease-race trace was OPEN here and is superseded by the 0.828.0 close line above.** `_gate-procedures.md` joins the serial and unsharded remediator with `wait-for-deliverable.sh --complete-repair --dispatch <agentId>`, and the auto-handoff and `handoff.md` step 1 split a mixed in-flight set into plain, `--complete` and `--complete-repair` calls. The second subject, whether `ai-dlc-continue.sh`'s `beat_lease_live` misreads a lease a just-returned beat rewrote, was untraced at v0.815.0; the 0.828.0 close line above supersedes that, and the lease is written atomically (`core/scripts/wait-for-deliverable.sh:1202-1204`: a temp file inside `.wait-beats/` renamed over the marker), so the hook never reads a partial line.

**PARTIAL (v0.794.0, verified 8aab009d) — the prose half shipped.** remediator.md: the serial remediator, and the one unsharded remediator, end the record with `repair-complete: serial <pass>` at column 0; a part or shard remediator writes no such line.

**DEFECT (efficiency).** `_gate-procedures.md:975` joins a repair with `scripts/ai-dlc/wait-for-deliverable.sh <repair_record_path>`. For the serial cross-section or cross-part remediator, that path is the JOINED record, which exists before the dispatch, and the remediator APPENDS its entries to it (`:940-942`). The plain predicate, "non-empty and written since the join armed", is therefore met by the first append and by every one after it. `--complete` cannot help: it keys on a last line beginning `seat-complete:`, and a repair record carries none (`wait-for-deliverable.sh:254-312`). So nothing distinguishes a serial remediator that has finished from one that has written one entry.

Measured in graph s319 lead session `62eadbbe-59c3-436d-bf7c-931ebc09ecba`, 23:54Z to 00:04Z: 21 `wait-for-deliverable.sh` invocations on `s319/requirements-party-repair.md` and two lead-made sentinel files. 9 were plain `--since <ts>`, 6 used `--reset --since`, and two waited on sentinels the remediator would never write, so they could only time out. Each return woke a ~300K-token lead turn (BL-538). The lead diagnosed it itself ("each beat returns as soon as the remediator writes again") but had no correct form to arm.

The stop hook also fired "NO LIVE WAIT-BEAT IS ARMED" at 00:00:49Z, seconds after the lead armed beat `bcd634p7x`. That repeats 23:13:02Z, when the lead read `.beat-inflight` five seconds later as holding a future epoch. Both are consistent with `ai-dlc-continue.sh`'s lease test (`beat_lease_live`, `core/hooks/ai-dlc-continue.sh:169`) misreading a lease a just-returned beat rewrote. Neither is proven, because the marker may have cleared between the hook and the read.

Owed: a completion signal the serial remediator writes last (a terminal line in its appended block that the beat can key on, as `seat-complete:` does for seats), and a step that tells the lead to arm `--complete`-style on it; or a join on the agent's own completion notification instead of a file. Separately, trace whether the stop hook's lease read can race a beat's own marker rewrite.

Former receipt (the mechanism, not the outcome): sh S=core/scripts/wait-for-deliverable.sh; [ -f "$S" ] || S=scripts/ai-dlc/wait-for-deliverable.sh; [ -f "$S" ] || exit 9; S="$(pwd -P)/$S"; command -v mkfifo >/dev/null || exit 9; d="$(mktemp -d)" || exit 9; mkdir -p "$d/_bmad-output" || exit 9; mkfifo "$d/_bmad-output/spawn-ledger.jsonl" || exit 9; row="$(printf '{"v":1,"ts":"%s","name":"r","tool_use_id":"toolu_R541"}' "$(date -u -r $(( $(date +%s) - 30 )) +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)")"; ( exec 3> "$d/_bmad-output/spawn-ledger.jsonl"; : > "$d/reached"; while [ ! -e "$d/release" ]; do sleep 0.1; done; printf '%s\n' "$row" >&3 ) & h=$!; ( cd "$d" && exec env -u CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID AI_DLC_STATE_DIR= AI_DLC_TEAMMATE_DIR=/nonexistent AI_DLC_PROJECT_ROOT="$d" AI_DLC_WAIT_BEAT_SECS=2 AI_DLC_WAIT_POLL_SECS=1 AI_DLC_WAIT_MARGIN_SECS=1 bash "$S" --dispatch toolu_R541 x.md ) > "$d/o" 2>&1 & b=$!; n=0; while [ ! -e "$d/reached" ] && [ "$n" -lt 300 ]; do sleep 0.1; n=$((n+1)); done; [ -e "$d/reached" ] || { kill "$b" "$h" 2>/dev/null; exit 9; }; m="$(cat "$d/_bmad-output/.beat-inflight" 2>/dev/null)"; : > "$d/release"; wait "$h"; wait "$b"; grep -q '^WAITING   x.md' "$d/o" || exit 9; [ "${m#* }" = "$b" ]

Control, same counter, s319 lead `62eadbbe`, 2026-10-15T23:54Z to 2026-10-16T00:05Z: 20 executed calls, against this entry's recorded 21 (6 `--reset --since` as recorded; 10 plain `--since` against the recorded 9; the one-call gap is unexplained). This entry's mechanism cut the join's wakes from 20 in ten minutes to 2 cap returns in 22 minutes. The step-group verdict above is unchanged. No planning run started after s323's handoff: there are 0 references to an `s32[3-9]` planning artifact in graph assistant records after 2026-11-13T13:14Z, out of 313 such records up to 2026-11-14T16:51Z. The same pattern reads 324 between 09:00Z and 13:14Z, and the token `ZQV-5e0de3c8-nevertoken-b257w5`, absent from this tree before this paragraph, reads 0 in that corpus.

**Progress (batch 257 W9-11; the entry stays PARTIAL, `verify: manual` unchanged, no LANDED line).** The 2 cap returns that the wave-5 paragraph above measured in a 22-minute pass are removed by construction. Under `--complete-repair --dispatch`, `wait-for-deliverable.sh`'s new `live_extend` keeps a beat polling past its deadline when the dispatched agent's own transcript took a turn strictly after the current quantum started (`dispatch_live` LIVE, not stalled, not FAILED). It charges one beat per extension and never extends a counter at or past `max_wait_beats`, so the sequence bound and the cap/grant block on the next call are unchanged. A transcript that exists but stops advancing returns at the quantum as before. Nothing in the script refuses a second beat on a target that already has a live one: the `.beat-inflight` lease is project-wide and the sibling guard is per shell. So each sleeping beat in this mode stamps `<key>.arm` with its pid and start epoch, the newest arm overwriting it, and only the beat whose stamp still stands may extend. An overlap of two beats on one record therefore lasts at most one quantum, as before. `rule-29.md` states the exception, which applies only to the backgrounded lead join; its foreground half has no enforcer (BL-1142). Hand probe (quantum 4 s, poll 1 s, four probe runs in parallel): with a writer taking a turn every second for 9 s before `--mark-repair`, base returns WAITING at 4 s and tip returns DELIVERED at 10 s in one call. With a static transcript, tip returns WAITING at 3 s. With a writer that stops after 2 s, tip returns WAITING at 7 s, one quantum after the stop. With two beats on one record, armed 1 s apart from separate shells, tip's older beat returns WAITING at 5 s and the newer one DELIVERED at 9 s. The mutant that tests only that the transcript exists runs the static and stopped arms to 24 s and 23 s. The mutant without the newest-arm check keeps the older beat alive to DELIVERED at 10 s. Still owed: the step-group outcome above, and a re-taken wake count on the first consumer serial pass that runs this release.

## BL-543 — the serial remediator discovers its sibling and moved-cite worklist by hand, making it the longest single wait in the planning steps

Closed in 0.815.0 on a tree receipt, reopened by BL-817. Its decision rule is the step-value ruling, "A step with no measured value goes" (`.claude/rules/operator-rulings.md`).

Closed in 0.815.0 (verified a88f88d4) on a tree receipt, reopened by BL-817. `core/scripts/serial-remediation-worklist.sh` derives the serial remediator's replaced-text HITs and MOVED-CITEs from the pre-repair copies the splitters now keep (`sections/.before`, `.before/<stem>`), and removes each copy it resolved unless `--keep` is passed. A file with no copy is UNASSESSABLE, exit 3. The sections and subject repair dispatch runs it once after the join and before the serial dispatch, and the remediator adjudicates its hits instead of deriving them. Fixture `serial-remediation-worklist`.

**DEFECT (efficiency).** In s319 carry-over-evaluation (2.8 h), the two serial cross-section remediators took 42 min (party repair, 19:54–20:36Z) and 29 min (elicitation repair, 20:53–21:22Z), about 42% of the step. Every parallel fan-out in the same step finished in 5–15 min. In requirements, the serial cross-part remediator ran 52 min (23:11Z–00:04Z). Most of that work is mechanical. For every `repaired` entry it runs `grep -rn -F '<replaced text>'` over the assembled files, and it re-reads every cite into an edited file (`_gate-procedures.md:991-1002`). BL-508 (v0.786.0) made the serial remediator run whenever `SERIAL-REQUIRED` is above 0, so this cost now lands on every sharded repair. Owed: the join emits that worklist itself, meaning each repaired entry's replaced text with its grep hits over the assembled files, and each cite into an edited file whose line moved. The serial remediator is then briefed to adjudicate the hits rather than find them. Measure by re-timing the serial remediator on the next consumer sprint.

**Fourth timing (graph s319 requirements elicitation repair).** The serial cross-part remediator ran 31 min, 01:19:04Z to 01:50:12Z. ## BL-716 — after a parallel repair fan-out, every SERIAL-REQUIRED item goes to one serial remediator, so a 19-shard round that finishes in 8 minutes waits an hour on one agent

Closed in 0.913.0 on a tree receipt, reopened by BL-817. Its decision rule is the step-value ruling, "A step with no measured value goes" (`.claude/rules/operator-rulings.md`).

Closed in 0.913.0 (verified 5b5388924) on a tree receipt, reopened by BL-817. **DEFECT (efficiency).** Relayed by ai-dlc-5d from graph S319; dedupe re-derived on the distribution by lead ai-dlc-37; id allocated by ai-dlc-37; filed by peer session ai-dlc-c0 (batch 236), with BL-713's release.

`core/scripts/join-remediator-shards.sh:741` defines SERIAL-REQUIRED (parts mode only) as the count of `repaired` entries with a real edit, each owing the cross-file sibling grep and moved-cite check that no shard can run, and the procedure dispatches ONE serial remediator whenever that count or ESCALATED-COUNT is above 0. However many cross-part items there are, one agent works them all, in sequence.

Evidence, relayed by ai-dlc-5d and not re-measured by this filing (load 2–28, so the wall clock is an observation, not a timing): graph S319 stories, 2026-10-19. The 19 parallel shards finished in about 8 minutes (20:10–20:17 EDT); the join reported SERIAL-REQUIRED 148; the one serial agent ran 62 minutes (20:24:49–21:26:53, 172 tool calls, 77 Edit), while the lead re-armed its wait beat 7 times and ran nothing else. That is 148 items in 62 minutes, about 0.42 minutes per item, as an observation; no per-item baseline exists to compare it against.

Prior art, not a duplicate: archived **BL-543** (LANDED v0.815.0, verified a88f88d4) cut the serial remediator's DISCOVERY work — `core/scripts/serial-remediation-worklist.sh` now derives its replaced-text HITs and MOVED-CITEs from the splitters' pre-repair copies instead of the agent finding them by hand — on S319 rounds whose serial remediators took 42, 29 and 31 minutes. Those rounds recorded no SERIAL-REQUIRED counts (BL-543 records one round's 28 appends and 48 edits only), so they are not a baseline for tonight's figure and are not compared with it. BL-543 did not change the COUNT: every cross-part item still goes to the one agent, and that is what this filing owes.

Dedupe, re-derived with `grep -c` at origin/main: "SERIAL-REQUIRED" 3 in docs/backlog.archive.md and 0 in docs/backlog.md; "serial remediator" 35 in the archive, the relevant one BL-543; control BL-583 6.

Owed, the mechanism left to the builder: partition the SERIAL-REQUIRED items by the parts they touch, so disjoint sets run as parallel serial lanes and each lane keeps the serial guarantee within its own set. A fixture arm (or receipt): a seeded join whose two disjoint item sets dispatch to two lanes, while an overlapping pair stays in one lane.

Measured input for the partition, graph-watch, batch 238. These figures come from the graph watcher's scratch run of `core/scripts/serial-remediation-worklist.sh` over s319's stories party repair, relayed by the batch-238 lead; this filing did not re-measure them. The run's SUMMARY read `files 19 replaced 115 hits 135 moved-cites 1`, over 19 parts and 148 SERIAL-REQUIRED entries. Taking the parts each item touches as edges, the lane components are: edit only, 19; edit ∪ MOVED-CITE, 18; edit ∪ HIT, 1. One boilerplate line merges every lane: the sprint-title `architecture_refs` line that every story carries (`story-2-1:8-10`, hunk `6.md#F-9`), which HITs line 9 of 18 other stories. On this corpus, a partition on HIT edges therefore yields one lane, the status quo, unless a HIT on a line common to all parts is excluded from the edge set. The builder must decide whether such a common-line HIT can be excluded safely, or must be routed as a single fan-in item.

Built (batch 240, R2): lanes are keyed on FILES from each part's `edit:` set, joined only by MOVED-CITE edges from the worklist the join now runs after a sections or subject assembly (`--keep`); a HIT or escalation reaching two or more lanes is one FANIN item, so the common-line HIT is routed to the fan-in and never excluded. Lanes run as a second parts-mode round through the same join (`--artifact <name>-serial`), and `--escalations`/`--sources` read that round's record after round one's.

Former receipt (the mechanism, not the outcome): the receipt file was removed under BL-915 as unreferenced.

Re-audit (BL-817): graph s319 against s320, `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s320/scripts/` (extract.py, roll.py as committed at fc71a7b10 on main; the step rows of steps.csv per story); per story, stories-test-strategy: before (s319 pooled, 20 stories) 0.80 h gap-inclusive, token-equivalent $26.0 (API $26.0), 44.8M tokens [BL-927 extractor fix (b251): now 0.78 h, token-equivalent $25.0 (API $25.0), 42.7M tokens; conclusion unchanged]; after s320 (13 stories) 0.93 h gap-inclusive (gap 0.40 h), token-equivalent $39.1 (API $39.1), 65.0M tokens. Lead-seat local-model share of calls in this step group: s319 0%, s320 0%; subagent seats ran hosted models only (opus, sonnet, haiku). The step did not fall.

**Measured (batch 255 analysis, s321 complete, s322 partial).** `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s322.md`. Population for every figure: graph's Claude Code transcripts, 151 lead and 6,018 subagent, read only, window end 2026-11-11T10:30:00Z, extracted by `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s320/scripts/extract.py` with the s321/s322 edge at 2026-11-09T00:00:00Z; s322 is partial (planning complete, 3 of 8 stories done). In-session time only; handoff gaps are excluded by ruling. `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s322/scripts/poles.py` measures the wall clock during which every running subagent has one role, on busy intervals cut at resume messages. Solo-remediator hours: s318 7.8, s319 11.9, s320 9.4, s321 9.6, s322 9.1 (partial). The lanes shipped in 0.913.0 first ran in s320, and the figure did not move. s322 instances: a "Fan-in writer" of 73 minutes in stories-test-strategy and a "Serial lane 1 (prd)" of 57 minutes. Wall clock at stake: up to 9.6 h a sprint; dollars about $0, because the serial agent's tokens are spent either way. Filed here as evidence and not as a new entry (lead ai-dlc-7f offered BL-973; it is not used).

**PARTIAL (v0.1058.0).** Derived first, from committed data: `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s322/scripts/serial_tail.py` (output `out/serial_tail.txt`) reproduces `poles.py`'s remediator solo-role total per sprint and splits it. Of the 7.08/7.37/7.27 h of s320/s321/s322 with one remediator alive, 4.03/3.74/5.48 h sat in rounds whose join printed `LANE-COUNT: 1` (a document-mode round maps every section to one assembled document, so a file-keyed lane can never split it) and 2.19/2.46/1.51 h in the fan-in after a multi-lane round. Writer turns were 35/29/33% of those agents' time; the rest was read-only adjudication. Shipped: `core/scripts/join-remediator-shards.sh` deals a lane's or the fan-in's items, when there are 4 or more, to `ADJUDICATE:` batches (round-robin, k = min(4, floor(n/2)), then `ADJUDICATOR-COUNT:`). They go to parallel read-only adjudicators dispatched beside the lanes, each writing one batch file under `shards/<lane artifact>-adjudicate-p<M>/`, and the lane or fan-in remediator stays the one writer and applies their decisions. The lane round refuses an adjudicator that wrote anything but its batch file. In files mode an escalation naming only one lane's files, or files no part edited (on disk under the slot's `stories/` or `epics/`), is that lane's item or a lane of its own, not a fan-in item. `core/scripts/wait-for-deliverable.sh --mark` and `core/scripts/content-seal.sh` `_seal_remedy` give a Bash-changed adjudicator batch file its own remedy (delete it, voiding its decisions, and re-dispatch that batch's adjudicator), ordered before the generic adversary-shard arm. The carriers are `core/skills/ai-dlc/steps/_gate-procedures.md` and `core/team-roles/remediator.md`. The receipt is `core/fixtures/remediator-shard-join` LN10 to LN16, with mutants LN10m, LN13m and LN15m, each killed by its named arm, and NL2 re-expected for the new routing; and `core/fixtures/content-seal` CS-U-R2 and CS-K2a, with CS-K2 extended. Expected, as an UPPER BOUND (even item splits, and the time that decides an edit counted as writer time): a width-1 tail of 4.78/4.74/4.84 h at k=2 and 3.63/3.43/3.63 h at k=4, against the 7.08/7.37/7.27 h above. Consumer cost: graph moves nothing on this release until the rulebook reaches it. The engine half is live from step 3 of graph's delivering pull, and under the old rulebook its `ADJUDICATE:` lines are ignored. The rulebook half reached graph mid-s322 (0.1058.0 at 2026-11-11T22:16Z, after s322's planning steps), and graph's main c8ab65397 ("reconcile distribution 0.1096.0 → 0.1101.0") carries `.claude/.ai-dlc-version` 0.1101.0. Only a sprint run on the merged tree is outstanding. Exit condition: this entry closes on an `Outcome measured:` line from the first graph sprint whose planning ran on v0.1058.0 or later, naming that sprint, `poles.py`'s remediator solo-role hours and `serial_tail.py`'s width-1 split, before and after.

**Known gap (batch 254, unbuilt):** no mutant covers `lanes never merge` (the LN14 seed kills it), and no engine check covers an adjudicator that writes nothing before a mis-chosen window (the procedure's `--since` rule covers it).

**PARTIAL (v0.1133.0).** The first round on the adjudication engine dispatched no adjudicator, because the join's plan existed only in its own stdout. Evidence: graph s323 carry-over-evaluation party repair. Engine 0.1101.0 (graph main `c8ab65397`, merged 2026-11-13T06:57Z, before the round). Lead transcript `0ef7ba46`, read-only scratch counters over `~/.claude/projects/-Users-n8-git-graph`, not committed. At 09:17:11Z the lead ran `join-remediator-shards.sh --document … 2>&1 | tail -30`. The join wrote the record, and of its output the lead kept 29 `ADJUDICATE: fanin` lines and `ADJUDICATOR-COUNT: 8`; the LANE, LANE-ITEM and other batch lines were cut. The re-run at 09:17:25Z refused, rc 2, `already exists; a join never overwrites a repair record`. At 09:18:15Z the lead grepped `--help` for `plan|dry|adjudic` and found no way to re-emit the plan. Instead, at 09:18:46Z it hand-built a 44,439-byte serial brief of 28 multi-section findings and dispatched ONE remediator, `agent-a37305a67843ee413`: 09:18:54Z to 09:41:25Z (22.5 min), first write 09:23:56Z, 269 tool calls. ## BL-804 — a remediator writes `derivations: not replayed` although replay is its default, and the join's refusal costs a whole re-dispatch

Closed in 0.949.0 on a tree receipt, reopened by BL-817. Its decision rule is the step-value ruling, "A step with no measured value goes" (`.claude/rules/operator-rulings.md`).

Closed in 0.949.0 (verified 94c8dcdc7) on a tree receipt, reopened by BL-817.

**DEFECT (efficiency).** Filed at the batch-242 close from the graph watch (ai-dlc-fa, poll 16, 01:07 EDT 2026-10-26). In graph s320, the `stories-party --pass 1` repair join refused at 05:05:46Z with `REFUSED: 11.md: derivations: not replayed; …` (the checks-field refusal in `core/scripts/join-remediator-shards.sh`). Part 11 is story 3.5, in `s320/shards/stories-party-repair-p1/11.md`. `core/team-roles/remediator.md:134-139` makes replay the default ("Replay is the default: run `scripts/ai-dlc/validate-artifact-derivations.sh <your record>` after your last edit"), but the remediator wrote `not replayed` anyway. Graph's lead recovered by contract: a record-only re-run (agent a0481edc5f18691e2) replayed 5 derivations and re-marked, and the re-join returned rc=0. The cost was one dispatch and about 2 minutes. One instance, measured from the transcript.

Owed: a mechanism that makes the default the act, not the instruction. For example, the `--mark` path of `wait-for-deliverable.sh` could run the replay itself when a repair record says `not replayed` and its fences name no deferred document, then write the count. That moves a refusal the join already makes to the point where the remediator can still fix it. Measure the false-positive set first: a record whose fences are legitimately deferred must not be forced.

Former receipt (the mechanism, not the outcome): `core/fixtures/remediator-shard-join/receipt-bl804.sh`, removed under BL-915 as unreferenced, since no runner invoked it.

Re-audit (BL-817): graph s320, `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s320/scripts/` (extract.py, roll.py as committed at fc71a7b10 on main; the step rows of steps.csv per story); per story, stories-test-strategy: 0.93 h gap-inclusive, token-equivalent $39.1 (API $39.1), 65.0M tokens, the baseline. No graph sprint has run 0.949.0 yet. Owed: first graph sprint on >=0.949.0. The shipped mechanism (0.949.0, `wait-for-deliverable.sh --mark` replays the derivations) stands; the reopen owes the outcome, not a rebuild.

Owed (batch b244, c4), the premise of the mechanism above corrected. The ordering was not the defect: `core/team-roles/remediator.md` orders `join-remediator-shards.sh --escalations` before `wait-for-deliverable.sh --mark-repair` on purpose, `core/skills/ai-dlc/steps/_gate-procedures.md` agrees, and graph's repair subagent (agent-a2ba43cd07934d6e7, 2026-10-27T09:01Z) followed it. Its join refused `derivations: not replayed`; it replayed, edited the line to `replayed 1`, re-joined at rc 0, and then marked. That cost about 16s inside the dispatch and no re-dispatch, so the join before the mark is the designed loop. The real defect was one sentence in `remediator.md` that said `--mark` and `--mark-repair` replay the record themselves, which offered the serial or unsharded remediator `not replayed` as an option its own earlier join refuses. Resolved: the sentence now scopes the replay to `--mark` and says the serial remediator replays before its join and writes `replayed <n>` itself, pinned in `process-rule-pins` by the `rem-serial-replay` row, a named mutant that restores the old clause, and the `rem-serial-optout` arm. The entry stays OPEN: it is decided by its receipt plus a measured outcome under BL-817, the first graph sprint on a release carrying this sentence showing no `not replayed` refusal at the join.

**Progress (BL-804, batch b250, 2026-11-05: the close predicate is a DEAD-PREMISE).** The predicate in the paragraph above, "no `not replayed` refusal at the join", cannot be met by a correct engine. The refusal is correct whenever derivation fences go stale. Separately, `--mark-repair` already runs the replay (`core/scripts/wait-for-deliverable.sh:650-665`, term 6), and `core/fixtures/remediator-shard-join` arm EC41 asserts it (`run.sh:230-231`). On graph s321 (subagent agent-aa2b18d38429a92ba, 2026-11-04 11:13:37Z) the refusal came from 21 stale fences inside the sealed block. The agent fixed them in the same dispatch, the join returned rc=0 at 11:16:18Z, and there was no re-dispatch.

The question is re-keyed: does a `not replayed` refusal cost a re-dispatch. Measured over s321 (707 graph transcripts, from extract.py's s320/s321 edge 2026-10-30T00:10:03Z to graph e32f9ce2f), with patterns keyed on the emitters' text at `core/scripts/join-remediator-shards.sh:1684` and `core/scripts/wait-for-deliverable.sh:914`. After the fix reached graph (reconcile #1271, merged 2026-10-28T01:36Z, 0.958.0) there were 3 refusals (one in s321, two in s320 under 0.969.0), all fixed in-dispatch, and 0 re-dispatches. Before it, 1 of 5 refusals cost a re-dispatch (11.md, 2026-10-26, under 0.948.0).

The entry does not close on this. At the before-rate, 3 refusals predict 0.6 re-dispatches, which is below 1, so the zero cannot discriminate. The Baseline (stories-test-strategy per-story cost) is UNMEASURABLE on s321 because that step is incomplete. The close condition is now re-dispatches per refusal, measured once enough refusals have accrued that the before-rate predicts at least one. The entry stays LIVE and PARTIAL, with `verify: manual`.

NOTE: on s321 the join accepted an unearned `replayed 147` at 11:15:11Z while the validator still failed, and `--mark-repair`'s own replay refused it at 11:15:19Z. The cost was one extra in-dispatch refusal and no re-dispatch. The join's checks field trusts the stated count, and `--mark-repair` is the backstop.

## Entries filed after the re-audit

## BL-878 — every fixture is converted to in-process unit cells, and no session de-scopes a conversion

**DEFECT.** Operator ruling, 2026-10-30: "those entries would not allow for future sessions to descope the conversions. We would move forward on everything, full stop." Every fixture under `core/fixtures/*/run.sh` is converted. No fixture is dropped, deferred or judged not worth converting; the scope changes only by an operator ruling.

**Population, derived.** The fixtures still to convert are the rows of `scripts/fixture-inprocess-allowlist.txt`, which BL-879 seeds once from the tree and which only shrinks. Never hand-list them here; read the allowlist.

**Batches.** Group the allowlisted fixtures by shared subject script: build the graph whose edges join a fixture to every script it runs, taken from its `invocations.decl` subject rows and from its calls into `core/scripts/`, `core/hooks/`, `core/skills/` and `scripts/`. A batch is one connected component of that graph, so two sessions never change the same script in parallel. One session owns a component until every fixture in it is converted. Order the components by the summed loaded seconds of their fixtures in `$(git rev-parse --git-common-dir)/ai-dlc-fixture-durations`, costliest first. The durations decide ORDER only, never membership.

**Done, per fixture.**

1. Every cell runs in process. The only command-line runs left are one per distinct CLI outcome of the subject, each with an equivalence cell proving the in-process answer is byte-identical to the command-line answer (stdout, stderr and exit code) on a refusing input and on a passing input that differ.
2. The subject is made loadable by a PURE MOVE behind `[ "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}" = "$0" ] || return 0`, verified by a sorted line-multiset diff showing only the guard and its comment, and by `bash -n`. Where a pure move cannot reach a decision, the larger change is made: embedded code ported to a module, a new shipped file packaged (`install.sh`, both manifests, `uninstall.sh`, per `.claude/rules/consumer-boundary.md`), a self path read from `BASH_SOURCE`. Each of these carries the same equivalence proof as item 1.
3. Mutant cells and the `--matrix` scoring are converted. Each mutant's scratch copy is sourced once, and only its owning cell or cells run against it. Every mutant is killed by the cell that owns its property, never only collaterally. A fixture with no `MUTANT-MATRIX` block gets a kill harness and a matrix written from it, and leaves `scripts/fixture-matrix-allowlist.txt` in the same change.
4. Seeded worlds are built once and copied per cell (clause 4 of `.claude/rules/fixture-mutants.md`).
5. A fixture that sleeps or polls gets the injected clock as part of its conversion; the sites and the lever are BL-868 item 8, which stays the owning entry.
6. `invocations.decl` is re-measured by a counted hermetic run, and the read set is re-traced with the sandbox tracer.
7. A cell stays end to end ONLY where a mechanism the subject relies on cannot exist in a subshell (example: `reconcile/lib.sh` exports its memo only at `BASH_SUBSHELL` 0). The reason is written next to the cell, in the grammar BL-879 reads.
8. The fixture's row is deleted from `scripts/fixture-inprocess-allowlist.txt` in the same change, and BL-879's check passes on it.

**NOTE (item 3, batch b247 close; read at 8f25c6904).** review-reroute starts its subjects only through relocated copies, so BL-879's C1 can never fire on it, and C1's header does not list it among the relocated-copy fixtures. `core/fixtures/review-reroute/run.sh:326-333` (`mkcopy`) copies partition-review-diff.sh, merge-review-shards.sh and the other siblings into `$M/<name>/`, and its `invocations.decl` declares 0 starts for the three subjects. C1's header at `scripts/validate-fixture-invocations.sh:101-106` names only ledger-reverify's mut-memo-*, artifact-derivations' s-m-* and the sib.* stubs. The conversion of review-reroute therefore reaches these starts through item 3's uncounted route, not through C1.

Wall time was not measured for any row; the cost evidence is the counts the gate records (starts, in-process passes, fork total).

**Owed items carried from BL-850 at its closing (batch b247).** Each is part of the conversion of the fixture it names, and none is a choice.

Both carry a matrix block, so neither is a matrix-allowlist row, and both are convert-in-process fixtures under this entry.
- **self-update-gate's W3 base repeats** (BL-850 recorded a24, b3 and d5 per shard, a1's work) are converted with item 4 in `core/fixtures/self-update-gate/run.sh` (the `pl_world w3` world, three lines at the b247 base). The counts are as BL-850 recorded them; they were not re-measured at the b247 base.

Those four are off the allowlist in 0.986.0. The other eleven are still rows of `scripts/fixture-inprocess-allowlist.txt`, each with the subject start counts its `invocations.decl` declares at the 0.986.0 tip (read from each file by script, not at the base): content-seal (core/scripts/content-seal.sh 132 starts; core/hooks/ai-dlc-content-seal.sh 80 starts; core/scripts/merge-adversarial-shards.sh 20 starts; core/scripts/join-remediator-shards.sh 8 starts; core/scripts/validate-gate-adjudication.sh 3 starts; core/scripts/wait-for-deliverable.sh 4 starts), gate-adjudication (core/scripts/validate-gate-adjudication.sh 248 starts), adversarial-shard-merge (core/scripts/merge-adversarial-shards.sh 11 starts; core/scripts/content-seal.sh 397 starts), review-shard-merge (core/scripts/partition-review-diff.sh 4 starts; core/scripts/merge-review-shards.sh 7 starts; core/scripts/content-seal.sh 21 starts; core/scripts/wait-for-deliverable.sh 2 starts; core/hooks/ai-dlc-content-seal.sh 2 starts), wait-beat-liveness (core/scripts/merge-adversarial-shards.sh 33 starts; core/scripts/wait-for-deliverable.sh 5 starts), artifact-derivations (core/scripts/validate-artifact-derivations.sh 17 starts), remediator-shard-join (core/scripts/join-remediator-shards.sh 44 starts; core/hooks/ai-dlc-gate-remediation-guard.sh 64 starts; core/hooks/ai-dlc-derivation-capture.sh 2 starts; core/scripts/serial-remediation-worklist.sh 35 starts), ledger-reverify (core/skills/ai-dlc-update/reconcile/ledger-reverify.sh 7 starts), backlog-receipt-binding (scripts/validate-backlog-receipts.sh 2067 starts), postcompact-rulebook-recovery (core/hooks/ai-dlc-postcompact.sh 3 starts; core/hooks/ai-dlc-recover-gate.sh 2 starts; core/hooks/ai-dlc-recover.sh 3 starts; core/scripts/validate-reattach-budget.sh 3 starts; core/scripts/verdict.sh 1 start), readset-skip (core/scripts/derive-fixture-readsets.sh 87 starts). **Progress (batch 255).** `scripts/validate-enforcement-map.sh` is now loadable and callable in process: sourcing it defines `vem_entry` and returns at a column-0 guard, and its command-line behaviour is byte-identical. `vem_entry` resets the shell state a caller can carry and clears the caller's unexported functions and variables, keeping exported ones and bash's own variables. An earlier draft located the source guard with a longest-suffix strip over the 1.1 MB file, quadratic on bash 3.2 at about 5 CPU-s per call; it is fixed in this release by a `case` presence test, a shortest-prefix strip and a length slice. In-process passes are counted per source, in the unit `core/fixtures/lib/mutant.sh` documents for `fx_inproc`: "one IN-PROCESS pass of a subject: a drive that SOURCES the subject and calls its functions, which starts no bash and so is invisible to the start counter. Call it once per pass, on the line before the `.`/`source` of the subject". So one counted source can feed several in-process calls.

For four fixtures, the counts below are N sources / M calls fed by the subject's source. Each source count equals the fixture's `inproc:` row. Each call count is a static count of `<p>_main` calls per source on the per-push path (no `--matrix`), read from the fixture's `run.sh`:
- file-mtime, 5/9: the subject and the four mutants `mutate()` sources. The subject's source feeds the six `arm_*` arms and the three `eqv` cli twins. Each mutant runs only its deciding arms and its twin: nolocal 3; exitcode, missok and datepath 2 each.
- spec-adoption-floor, 1/18: three `eqv` cli twins, twelve `rcof` calls and three direct calls.
- dist-clone, 5/24: one `src_dc` per `suite()` run, for the real suite and the four mutant suites. `suite()` makes 21 `dc` calls with no loop, and the real subject's source adds the three `eqv` cli twins. Each mutant source feeds 21.
- shadowed-local-validators, 8/4: the real script's source feeds one `run_warn` and the three `eqv` cli twins. The control, the four mutants and the two `refusal_arm` sandboxes each feed one `run_warn_2` call.

The batch-255 paragraph's attribution of validator-fork-budget's validator starts was wrong. It put them on `scripts/fork-profile.sh`'s untagged `bash -x` child and on the renamed profiler copies `fp-*.sh`. One counted hermetic run at 0bd891bd7, after C6 landed, recorded 22 validator starts. Joining each start's parent pid to that parent's logged path splits them:
- 16 from `receipt-bl898.sh`'s `--arms I83,I84` run over five worlds;
- 4 from `receipt-bl981.sh`: 2 live, plus 2 from the m20 stub that C6 added;
- 2 from `scripts/fork-profile.sh`'s traced child;
- 0 from `fp-*.sh`.

So converting the row did not require editing `fork-profile.sh`, and it is unedited.

Rows remaining: 149, against 164 at base. `bash scripts/validate-fixture-inprocess.sh --complete` exits 1 at the tip with `INCOMPLETE  149 allowlist row(s) remain`. The same run's control line is `273 fixture(s) scanned; 122 predicted to pass, 149 refused, 2 out of population`, and `ls core/fixtures/*/run.sh | wc -l` reads 273.

Item 3. escalation-status-vocabulary, dist-clone, hermetic-consumption, claude-rules-joins, derivation-capture and cycle-commits-enforce each gain a `MUTANT-MATRIX` block and a `--matrix` handler. All six leave `scripts/fixture-matrix-allowlist.txt`, which goes from 90 to 84 rows. derivation-capture's block scores its 6 mutants, and J5 joins all 6 names.

`validate-fixture-hygiene.sh`'s H5 S2 no longer counts `mutant` inside the literal path `lib/mutant.sh` in a fixture that has a line sourcing that lib. Without that discount, a converted fixture that scores no mutant read as a battery. S2 has its own self-probe cell. Over 457 fixture `.sh` files, S2's FP set moves 224 -> 223 and the P-row union 228 -> 227; the one fixture that leaves is spec-adoption-floor.

A consumer runs its own installed subject, which can predate the wrap. Each shipped converted fixture whose subject is wrapped here reads that subject's halves before sourcing it: artifact-path-conformance, carried-findings, dist-clone, escalation-delivery, escalation-status-vocabulary, file-mtime, shadowed-local-validators and spec-adoption-floor. path-mapping-render, which is dist-only, carries the same gate.
- Neither the `<p>_main() {` line nor the column-0 guard: the fixture prints `SKIP: subject predates the in-process wrap` and exits 0, never an `ok`.
- Exactly one half: it FAILs, naming the missing half.
- A subject the gate cannot read (`grep` exit 2): FIXTURE ERROR, exit 2, never the SKIP.

The tip adversary ruled that the SKIP cannot be reached over a readable subject that is wrapped but broken. A broken body still carries both halves, so the fixture runs and FAILs. Respelling both halves does reach the SKIP, but in the distribution that run makes zero cli and zero in-process starts, and J3's exact compare turns the push red. So a consumer only ever receives a subject that passed J3. C5 also refuses a cli subject without the guard.

dist-clone keeps the SKIP it had at base for a missing `reconcile/dist-clone.sh` beside an installed `reconcile/`.

wait-stale-deliverable carries the same gate (D1), keyed on its subject's three halves: the `wfd_mark_main() {` line, the `wfd_beat_main() {` line and the column-0 guard. The gate is proven on a tree built by `scripts/install.sh`:
- The current subject passes, with 74 ok.
- A subject from before 0.971.0, when the wrap landed, reads SKIP.
- With the gate deleted, the fixture goes red, with 42 FAIL.
- A subject set to `chmod 000` gives FIXTURE ERROR.

derivation-capture deviates from the gate. A splitter carrying neither `pdoc_main` nor the guard is treated as absent, and the seed takes the header-specified manifest path, which the split line states. That is not a SKIP, and one half alone FAILs. The tip adversary ruled this deviation sound: the splitter is not that fixture's subject (the hook is), and the base seed already took the manifest path when the splitter was absent. Respelling both halves turns J3 red on the 2 cli starts and 4 in-process passes.

carried-findings' `selfloc` mutant now reads `$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")`, because in process `$0` is `run.sh`. At the conversion, its `--matrix` vector measured `W1 P1 S6 G1 G2 H1`, wider than the recorded `W1`. The tip adversary found that widening was an artifact. With no copy inside a checkout, the mutant could not be told apart from an unconditional `return 1` in `locate()`.

The fix: the mutant now sources its copy from inside the R world, and the unmutated control is widened to the topology arms. The want is now the measured `W1 S6 G1 G2 H1`, not the base's `W1`. The mutated property is location-dependence: in process, one sourced copy sits under one review TOP, so the mutant trips every topology arm whose answer depends on that location, not only W1. A fixture cell proves the mutant is not vacuous: a `return 1` copy at the same path FAILS P1, where `selfloc` PASSES it. The matrix comparison stays a set comparison (`same_set`), because `died` lists arms in ARMS order while the want names the decider first.

Left open (D2): `core/scripts/wait-for-deliverable.sh` still reads `$0` in its `--help` and in its `usage:` line. Neither is ported: no in-process drive reaches `--help` or the usage path, and the subject stays unedited because many fixtures share it. If a later in-process drive reaches either path, it prints the caller's script name instead of the subject's.

wait-stale-deliverable, item 5: C14's accepted guard beat was the one beat in the fixture that entered the subject's poll sleep on the default PATH. It now runs under a fast sleep stub with its own virtual clock; the stub records 3 sleeps and its clock reads 3. The condition polls stay real (`deliver_on_sleep`, C02, `run_stale`, `until_event`), and so do the stubs' gate and hold loops: each waits on an event and decides no verdict. The fixture's `--matrix` was re-taken on the converted fixture, with the D1 gate in: 240 cells, 0 FAIL, and 0 mismatches against the block over 22 cells.

Two converted rows' `*` rose: validator-fork-budget (1120 -> 1145, while its validator starts fell from 22 to 8) and wait-stale-deliverable (371 -> 404). Wall time was not measured for any row; the cost evidence is the counts the gate records (starts, in-process passes, fork total).

Item 4, worlds built once (no allowlist row moves):
- artifact-derivations: its git worlds were already built once. Two scratch worlds were rebuilt from identical inputs, the `t_arms` cd root (8 builds) and the `t-a1` render (2 builds). Each is now built once and copied. Three worlds stay fresh, each for its own reason:
  - the split root: `t-failclosed` asserts that it holds only what was seeded;
  - the canary root: `t-m4` lets a canary body dirty it;
  - `inplace_contained`'s reset: its control's bash rewrites `data.txt`.

  After mktemp names are normalised, verdicts are byte-identical, and starts stay at 131.
- apply-restamp-worklist: shard a was already build-once (1 build, 0 repeats, 33 copies). There the logger run and a plain run agree: 1207 starts each, and 68 verdict lines equal once mktemp names and printed shas are normalised. The `ar_w` world is now built once and copied. Starts are unchanged in shards a, b and c (1207, 1809 and 451).
- apply-drift-refile: `seed.sh` now runs once and is copied per call. The in-file builders `h_world`, `t_aworld`, `u_world` and `x_world` build one template per argument list and copy it. `y_init` is not hoisted, because its worlds are built differently per case. Starts fall from 899 to 877.
- snapshot-archive-rotate is build-once, cited by text: `world()` copies a template built once, and the kill-point sweep copies one world per point. Both logger runs on this fixture are VOID. Under the logger, the sweep's first arm lost every worker result and exited 1, and the cause is not yet identified. One toy tested one hypothesis, that a `$( )` inside a backgrounded subshell fires the inherited EXIT trap. Its two sides read equal, so that hypothesis is not supported, which is not evidence that the logger is sound on this fixture.
- backlog-receipt-binding is build-once, cited by text: its `seed()` builds a template once. Its build count is OWED; it needs a counted run at push time, since the fixture is not run outside a push.

A logger reading stands only where a plain run of the same unit agrees with it. A unit whose arms background a function gets the plain pair or a by-text citation, never the logger alone.

An instrument that shadows a command name changes what `command -v` answers, so a fixture that captures its real tool that way runs a different program. That caused this batch's 1,724-process burst and the hang. And a logger keyed on `git init` and `cp -R` cannot see a world built with `mkdir` and `printf`, so its "0 repeats" is a floor, not a count.

Not converted in this release: the remaining allowlist rows, counted above. `inproc:` rows count CALLS, not sources: P2 pairs `fx_inproc` with each call of an entry function, so one source can feed several counted passes. Every shipping converted fixture carries the pre-wrap consumer gate (SKIP on neither half, FAIL on one, FIXTURE ERROR on an unreadable subject); self-update-join-gate and suite-content-key are dist-only.

Deviations from a pure move, each named:
- `self-update-gate.sh` ports `SELF_SRC` to `${BASH_SOURCE[0]}`, and its `gate_load_preclassify` rewrites the `"$0"` inside the eval'd `machinery_paths()` to the loaded preclassify path (`GATE_PRE_SRC`), so an in-process gate resolves the manifest beside that file.
- `preclassify.sh` ports its `_pc_d0` case to `${BASH_SOURCE[0]}` and `setup_sited_paths()` to `${BASH_SOURCE[0]:-$0}` (both preclassify fixtures call it in process; apply.sh evals its text from the preclassify.sh beside it, so the eval'd copy resolves the same directory). `machinery_paths()` and the `SETUP_SITED_PATHS=` line keep `$0` and are byte-identical to the base: self-update-gate.sh, unregistered-drift.sh and three fixtures eval `machinery_paths()`'s text, reconcile-blocking-list runs it under `bash -c`, where `BASH_SOURCE[0]` is empty, and apply-restamp-theirs pairs this preclassify.sh with a pre-R1 apply.sh that extracts the `SETUP_SITED_PATHS=` line under `bash -c`. Left open: an in-process preclassify run whose `skill_commit` differs from BASE resolves `machinery_paths()`'s manifest beside the caller, not beside preclassify.sh.
- `render-agent-definitions.sh` ports its two `$0` reads (the root walk's start and its refusal text) to `${BASH_SOURCE[0]}`; `retired-tokens.sh` ports its `SELF` case and `self-update-fixtures.sh` its `SELF=` line the same way.
- preclassify-rename-row: preclassify refuses a git failure by `kill -USR1 "$PC_TOP"` under `trap 'exit 2' USR1`, which in process would signal the fixture. Each in-process call runs in a background subshell that learns its own pid over a fifo and shadows `kill` to signal that subshell (`mkfifo` is added to tools.decl). Cell mE gives the call an explicit fresh memo directory where the base unset `AI_DLC_RECONCILE_MEMO` for a separate program. preclassify-mode-bucket forces no git failure and ignores USR1.
- askuserquestion-citation's P6 runs a relocated copy of the subject as a program, an untagged start by design (the walk from the script's own location is the property).
- suite-content-key drops a failure-only diagnostic and drives the hook spans through a `bash()` shim: no `command -v`, `type` or `which` sits in the FIXTURE_POOL, SUITE_STEP or SUITE_BANNER spans, the pool's bash calls pass through, and the step's calls of the key script are answered in process.

**Progress (batch 257, W7-03; the entry stays PARTIAL, `verify:` unchanged, no LANDED line).** The installer pair and the rules-floor hook are converted, and so are their two fixtures. Both rows are deleted from `scripts/fixture-inprocess-allowlist.txt`.
- `scripts/install.sh` is wrapped as `ai_dlc_install_main` and `scripts/uninstall.sh` as `ai_dlc_uninstall_main`. `core/hooks/ai-dlc-rules-floor.sh` is wrapped as `arf_main`. Each wrap is followed by the column-0 guard and then `<p>_main "$@"`, and no body line is re-indented. Against the base, the sorted line-multiset diff shows only the seven wrap lines in each file. The one other change is the `SCRIPT_DIR` self-read in install.sh and uninstall.sh, which moves from `$0` to `${BASH_SOURCE[0]:-$0}`.
- One named deviation from a pure move: install-scaffold-ignored runs its edited copies of install.sh as `bash -c "<text>" <real install.sh>`, where `BASH_SOURCE[0]` is empty. The `:-$0` fallback keeps `SCRIPT_DIR` on the base's answer there, and the shipped program always runs as a file. The column-0 guard's `return` is outside any function in that shape and prints bash's "can only return from a function or sourced script" error on stderr; execution continues into the program. A hand differential in that shape, base against tip into fresh targets, gives the same exit, the same stdout (398 lines) and the same file list (1706 entries), and stderr differs by that one line. With the bare `${BASH_SOURCE[0]}` the tip exits 1 after 9 lines, which is the red install-scaffold-ignored reported (AB1 and AB2).
- CLI identity was checked by hand, base against tip, from one `file://` clone and one target path:

Each case runs by relative and by absolute path.
  - Normalising only the sha and `installed_at`, 12 of 63 artifacts differ. Run base against base, the same harness shows 9 of them: the hook's per-run provenance nonce and the `docs/pre-ai-dlc/<stamp>/` archive stamp. The other 3 are the installed hook's own bytes, which `cmp` equal to the same phase's `core/hooks/` source.
