- Both fixtures drive the subjects through `core/fixtures/lib/inproc.sh`'s `inproc_run` and never through `inproc_load`, so each pass is logged under the path it sources, the `$TMP/nested-*` copies included. No call sits under `if`, `!`, `&&` or `||`, because `set -e` is suspended there. Both fixtures refuse a subject that carries one half of the wrap or neither.
  - shipped-rule-version-floor has six cli cells: install.sh exit 0 and 1, uninstall.sh exit 0 and 1, and the hook silent and loud. Each compares stdout, stderr, exit and the target's file list with the in-process call. cli-install-ok is arm F's install and cli-uninstall-ok is arm G's, so neither adds a run. Counts, base -> tip: `*` 33 -> 31, install.sh 5 -> 2, uninstall.sh 3 -> 2, the hook 5 -> 2, plus 6, 4 and 7 inproc passes.
  - schema-install-fallback has two cli cells, install.sh exit 0 and 1. cli-install-ok's in-process twin builds the tree every arm reads. Counts: `*` 50 -> 54, install.sh 2 -> 2, and 3 inproc passes. The rise of 4 is the one extra full install that the equivalence pair needs.

Under `|| :` it runs past the abort.
- `run.sh --nested-only` passes.

**Close condition.** The allowlist is empty and BL-879's check passes over the whole suite. The receipt runs that check in its completion mode, which exits non-zero while any allowlist row remains, so the entry cannot close the day the check lands. At the 0.986.0 base `bash scripts/validate-fixture-inprocess.sh --complete` exits 1 with `  INCOMPLETE  177 allowlist row(s) remain in scripts/fixture-inprocess-allowlist.txt`, so the receipt reads STILL-LIVE for that reason.

The receipt's reads of `scripts/validate-enforcement-map.sh`, `scripts/validate-fixture-hygiene.sh` and `scripts/validate-fixture-invocations.sh` are guarded (W6-10): under `--complete`, an absent or unopenable input makes the driver exit 9, which R13 reads as could-not-measure. R13 still reads `core/fixtures/lib/mutant.sh`, `scripts/fixture-inprocess-allowlist.txt` and the ENUMERATED removal as unguarded (exit 1). Its ceiling is `DEFAULT_MAX_R13` in `scripts/validate-backlog-receipts.sh`, which the two-sided ratchet holds equal to that live count.

## BL-909 — keyword-free mutant-kill verdict lines outside BL-828's 22 delisted fixtures are invisible to M1

**WITHDRAWN (56877eb4d).** Its subject is gone: 0.1130.0 (56877eb4d) deleted `scripts/validate-mutant-kills.sh` (M1 and M8-M10) and `scripts/mutant-verdicts-allowlist.txt`, and `core/fixtures/lib/mutant.sh` now holds only `fx_inproc`. At 39184970b, `git grep -l -E 'mutant_killed[[:space:]]*\(\)'` lists 0 files and `grep -l mutant_killed core/fixtures/*/run.sh` lists only fixture-hygiene's comment saying it was retired; at `56877eb4d^` the same commands list 3 definitions and 216 of 275 run.sh files. This entry's own grammar (`docs/analysis/bl909-loose-grammar.awk`, run as its header says) prints 19 lines in 7 of 294 run.sh files at 39184970b against 546 at `56877eb4d^`; with no `mutant_killed` none of the 19 has a routing target. Whether a fixture still carries a battery is `scripts/validate-fixture-hygiene.sh` H12's question.

**DEFECT.** `scripts/validate-mutant-kills.sh` M1 refuses an unrouted kill only on a line carrying a KILLED/killed token, so a mutant verdict worded without one is never asked to go through `mutant_killed` and its base-pass flag. BL-828 item (3) routed the 15 such lines in the 22 fixtures delisted by 78966dd63; the same residue exists across the rest of the corpus. A loose grammar (non-comment `ok`/`note` lines that name a mutant or mutation, without `mutant_killed` and without KILLED/killed) hits about 669 lines across 154 fixtures, a figure from the b249-c4 contract derivation; its false-positive set is unmeasured. Examples at afea572a5: postcompact-rulebook-recovery :525, :563, :583 (`mutant: a protocol without the mandate FAILS ...`) and process-rule-pins :1529, :1562 (`MUTANT slot-target`, `MUTANT bare-marker`). Owed: measure that false-positive set, enumerated, before scoping any arm.

**Progress (batch b255, release F7; records and data only, no code).** The population is re-derived at cb6e0ab80 with the loose grammar above, committed as `docs/analysis/bl909-loose-grammar.awk` with its output `docs/analysis/bl909-loose-pop-cb6e0ab80.txt` (the run line is in the grammar file's header): non-comment lines of `core/fixtures/*/run.sh` (265 files) carrying `ok` or `note` before a quote, naming a mutant or mutation, with neither `mutant_killed` nor KILLED/killed. It holds 747 lines in 169 fixtures, against the 669 / 154 recorded. A line classifier splits them four ways. N is 108 lines that are not `ok` verdicts (`note` lines, mostly DID NOT APPLY reports). C is 227 control or apply-check lines (unmutated, baseline, anchor, applied, cmp). T is 111 twin, matrix or confinement lines (stays green, intact, only its own). K is the remaining 301, read as kill verdicts. A 30-line hand sample of K read about 83% true kills, so roughly 250 true keyword-free kills in 82 fixtures, and 47 of those 82 never call `mutant_killed` in code (control in the same run: review-shard-merge does). The classes, the sample and the precision are the b255 diagnosis hand's; this release re-derived the 747/169 population (byte-identical to the diagnosis hand's), re-counted the class files, and re-derived the 82/47 split against the cb6e0ab80 tree.

A line-level arm cannot ship. Its false-positive set is about 17% of K plus any C/T line the classifier's word lists miss, and that set cannot be enumerated, because a kill is distinguished from a control by meaning, not by text. The buildable shape is fixture-level. `scripts/validate-mutant-kills.sh --derive` would derive the set of fixtures carrying a K-class line that does not route through `mutant_killed`, and hold it as a down-only list, with the C/T/N word lists as an enumerated deny-mask. Seeds owed before building it: a fixture with a keyword-free kill line and no `mutant_killed`, absent from the list, which must FIRE; the same line routed through `mutant_killed`, which must stay quiet; a C-class control line (`unmutated copy passes`), which the deny-mask must acquit; a K line carrying a deny-mask word in a kill sense (`mutant ... intact` read as a kill), which proves the mask's reach is stated and does not cover the arm's own subject; a listed fixture that has since routed every kill, which must read STALE; and an added row absent at origin/main, which must be refused. BL-909 stays LIVE/PARTIAL.

There is no graph pipeline step behind this entry: it is a coverage gap in M1, and the two `docs/analysis/` files above are its population as data, not a reading of a consumer sprint; the cost lives in scripts/validate-mutant-kills.sh, whose M1 cannot see a keyword-free kill line.

**Progress (batch b255, release F12).** `scripts/validate-mutant-kills.sh` gains M8, M9 and M10 and a derived, count-bearing, down-only list `scripts/mutant-verdicts-allowlist.txt`, one row per fixture, `<fixture><TAB><K-line count>`, rows exactly `--derive-m8`. M8 refuses a fixture whose count of keyword-free mutant verdict lines (K lines, grammar M8_FN, built on F7's `docs/analysis/bl909-loose-grammar.awk`) exceeds its row, an unlisted fixture being 0, and prints every K line with the remedy: route a kill through `mutant_killed`, or name a non-kill sense in the first 40 characters of the message (unmutated, control, baseline, twin, stays green, intact, matrix, did not apply, applied, pairing, cmp -s). M9 refuses a stale row, a row for a fixture with no K line or no run.sh, a duplicate and a malformed row. M10 refuses a row not on origin/main's list or above its count there, so the list only goes down. The list carries a count and not membership because a membership list would acquit every NEW keyword-free kill in each listed fixture, about a third of the corpus, forever; the residual is a K line reworded or moved within a fixture at constant count, which is not seen. M8_FN reads the `ok` opener and, by the lead's ruling, the `echo "ok` and `printf` ok-format openers that F7's census did not. `scripts/validate-fixture-hygiene.sh` H5 reads the list as an added source (MVA).

## BL-910 — enforcement-map-sites I81-M3's C4 cell reads differently under the push's pool than solo (load-dependent, BL-868)

**DEFECT.** In the 0.998.0 gated push (pool width 8, 54 fixtures run), `core/fixtures/enforcement-map-sites` I81-M3 scored 1100 where 1101 is expected, with rc tuple `rc scratch=1 live-root=1 relative=2 inherited-VEM_SELF=1`. Run solo through `core/scripts/hermetic-run.sh`, counted, with a mktemp store, the same tree e90d3a159 and the base 1d1f7c368 both score 1101 with the identical rc tuple. C4's rc is 1 in both, so the bit flipped on C4's other two conditions in `cwd_cells` (`core/fixtures/enforcement-map-sites/run.sh`, the b4 line): `grep -qE "$want" env.out` or `cmp -s s.out env.out`. One of them depends on load or timing. C3's rc=2 is by design, and M3's expected bits are 1101. Neither `validate-enforcement-map.sh` nor the fixture changed in this release. Provenance: filed batch b249, release 0.998.0 gate. Owed: find the shared resource or timing C4 reads (for example the decoy tree `EMS_DECOY`, shared `$CW_DIR` files, or anything concurrent fixtures write) and fix it in the test, never by retry, a widened bound or fewer jobs (BL-868, operator ruling: zero tests depend on load).

**PARTIAL (v0.1043.0).** Forced input rules out static fork limits (they give rc 2, never rc 1 with a 0 bit), a mktemp or TMPDIR failure, async work in `--arms I81` and decoy writes; one transient failure of the subject's own extraction subprocess (`i81_expr_of`'s awk on the acknowledge hook) in C4 alone reproduces 1100 with rc 1/1/2/1 exactly. The cells' outputs were discarded by `cwd_finish` and printed nowhere, so the cause could not be confirmed; the FAIL line of a non-green cwd verdict (`cwd_control`, a `cwd_mutant` SURVIVED, the `cwd_full` M5 SURVIVED) now carries each failing cell's rc, its want-grep result and its diff against C1's `s.out`, through `cwd_why`, which a self-probe in `A43_i81_cwd_invariance` fires on the recorded shape, so the next occurrence names its cause. Owed: read that diff at the next occurrence, then fix in the test per BL-868, never by retry, a widened bound or fewer jobs.

**Progress, batch 257 (stays PARTIAL; `verify: manual` unchanged).** The mutant this entry names (I81-M3) was deleted with the cwd mutation battery in 0.1130.0. The C4 cell survives on the I81 control (`cwd_control`, expected 1111), with the same predicate and `cwd_why` behind it. The push-versus-solo difference in the filing matches one real environmental difference: `git push` runs the hook with SIGPIPE ignored (measured; see BL-1008). Forced on this tree it does not reach C4. `validate-enforcement-map.sh --arms I81`, run from a scratch cwd with every piped awk, sed, grep, sort and tr made to write its first line, pause, then write the rest, under SIGPIPE ignored, took the shim's pipe branch 57 times (the control) and lost 0 writes. The only early-exit reader on that path (`:1240`, `sed -n … | head -1`) has a single-flush writer. Still owed: the cause, read from `cwd_why` at the next occurrence, then the fix in the test (BL-868).

**Progress, batch 257 wave 5 (stays PARTIAL; `verify: manual` unchanged).** An external tool losing writes to an early-exit reader on the `--arms I81` path is ruled out as C4's cause at this entry's tree, by a forcing shape the line above did not use. A scratch PATH shim (grep, sed, awk, sort, cat, cut, tr, uniq, find, ls, comm, head, tail, diff, wc, paste, xargs, basename, dirname, git, md5, shasum, cksum, jq, python3, perl) re-emits a piped tool's output one line per write, pausing 2 ms before each of lines 2 to 61, under SIGPIPE ignored, so a reader that leaves after any of those lines makes its writer report EPIPE. Controls in the same run: `grep . <300000 lines> | head -1` reported EPIPE through the shim, and `grep: stdout: Broken pipe` through the real grep. `--arms I81` on a seeded I81 divergence, from a scratch cwd with VEM_SELF exported (C4's shape), took the shim's pipe branch 57 times at both e90d3a159 (the filing tree) and 39184970b and lost 0 writes, rc 1, the seed named. Without VEM_SELF, and at e3d2dfc35, it also lost 0. The same shim caught 35 to 36 lost writes per full or all-ids run at e90d3a159 and e3d2dfc35 and 0 at 39184970b, from two early-exit readers off the I81 path. The first is I75's `i75_norm` sed feeding `grep -qE` in `i75_failsclosed`, which 0.1081.0 replaced with an awk that reads to the end. The second is I82's `printf … | grep -E '.' | head -1` (`i82b_a1`), which 0.1125.0 replaced with one awk. The grep one matches the one recorded EPIPE red, `validator-arm-selection` at 2026-11-12T11:29Z (`.git/ai-dlc-fixture-failures.20261112T112907Z.80628`: the all-ids run's stderr gained `grep: stdout: Broken pipe`). Builtin writers are outside the shim. A one-line scan of the prologue, the I81 unit and the verdict block for `printf` or `echo` piped into `head`, `grep -q`/`-m`, a quitting `sed` or an exiting `awk` finds only I81's probe text, which is written to a file (7 matches file-wide at each tree, the control). Multi-line pipelines are outside that scan. No diagnosed recurrence exists: the 20 failure archives from 2026-11-06 to 2026-11-15 carry no enforcement-map-sites section (they carry 49 red-unit sections in all). Still owed: the cause, from `cwd_why` at the next occurrence, then the fix in the test (BL-868).

**PARTIAL (v0.1145.0) — a seed's red now keeps `cwd_why`'s evidence; the cause and the fix are OPEN.** `cwd_why` writes its evidence (each failing cell's rc, its want-grep result, its diff against C1's `s.out`) as separate lines indented `      | ` under the one `  FAIL  ` line `bad` prints. `scripts/seed-verdict-store.sh` printed a red unit's output only on lines matching `FAIL|J[1-5]|BROKEN|ERROR|REQUIRED input|^hermetic-run: <fx>: rc=` and deleted the rest with its work directory on exit, and `scripts/pregate-self-check.sh` deletes its `--keep` copies on exit and prints a tail of the same filtered lines, so an occurrence in a seed or a pregate would have lost the evidence this entry waits for. The red-print filter now also passes `^      [|] `, held by `core/fixtures/seed-verdict-store-red-print` arm a5. A pregate red still shows only the last 20 lines of that print (`scripts/pregate-self-check.sh:324`), so a long red's evidence can lose its head there. Push reds were never affected: the push's failure archive keeps each red unit's whole output. A bash fork retry adding a stray stderr line to one cell (rc unchanged, `cmp -s` failing) is refuted for `/bin/bash` 3.2.57: under a static `ulimit -u` below the live process count, fork EAGAIN is fatal and prints `fork: Resource temporarily unavailable` with no `fork: retry` line. A limit exhausted only for a moment was not forced. No recurrence: the 20 timestamped push failure archives the hook keeps in the main checkout's `.git` (every worktree's push writes there), 2026-11-09T02:40Z to 2026-11-15T16:00Z, carry 32 red-unit sections (the control) and no enforcement-map-sites section; of the live record and `.clean`, only `.clean` carries one. Still owed: the cause, from `cwd_why` at the next push or seed occurrence, then the fix in the test (BL-868).

## BL-974 — a record-format error in a shard or repair part is found by the lead's join, so one malformed line costs a resume round trip

**DEFECT (efficiency).** An adversary or remediator seals its part, the lead runs the merge or join, the join refuses the part on one line (an `artifact_sha:` stem in the provenance block, an `edit:` line that does not resolve, a coverage gap in the part record), and the lead resumes the finished agent to correct that line. Population for every figure: graph's Claude Code transcripts, 151 lead and 6,018 subagent, read only, window end 2026-11-11T10:30:00Z, extracted by `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s320/scripts/extract.py` with the s321/s322 edge at 2026-11-09T00:00:00Z; s322 is partial (planning complete, 3 of 8 stories done). In-session time only; handoff gaps are excluded by ruling. Resume messages to finished subagents, classified by their text: s321 13 join or merge refusals with 4.5 agent-hours of idle before the resume (21 minutes each); s322 16 with 11.2 agent-hours (42 minutes each). Each loop is also a lead turn to diagnose, a message, and a second join. Dollars: about $30 across the 29 loops, estimated at about $1 a resume; not measured per loop. The idle is agent idle, an upper bound on wall clock, because other agents may run meanwhile. Instances: s322 `requirements-p2/6.md` (`artifact_sha:` stem), s322 `requirements-repair-p1/1.md` (`edit:` line of F-1), s321 `stories-party-repair-p1/15.md` (coverage gap at line 112).

Fix direction, not built: the writer runs the join's own per-part check before it seals, so the refusal reaches the agent while it still holds the context. `--check-part` exists and the s322 agents ran it clean before the join refused them, so the gap is the difference between what `--check-part` checks and what the join and merge refuse. Close that difference in the per-part check; do not add a second checker. False-positive set and consumer cost unmeasured.

Baseline: s321
Step: requirements
Expected movement: join and merge refusals that end in a resume of the writing agent fall from 13 (s321) and 16 (s322 partial) a sprint to 2 or fewer, and no part that passed its own per-part check is refused by the join for a format reason.

Decision rule: the step-value ruling, "A step with no measured value goes" (`.claude/rules/operator-rulings.md`). Related: BL-804 (the `derivations: not replayed` refusal, one member of this class), BL-532, BL-544.

Receipt: `verify: manual`; closes on an `Outcome measured:` line counting refusal resumes on a graph sprint. What else would satisfy a weaker receipt: a fixture showing `--check-part` refuses one of the three named lines passes while the other refusal classes stay join-only. What this does not establish: which refusal classes exist beyond the three instances read; the 29 were counted by message text and not enumerated by class.

Provenance: batch 255 efficiency analysis (branch b255-efficiency-analysis), measured by the analysis session; id allocated by lead ai-dlc-7f.

**PARTIAL (v0.1057.0) — the seal now runs the join's own per-part check, so a format refusal reaches the writer at `--mark`; the outcome on a graph sprint is still owed.** Expected, as measured: the 29 resumes are 23 format refusals and 6 not this entry (5 BL-976 shard-dir corrections, 1 misclassified NEEDS_REWORK). Of the 23, 21 have a join refusal behind them; 2 do not (escalation closure is reported and never refused, `join-remediator-shards.sh` header "an open escalation is the lead's"; party-source coverage has no join arm), so refusing them at the seal would refuse bytes the join accepts. Of the 21, 20 are decidable at the seal and 1 is merge-only (the INVOKED_AT BOUND needs the dispatch id, whose probe row is written at SubagentStop). The reachable movement is therefore the 20, leaving 3 of the 23 per two sprints (s321+s322) that still end in a resume. `wait-for-deliverable.sh --mark` now runs `merge-adversarial-shards.sh --check-shard` on an adversary shard and `join-remediator-shards.sh --check-part` on a term-5 part, and refuses the seal (exit 64, nothing written) on the join's own REFUSED lines. Receipts: on real pre-fix bytes recovered from graph's transcripts, 20 of the 21 are refused at the seal (the miss is INVOKED_AT, merge-only by design), and the corrected bytes mark clean for every class but the three lanes, whose corrected bytes graph's history does not hold; a false-positive census over graph HEAD found 0 false positives in 1115 merged adversary shards and 890 merged term-5 parts, with a real-join differential (identical refusal sets, join against `--check-part`, 4 dirs, two engines) on the parts the census refused. ## BL-975 — a subagent killed by an API connection error is noticed about an hour later

**DEFECT (efficiency).** Nothing reports a subagent whose run has ended on an API error. Population for every figure: graph's Claude Code transcripts, 151 lead and 6,018 subagent, read only, window end 2026-11-11T10:30:00Z, extracted by `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s320/scripts/extract.py` with the s321/s322 edge at 2026-11-09T00:00:00Z; s322 is partial (planning complete, 3 of 8 stories done). In-session time only; handoff gaps are excluded by ruling. On 2026-11-09 nine requirements remediators were dispatched together at 17:54Z (parts 1 to 9); four of them (parts 5 to 8) died within four minutes, and the lead later resumed five ("Repair part 1, 3, 4, 8, 9 brief"), four of them agents that had already delivered (see the premise-corrected paragraph below), between 19:00Z and 19:08Z with "Your run terminated on an API connection error (the cause is now resolved)". An earlier reading counted four resume messages, with 62 to 72 minutes of idle before each (4.2 agent-hours); the premise-corrected paragraph below supersedes it (five resumes, and each of the four dead seats ended in an unanswered `api-request`). s321 has none. Wall clock: 1.2 h for the event, on the critical path, since the round could not join without dead parts 5 to 8. Dollars: $0. Frequency: one event in the two sprints read. Judged on consequence: every occurrence stalls a whole round until the lead happens to look.

Fix direction, not built: the wait the lead is already armed on reports an agent that has exited without sealing its deliverable, or whose transcript has had no record for longer than a bound while a request is open, as FAILED with the agent named, instead of continuing to wait. `wait-for-deliverable.sh` already has FAILED and MISSING states; the question is what signal sets them for a dead agent. No timer-based retry is asked for.

Baseline: s322
Step: requirements
Expected movement: a beat names a dispatched agent whose last record is an unanswered api-request within `AI_DLC_OPEN_REQUEST_SECS` (1200 s) plus one 60 s check of that request, and an agent with a harness failed notice at its next check, while the lead can take a turn. Deviation, stated plainly: the reachable bound for the open-request arm is about 21 minutes after the last send (1200 s plus one 60 s check), and that does NOT meet the entry's original "under 10 minutes" wording. The harness-failed-notice arm still fires at its next check.

Decision rule: the step-value ruling, "A step with no measured value goes" (`.claude/rules/operator-rulings.md`). Related: BL-538 (wake beats), BL-576.

Receipt: `verify: manual`; the mechanism's test is the behavioural fixture `core/fixtures/wait-beat-liveness`, which drives the wait over agents that died without their deliverable; the entry closes only on the `Outcome measured:` line its PARTIAL paragraph names. What else would satisfy a weaker receipt: a prose instruction to the lead to check on silent agents. What this does not establish: why the lead noticed after an hour and not sooner. The step is `requirements`, corrected from `gate-validation[carry-over-evaluation]`, which was the pre-0.1100.0 extractor's label for these spawns while the lead had left the planning gate open (BL-1003). Each of the nine 17:54Z remediators (parts 1 to 9) opens on a brief at graph's `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/s322/rounds/requirements-repair-p1/<n>.brief`, whose second line reads "sprint 322, step requirements"; the seven `carry-over-evaluation-party-repair-p1` briefs, as a control, read "step carry-over-evaluation". Re-extracted over graph's transcripts to EXTRACT_WIN_END=2026-11-12T18:53:06Z, joined by each agent's meta.json `toolUseId`, the extractor at 0.1099.0 owns all 9 spawns to `gate-validation[carry-over-evaluation]` and the 0.1100.0 extractor owns all 9 to `requirements`, while the 7 spawns whose brief sits under `rounds/carry-over-evaluation-party-repair-p1/` read `carry-over-evaluation` on both.

## BL-972 — a pending AskUserQuestion holds the lead, so a lane that finishes during the question waits for the answer

**DEFECT (efficiency).** Population for every figure: graph's Claude Code lead transcripts and their subagent transcripts, read only, to 2026-11-11T10:30:00Z: 247 asks over s317–s322, with sprints from the `EDGES` in `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s320/scripts/extract.py`. s322 is partial. In-session time only; handoff gaps are excluded by ruling. Measured by `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s322/scripts/ask_split.py` (output `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s322/out/ask_split.txt`). Its per-sprint call counts and hours equal `operator_asks.py`'s for s317–s321. A wait is FINISHED-INSIDE when a subagent of the same lead session, started before the ask, wrote its last record during it. FINISHED-INSIDE minutes of all answer-wait minutes: s317 197 of 398, s318 271 of 811, s319 164 of 1113, s320 791 of 3052 (26%), s321 164 of 1576 (10%), s322 20 of 537. Those lanes are not joined or followed while the question is open. 290 task notifications were enqueued inside 77 of the 247 asks: 133 from agents, the rest from background Bash and Monitor. Every FINISHED-INSIDE ask has at least one agent notification. Yet no notification-carrying attachment or user record, and no lead assistant record, falls between any question and its answer (`DLV-INSIDE` 0, `ACT-INSIDE` 0). The script's self-probe proves both counters fire on a seeded inside record and stay at 0 on a same-message near-miss. 67 queue `remove` records do fall inside, and no record shows the lead's model receiving those before the answer. Control, asserted in the script: BL-841's 10-27 06:57Z ask scores FINISHED-INSIDE (95 min; two subagents finished inside it). Dollars: $0, a wait bills nothing.

The ceiling, stated so the entry is not read as owning the whole 26.3 h. The figure that names this defect is FINISHED-INSIDE. Most of s321's largest bucket, NONE-RUNNING, 1086 of 1576 minutes (69%), is deploy-validate asks with nothing running: 940 minutes, including the 645-minute "Router PUT" and the 264-minute "Merge #1305". These are operator actions on the serial deploy path, and no lead-side mechanism shortens an operator's answer. FINISHED-INSIDE also UNDERCOUNTS, in two ways. An agent that finished inside a wait and was resumed later has its last record after the ask: 3 asks outside FINISHED-INSIDE (397 min, all sprints) carry an enqueued agent `finished` notification. And background Bash and Monitor completions held by an ask are not bucketed at all. A lead that keeps working while a question is open could also dispatch ready lanes inside a wait of any bucket, and no transcript says where those were.

**Correction (2026-11-11, b255 F4b).** `ask_split.py` now deduplicates asks by `tool_use` id, so one s321 ask (23.9 min) that sits in two lead files is counted once. Under the deduplicated script the population above reads 246 asks, and s321 reads 1552 min with NONE-RUNNING 1063 and deploy-validate 916, against 247, 1576, 1086 and 940. FINISHED-INSIDE 164 is unchanged in minutes, and its share reads 164 of 1552 (11%) where the s321 figure above says 164 of 1576 (10%); NONE-RUNNING reads 1063 of 1552 (68%) where the ceiling paragraph says 1086 of 1576 (69%); any reading of s321's FINISHED-INSIDE minutes is therefore against 164 of 1552. The receipt's s321 ask-count baseline is `ask_split.py`'s deduplicated 56, not `operator_asks.py`'s 57. The two tools differ by that one duplicated row, so a closer must compare the ask count only within `ask_split.py`'s output, never across the two tools, and a count of 56 against 57 is not a fall in asks.

Fix direction, built at batch 256 (the Progress paragraph below). The contact shape is ruled. Immediately before the question, the lead joins every finished lane and dispatches every lane the question does not hold. The question itself goes out at once, in `AskUserQuestion`, unchanged. Asking in prose instead of the tool is NOT this fix: Rule 11(a) requires `AskUserQuestion` for an ambiguity question, and `core/hooks/ai-dlc-continue.sh:1637` records the measured failure of the prose form (the operator: "you haven't asked any question ... buried in the transcript"). No variant may suppress, batch away, defer or hold back a question, or lower the ask count. Whether the harness offers a non-blocking ask is unmeasured; BL-994 owns that probe.

**Progress (batch 256).** Operator ruling, batch 256, on contact shape: option 1, "dispatch, then ask". Immediately before each `AskUserQuestion`, the lead joins every finished lane and dispatches every lane the question does not hold, then asks at once in `AskUserQuestion`, unchanged. Option 2 (a non-blocking ask) and option 3 (accept the hold) were not chosen; option 2's harness question is filed as BL-994. Landed: `core/skills/ai-dlc/SKILL.md` gains the subsection `#### Operator decisions: dispatch, then ask (Rule 11(a))`, and Rule 11(a) item (4) now cites it. `core/fixtures/process-rule-pins/run.sh` pins that subsection's paragraph with the `exact`-mode row `r11a-dispatch-ask`, and its deferral mutants `r11a-dispatch-ask-D1` (the paragraph replaced by "Before every `AskUserQuestion`, wait until every running lane has finished, join them, then ask.") and `r11a-dispatch-ask-D2` (the drain sentence "Ask once every running lane has finished." appended) are both killed. The pin's `exact` row also requires its subsection to hold exactly one paragraph, so a drain paragraph added beside it fails the pin too. `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s322/scripts/ask_split.py` gains the HELD-AT-ASK, DISPATCHED-AT-ASK and ADJ-DISPATCH columns that the Receipt below reads. Two contract-adversary findings, which are the adversary's figures and are not re-derived here. First, item (4) of Rule 11(a) ("Dispatch ready actions on free lanes ...") was already live in graph's installed engine through s321, which ran 0.969.0 to 0.1028.0. Second, at all 10 of s321's FINISHED-INSIDE asks nothing was visibly held: the lanes finished after the question went out, so a join before the ask cannot reach them. ACT-INSIDE is dropped as the close key. It counts lead records positioned while the ask's tool_result is pending (`ask_split.py`'s ACT counter), so under a blocking ask a compliant lead reads 0 by construction, and DLV-INSIDE is 0 on every baseline ask. The literal first-draft HELD and DISPATCHED definitions failed too. Tail-only lanes scored as dispatched; DISPATCHED now opens a lane only at a harness-confirmed dispatch or resume and closes it at the agent's terminal notice, with no subagent record timestamp and no 300 s tail. Race notices scored as held; HELD now counts only an agent notice enqueued before the `api-request` record that began the ask's response, and one enqueued after it is a race and is not held. Known limits of the pin: inside its subsection the `exact` row does not catch a drain instruction written as a `#####` sub-heading, because headings are skipped; one inside a fenced block; or one inside an HTML comment, because fences and comments are invisible to every test. The lead model reads raw markdown, so these are real survivors; closing them changes the fixture's grammar for every pin, which is a separate change.

That anchor agrees with the api anchor on all 207 api-anchored asks (FB-DISAGREE 0, with 39 asks on the fallback). The ask count must not fall. A fall against s321's 56 is a contact regression: it is never credited, and it blocks the close whatever else moved. The count is compared only within `ask_split.py`'s output, never against `operator_asks.py`'s 57 (the Correction above). DISPATCHED-AT-ASK (s321 16 of 56, 28.6%, same file) is reported beside the close and is not a close condition. What else would satisfy a weaker receipt: a lead that holds a question back until its lanes drain. That turns every FINISHED-INSIDE wait into NONE-RUNNING and moves the minutes to 0 with the ask count intact. It is the deferral this entry forbids. The pure deferral dispatches nothing adjacent to the ask and reads ADJ 0, which is why the close is keyed on ADJ-DISPATCH and not on minutes. ADJ-DISPATCH alone cannot tell a lead that drains its lanes and then dispatches (it reads ADJ 1, HELD 0) from a compliant one (lead ruling, batch 256; `ask_split.py` probe (q) records it as the KNOWN LIMIT `defer_plus_bg`). DRAINED-AT-ASK is the column a reader checks for that shape. It is reported, never a close guard. ACT-INSIDE is no longer the key, because a compliant lead reads 0 on it by construction under a blocking ask. Fewer or faster-answered questions shrink every bucket without the mechanism, which the ask-count rule refuses. What this does not establish: whether every ready action was dispatched cannot be measured from a transcript, because no record names the actions that were ready. ADJ-DISPATCH shows a background dispatch next to the ask, not that it was the whole ready set. Subagents dispatched from another lead session are not joined, and asks issued from a subagent are not counted. Every column here is positional against a blocking ask. Every one is keyed on (timestamp, file index) or on message.id. It exits 0.
- RMV, DLV and ACT are positional in file order. The probe reports them and never asserts them: RMV and DLV read 1 early and 0 on both late orders. So a late write zeroes those three by construction, and none of them is a close key.
- Hand-run mutants, once. Keying `ask_split.py`'s positions on file index alone (`pos = (i, i)`) is killed on both late orders: race 0 against 1, and lanes running `['aL', 'aN']` against `['aN']`. Dropping the late file's background dispatch is killed by the same-lines control. With that control's exit removed, it is killed by ADJ (1 against 0) and by lanes running.

## BL-984 — nothing owns `tools.decl` completeness, so an undeclared tool silently kills a check

**DEFECT.** `core/scripts/hermetic-run.sh:608` runs every fixture under `env -i PATH="$HR_BIN"`, and `$HR_BIN` (`:556-565`) is a farm of the tools that fixture's `tools.decl` declares, in both layouts. A tool the fixture calls and does not declare is `command not found`, rc 127. Where that call sits in a branch condition (`if cmp -s`, `||`, `!`, `$( )`) or under `2>/dev/null`, the 127 is read as an ordinary answer and the check it feeds stops checking, while the fixture still prints PASS. BL-983 is the measured instance: both of advisor-gate-deny's DID-NOT-APPLY guards were dead. Nothing refuses this. hermetic-run resolves DECLARED tools only, and exits 2 on one that is declared and absent. `scripts/validate-fixture-hygiene.sh` H2 covers only `command -v <declared tool>` skips. `scripts/census-hermetic-coverage.sh` is a census, not a gate, and it compares ok-line sets, so it would read advisor-gate-deny EQUAL.

Proposed mechanism, not built (lead ruling, F15): a hermetic-run.sh arm that fails a fixture whose run log carries `command not found`. Its known limits. Consumer cost: hermetic-run.sh is the shipped engine, so the arm runs on every consumer push and can fail a consumer's own fixtures. False-positive set: unmeasured, for example a fixture that deliberately runs a missing command and does not redirect it. Blind spots: it is blind to redirected stderr (`2>/dev/null`, `2>&1` into a capture), which is exactly the SWALLOWED shape. It is also blind to code that runs only on a failing branch, or only under `--matrix`, which a green run never executes. So a clean log is a FLOOR, not a proof, and the static half of the census below has to stand beside it.

Starting population (the F15 census, static candidates plus executing confirmation under hermetic-run; the pairs this entry's progress paragraph lists as declared were declared in this release, and every other instance stays open as stated under LEFT OPEN):

CENSUS (taken at f76449762 by the F15 census hand; re-checked at the F15 tip). The method: every fixture with a static candidate, 179 of 270, was run through `core/scripts/hermetic-run.sh` with a fresh mktemp verdict store, 4 in parallel. A hit counts only when execution confirms it. PLAIN means an ANSI-stripped `command not found` line in a plain run. TRAP means a BASH_ENV DEBUG trap logged a command whose first word failed `type -t`, which also catches stderr-redirected sites. FORCED means the branch was forced in a scratch copy and that copy run through hermetic-run. Controls in the same runs: advisor-gate-deny read PLAIN 2 and TRAP 2, and absorbed-specifics-survive, whose decl is complete, read PLAIN 0 and TRAP 0. The executing count is a FLOOR. The census fixtures are declared in part: 37 fixtures and 51 (fixture, tool) pairs now carry the tool in `tools.decl` (listed in the progress paragraph below), and the rest are not declared. Operator ruling (b255): the declarations ship as a follow-up release, b256's opener by default.
- LOUD (PLAIN, the fixture still rc 0): advisor-gate-deny `cmp` (BL-983); prepush-ssh-keepalive `tr` (run.sh:258; :253 is mx_twins' definition line); readset-stage1-verdict `basename` (run.sh:262, :474, :478) and `cksum` (:466).
- FAILING-BRANCH-ONLY (FORCED): apply-legacy-script-path `diff` (run.sh:339); wait-beat-liveness `uniq` (run.sh:4450; the FAIL message loses its content).
- --MATRIX-ONLY (FORCED with MATRIX=1; 0 in a default run): retro-branch-behind-main `wc` (run.sh:474, :587); fold-architect-ledger-join `cut` (run.sh:580-581).
- SWALLOWED (TRAP, plain rc 0): escalation-citation and transient-ignore-block `md5` (`md5 -q … 2>/dev/null || md5sum`, so the before and after hashes were both empty and compared equal); pause-hook-origin `shasum` (ai-dlc-pause.sh:250, so REQ_SHA was empty); spec-join-integrity `wc` (settled-net.sh:18); dispatch-model-guard `date` and `od`; answer-handoff-routing, core-write-guard and operator-request-capture `od`, `tail` and `mv`, and upstream-routing the same plus `date` (ai-dlc-context-provenance.sh:95/120/125, `2>/dev/null || true`); askuserquestion-citation `git` (run.sh:278, `git -C … >/dev/null 2>&1` inside an `if`, BL-983's shape); adversarial-shard-merge and process-rule-pins `stat` (wait-for-deliverable.sh:528, where STAT_FLAVOR falls to gnu); apply-restamp-theirs, apply-machinery-stamp, apply-relabel-noop-row, emit-report-refusal, layer-crosswalk-home and self-update-fixture-log `uniq` (layer-drift.sh:1773); apply-relabel-noop-row `chmod` (apply.sh:571); apply-worklist-rows and layer-qualifier-grain `mv`; backlog-ledger `sort` (backlog-rotate.sh:541-542); cycle-commits-enforce `sort`; shell-portability `sort` and `paste`; adversarial-citation, backlog-real-ledger, backlog-receipt-binding and bmad-invocation-resolve `rmdir`; h2-attest-scripts-dir `seq` (check-17-bypass/run.sh:260); owed-derivations `ls`; prepush-pool-depth `diff`, `head` and `tee`; readset-skip-b `perl`; remediator-shard-join and validator-arm-selection-b `jq`; retro-branch-behind-main `find` (in a mutated copy of validate-mandatory-rules.sh); wait-beat-liveness and wait-stale-deliverable `perl`.

The probe: `env -i PATH=/nonexistent BASH_ENV=<file defining command_not_found_handle> /bin/bash run.sh`, where run.sh prints a control line and then calls a command that is on no PATH. Under /bin/bash 3.2.57 the control line printed, the exit was rc=127, and the handler recorded 0 hits, so the handler never ran. `man bash` agrees: `command_not_found_handle` has 0 mentions and `BASH_ENV` has 6. So a handler cannot be injected through BASH_ENV on the interpreter the sandbox launches. That interpreter is `HR_BASH`, resolved at `core/scripts/hermetic-run.sh:581` as the first `bash` found over the fixed tool dirs (`READSET_TOOL_DIRS`, `.githooks/pre-push:2036`, with `/opt/homebrew/bin` first); no bash 4 or newer exists on the box, so the first hit is the 3.2 one. BASH_ENV is not free for such a handler either: `core/scripts/hermetic-run.sh:600-615` sets it only when all three of FXCOUNT_RC, FXCOUNT_DIR and FXCOUNT_JUDGE are set, and per :601 never on a consumer, and when set it already names the invocation counter's rc. The launch is `env -i PATH="$HR_BIN" ... "$HR_BASH" run.sh` at `core/scripts/hermetic-run.sh:617-619`, over the farm built at `:565-576`. Its siblings `-b`, `-c` and `-d` were green in the same seeding. A solo run then returned rc 0 with 48 ok, and the fixture was ok in ai-dlc-48's gate from 13:35 to 13:47.

Provenance: batch 256, filed by ai-dlc-a7 (lead); id allocated by the lead.

**Progress, batch 257 (stays PARTIAL; `verify: manual` unchanged).** One candidate interleaving was forced and refuted. A `git push` runs its pre-push hook with SIGPIPE ignored, and ignored signals survive exec, so a pushed fixture's writers take EPIPE where a solo run's die silently. Measured in a scratch repo: inside the hook, `yes | head -1` printed `yes: stdout: Broken pipe` with status 1; outside git it exited 141 silently. Forced on a scratch seed: a PATH shim made every awk, sed, grep, cat, sort, uniq, tr, cut, find, ls and git whose stdout is a pipe write its first line, pause 0.2 s, then write the rest, under SIGPIPE ignored, so any early-exit reader exits before its writer finishes. A full `validate-enforcement-map.sh` run (A00's) took the shim's pipe branch 2519 times (the control) and lost 0 writes: rc 0, 0 `FAIL:` lines. Every early-exit reader in the validator sits behind a single-flush writer. Stdin kind, forced as well, since A00's control is the only `--run-one` started outside xargs and so inherits `run.sh`'s stdin: the full run with stdin a socket whose peer stays open and silent (a stdin reader would block) finished rc 0 with no hang. Stdin closed gave rc 0, and `/dev/null` (the control) gave rc 0. Also ruled out by reading: the validator reads no clock, starts no background job and uses no process substitution; under `hermetic-run.sh` the template and decoy are private and `tree_state` checks them. The one recorded occurrence of the A00 message (`.git/ai-dlc-fixture-failures.clean`, Sep 19) predates the 0.1108.0 stderr print, so no diagnosed recurrence exists. Still owed: the cause, read from the A00 stderr print at the next occurrence, then the fix in the test (BL-868).

**Progress, batch 257 wave 5 (stays PARTIAL; `verify: manual` unchanged).** The Sep 19 occurrence is a content red, not load: in the same run `layer-contract-conformance-b`'s control printed `validate-enforcement-map.sh`'s I63 finding on `core/skills/ai-dlc/SKILL.md`, and six fixtures that run that validator over a pristine tree read FIXTURE BROKEN together (`.git/ai-dlc-fixture-failures.clean`). ai-dlc-48's seed is therefore the only load-shaped occurrence on record. The two EPIPE writers found under BL-910's per-line shim with SIGPIPE ignored (I75 and I82, both since removed) did not move A00's rc: full runs lost 35 and 36 writes at e90d3a159 and e3d2dfc35 and still exited 0, and lost 0 at 39184970b, rc 0. That seed's tree, 680965157, also predates the runner's ignored SIGPIPE: its `core/scripts/hermetic-run.sh` carries 0 `trap '' PIPE` lines (0.1100.0 added it). No diagnosed recurrence exists: the 20 failure archives from 2026-11-06 to 2026-11-15 carry no enforcement-map-sites section. Still owed: the cause, from the A00 stderr print at the next occurrence, then the fix in the test (BL-868).

**PARTIAL (v0.1145.0) — a seed's red now keeps A00's stderr print; the cause and the fix are OPEN.** The one load-shaped occurrence was a seed, and the seed path dropped the evidence this entry waits for. A00 prints the validator's stderr (or "(the validator wrote nothing to stderr)") as lines indented `      | ` under the one `  FAIL  ` line `bad` prints. `scripts/seed-verdict-store.sh` printed a red unit's output only on lines matching `FAIL|J[1-5]|BROKEN|ERROR|REQUIRED input|^hermetic-run: <fx>: rc=` and deleted the rest with its work directory on exit, so only the validator's own `FAIL:` lines survived; a `tool-fail:` line, a `vem_entry` refusal, a shell error and the empty-stderr line did not. `scripts/pregate-self-check.sh` deletes its `--keep` copies on exit and prints a tail of the same filtered lines. The red-print filter now also passes `^      [|] `, held by `core/fixtures/seed-verdict-store-red-print` arm a5. A pregate red still shows only the last 20 lines of that print (`scripts/pregate-self-check.sh:324`), so a long red's evidence can lose its head there. Push reds were never affected: the push's failure archive keeps each red unit's whole output. Correction to the Evidence paragraph: at 680965157 A00 ran `vem_entry >/dev/null 2>&1`, and that tree's seeder carried the same filter, so "0 fork or not-found lines" could not see the validator's stderr and does not exclude a fork or tool failure inside the validator. A bash fork retry is refuted as a silent cause for `/bin/bash` 3.2.57: under a static `ulimit -u` below the live process count, fork EAGAIN is fatal and prints `fork: Resource temporarily unavailable`, with no `fork: retry` line; `kern.maxprocperuid` is 10666, above the occurrence's 3,877 processes. A limit exhausted only for a moment was not forced. No recurrence: the 20 timestamped push failure archives the hook keeps in the main checkout's `.git`, 2026-11-09T02:40Z to 2026-11-15T16:00Z, carry 32 red-unit sections (the control) and no enforcement-map-sites section; of the live record and `.clean`, only `.clean` carries one. Still owed: the cause, from the A00 stderr print at the next push or seed occurrence, then the fix in the test (BL-868).

## BL-1005 — s321 stories adversarial p2 and p3 made 19 shard reads of story text unchanged since the prior pass, at $7.57 and 0.48 spawn-hours, for 0 CRITICAL or MAJOR

**NOTE (efficiency).** OPEN. Filed 2026-11-12. This is ONE sprint's reading: graph s321, from `docs/analysis/step-value-stories-adversarial-s321.md`, section "Shards that re-read unchanged bytes", with the mechanical figures from `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s322/scripts/adversarial_yield.py`. A shard re-read unchanged text when its `artifact_sha` equals the same ordinal's in the prior pass. In p2 that is 5 of 25 shards ($1.86, 0.10 spawn-hours) and in p3 14 of 25 ($5.71, 0.38). Together that is 19 reads, $7.57 and 0.48 spawn-hours (the spawns' own minutes summed, not wall clock, $0.30 per story over 25 stories), and those shards raised 0 CRITICAL or MAJOR. The p2 and p3 passes as a whole read KEEP ("Keep while repairs write defects"): all 6 of their MAJORs sit in text the previous repair wrote, 4 of them on the native-spend bound. The document records the unchanged-text shards as a narrow candidate only, because 19 reads in one sprint rule out a frequent catch and not a rare high-consequence one.

Candidate change, shipped as BL-911 item 1: files-mode `merge-adversarial-shards.sh --derived-map` narrows a pass after the first to the parts whose bytes changed since the predecessor, the parts its CRITICAL and MAJOR findings cite, and the cross shard (`core/scripts/merge-adversarial-shards.sh:209`), and the merge refuses a shard outside that set (`:1958`). A story whose bytes the prior pass already read is not re-dispatched unless a CRITICAL or MAJOR finding cites it.

Progress (batch 257, the s322 reading; the zero does not discriminate, so this is not the decision count and the entry stays OPEN with `verify: manual`). Read from a scratch `file://` clone of graph at 6c30b864f with the comparison `adversarial_yield.py` uses: the shards in `shards/stories-pM` whose `artifact_sha` equals the same ordinal's in `stories-p(M-1)`, cross excluded. s322 stories p2 has 0 of 5 such shards, so 0 CRITICAL or MAJOR, $0 and 0 spawn-hours, and s322 has no stories p3. The zero is no evidence for or against narrowing: the p1 repair touched all 5 stories, so `--derived-map` owed all 5 and no unchanged-text shard was dispatched. Control in the same run: the same code on s321 reproduces 5 (p2) and 14 (p3) unchanged shards with 0 CRITICAL or MAJOR among them. The decision count is still owed from a sprint in which some p2 or p3 story is unchanged since the prior pass.

What decides it: the same count on the next measured graph sprint, by the same script: the p2 and p3 shards whose `artifact_sha` equals the prior pass's, and the CRITICAL or MAJOR findings they raise, with dollars and spawn-hours. Narrow if that sprint also reads 0 CRITICAL or MAJOR from those shards. Keep the full width if any such shard raises a CRITICAL or MAJOR that survives to the stories gate, since the narrowed pass could not have made that catch. Keep it also on any high-consequence catch from those shards, whatever the count, under the high-consequence guard. A catch that depends on another story's changed text, read through an unchanged story, is the case to look for, because the narrowing would drop it.

Baseline: s321
Step: stories-test-strategy
Expected movement: p2 and p3 adversary shards on stories with unchanged text fall from 19 on s321 to 0, removing up to $7.57 and 0.48 spawn-hours a sprint at s321's shape, while the p2 and p3 MAJORs raised from changed-text shards are unchanged.

Decision rule: the step-value ruling, "A step with no measured value goes" (`.claude/rules/operator-rulings.md`), with its high-consequence guard.

Receipt: `verify: manual`. The close is a measurement on a future sprint, so no tree receipt exists. It closes on an `Outcome measured:` line from the second measured sprint that names that sprint, the measuring script, the unchanged-text shard count, their CRITICAL or MAJOR findings, and their dollars and spawn-hours, the form BL-810 and BL-812 use. What else would satisfy a weaker receipt: a `has` on `stories-test-strategy.md` for a changed-stories filter, which a prose edit satisfies without any measurement.

## BL-1014 — a full `--matrix` re-measure of adversarial-shard-merge costs about 4.8 to 8.3 hours in one invocation and 6.8 to 10.3 hours in 11 chunks, so the per-start cost and the starts per cell are the lever

**WITHDRAWN (56877eb4d).** Its subject is gone: 0.1130.0 (56877eb4d) deleted the `--matrix` mode this entry costs. Counted with `git grep` over `core/fixtures/adversarial-shard-merge/` at `56877eb4d^` against 30a78d884: the `--matrix` case arm 1 to 0, `--mutants=` 6 to 0, `--jobs=` 6 to 0, the `mx_enqueue()` definition 1 to 0; `core/fixtures/*/run.sh` files naming `--matrix` 157 to 1, the survivor a comment at `core/fixtures/adversarial-shard-merge/run.sh:1044`. Its owed items (the PCN2 trip, a full-matrix wall clock and start count) measure a mode no fixture runs.

**PARTIAL (v0.1100.0) — `--matrix` now scores its rows in parallel, `--jobs=8` by default and `--jobs=1` for the serial matrix; the wall clock is the lever moved, and the starts and CPU are not lower.** Each row (MX0 and each mutant) runs in its own worker with its own WORK, scratch names and KE world. The workers copy the parent's cached worlds, and once every worker has answered, the rows print in the serial run's order. Measured on one tree in one window, the same 8 mutants plus MX0 against all 90 arms (810 rows), on the runner before the KE fix below: old serial 3101 s wall and 2744 s CPU (user 911, sys 1833); new at `--jobs=9` 1076 s wall and 3902 s CPU (user 1002, sys 2900). That is -65% wall and +42% CPU, almost all of the extra in sys, which is fork contention. Both runs started 8734 bash scripts, with identical per-script counts once the root and temp-dir names are normalized. The load included another session's gate running this fixture. The shipped runner adds 13 bash starts per parallel row that runs a KE arm, for the worker's own KE world (12 hook runs and 1 `partition-document.sh`, counted). The full-matrix wall time is an ESTIMATE, not a measurement: the paired 2.88× ratio applied to the derived 4.8 to 8.3 h gives about 1.7 to 2.9 h. That the estimate is conservative, because the serial base phase is a larger share of 9 rows than of 88, is reasoning, not a measurement.

Equivalence: 806 of the 810 rows were byte-identical. The other 4 tripped only in parallel. Three were KEnone_core and KEvac_core on CSW-call and KEvac_inst on KMb-inst: KE's subject world was shared by every drive, so two rows driving it at once each read the other's output as already written; KMb-inst's worker also printed a `mv` of a missing output. Each worker now builds its own (`ke_build`). On the fixed runner, a 9-worker re-run of those 9 rows against 8 arms (PCN2, PC1 and six KE arms) gave 72 of 72 cells byte-identical to the serial run's, 54 of them KE cells. The fourth, PCN2 on CM-doc-sha-unchecked, is UNEXPLAINED: a parallel-only trip in that one cell, with that row's 90 arms running in a worker beside 8 other rows under the window's load, and no stray output from its worker. It did not recur in the 9-worker re-run, in 16 workers × 6 forced concurrent PCN2 calls (96 of 96 held), or in 12 workers interleaving PCN2 with twelve other PC/DV arms (36 of 36 held). The probe's positive control, PC-scoped-map-continue's edit, tripped 3 of 3. `--jobs=1` rules the parallel runner out for any cell that trips. The search for other shared worlds covered the globals bound to a world or a temp dir and the memoized-global guards, and found only `SH_D`, which the arms only read. That search is not exhaustive. Ctrl-C does not reach the workers; stop a `--matrix` by its process group.

Still owed: the PCN2 trip explained; a measured full `--matrix` wall clock and start count (an operator instrument under BL-1022, not run here); and the per-start cost and the starts per cell, which this change does not move.

**DEFECT.** OPEN. Filed 2026-11-12. For `core/fixtures/adversarial-shard-merge`, a full `--matrix` re-measure is 87 mutants × 90 arms and costs about 4.8 to 8.3 hours in one invocation (a 701 s base phase plus 87 passes of 192 to 336 s, five passes measured), about 6.8 to 10.3 hours when run in 11 chunks. That figure is DERIVED; the full run was not timed.

Operator ruling: that burden is too much for one change, so no full or scoped matrix run is a per-change duty (`BL-1022` amends `.claude/rules/fixture-mutants.md` clause 2 to say so).

Starts: the matrix passes armed no invocation counter, so no starts figure was measured in the matrix. The starts figure comes from the counted adversarial-shard-merge runs: 3101 starts at the release tip, two identical per-start logs.

The lever is the per-start cost (about 0.32 s per start) and the number of starts per cell; the cell count itself is the matrix's coverage and is not the lever. Only adversarial-shard-merge's `--matrix` takes a mutant list (`--mutants=`); the other 78 of the 79 matrix-capable fixtures run it whole, so for them the burden is unchanged and this lever is what applies.

Receipt: `verify: manual`. The legend allows it for an OPEN entry, and no mechanical predicate exists yet: the closing receipt is a measured full `--matrix` wall clock and process-start count, taken on one tree with wall and CPU stated, that is short enough to be an operator's routine instrument, or a lower per-start cost with the same two figures beside the old ones. A receipt that greps the fixture for an option name would be satisfied by a flag that changes no start count.

Provenance: batch 256, b256-B2 (BL-911 narrowing); id allocated by the lead.

## BL-1030 — gate 1 has no zero-seat GATE1-SKIP answer after a gate-3 rework, so a records-only rework that gate 3 did not ask to re-review would still dispatch a gate-1 seat

**NOTE (efficiency).** OPEN. Filed 2026-11-13. The second subject of BL-844's title, "a gate-3 rework re-runs gates 1 and 2", split out when BL-844 closed on its gate-2 subject. A gate-1 pass M >= 2 does ask about its delta: `core/scripts/gate-transition.sh` runs `partition-review-diff.sh --route` (`:317`, a one-seat record-only route) and `--narrow` (`:326`). What gate 1 lacks is a zero-seat answer. The `--gate1-rerun` question that records GATE1-SKIP and dispatches no gate-1 seat is asked only in the Rule A block (`:393`-`:397`), when a gate-2 pass M >= 2 opens, so a gate-1 plan opened after a gate-3 rework always dispatches at least one seat.

First reading (batch 257), by `docs/analysis/bl844-gate2-route-seats/derive-g3.sh` (output `out-g3.txt` beside it) at graph `main` c8ab65397, identical at 1449b5c72 apart from the ref line. Population, derived from the `-g3-p<M>.plan` listings: every story with a gate-3 plan at pass >= 2, s321 1-4 and 2-4 and s322 1, 3, 4, 6 and 8, so 9 gate-3 reworks (s320 has no gate plans). Each gate-1 and gate-2 plan is joined to its review file by `reviewed-sha:`.

Classification of each seat, from its review file, first rule that holds: (c) a gate-3 remedy, when the review's text before its `## Verdict` heading names gate 3 and a `Check <n>` (for example "It exists because gate-3 Check 19 FAILed pass 1"); (b) a same-gate re-review, when the same gate's previous pass reads NEEDS_REWORK; (a) a re-run, otherwise. Class (a) is this entry's subject.

Order and the tie convention: by the committer time t of the commit each plan's `sha:` names (plan and review files were committed in one batch per sprint, so their own commit times order nothing). Rework M owns a class (a) or (b) plan with t(g3 p<M-1>) < t <= t(g3 p<M>), and a class (c) plan with t(g3 p<M-1>) <= t < t(g3 p<M>). A remedy runs at the very sha the failed gate-3 pass judged, with no new commit, so the tie is broken by the remedy citation, never by time. The first version of this reading used the time window alone; it dropped s322 1 g1 p2 and mis-assigned s322 3 g1 p4, and its "6 of the 9 reworks re-ran neither gate" and "12 seats" are withdrawn.

Controls in the same run: s322 1 g1 p2 (a remedy at gate-3 p1's own sha) classifies (c), and s322 3 g1 p2 (the partitioned gate-1 pass after gate-3 p1) classifies (a); a seeded remedy line reads 1 before the verdict heading and 0 after it; the impossible story `ZZNOPE-8f3a1q` joins 0 plans; `GATE1-RERUN` appears in 0 graph plans and `GATE1-SKIP` in 18 (s321 12, s322 6), and the matcher reads a seeded `gate: rerun` / `route: rerun GATE1-RERUN:` plan, the form `core/fixtures/gate-transition/run.sh` asserts.

| Gate-3 rework | Seats that follow (class, non-record paths since the same gate's previous pass) | (a) | (b) | (c) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| s321 1-4 p2, s321 2-4 p2, s322 6 p2 | none | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| s322 1 p2 | g1 p2 record-only (c, 0) | 0 | 0 | 1 |
| s322 1 p3 | g1 p3 serial, 0 reviewable files (c, 0) | 0 | 0 | 1 |
| s322 3 p2 | g1 p2 partitioned, 5 seats (a, 105); g1 p3 serial (b, 1); g2 p2 serial, records GATE1-SKIP (a, 1) | 6 | 1 | 0 |
| s322 3 p3 | g1 p4 serial (c, 0) | 0 | 0 | 1 |
| s322 4 p2 | g1 p2 serial (c, 1); g1 p3 serial (b, 3); g2 p3 serial (a, 4); g2 p4 record-only (b, 0) | 1 | 2 | 1 |
| s322 8 p2 | g1 p4 serial (c, 1) | 0 | 0 | 1 |

Totals: 15 seats after the 9 reworks, (a) 7, (b) 3, (c) 5; 3 reworks re-ran neither gate. Every class (a) seat reviewed a delta carrying code or test paths since the same gate's previous pass: s322 3 g1 p2 105 paths, s322 4 g2 p3 4 (`meta-gate.sh`, its test `test-s239-2-check26-discrimination.sh`, and two extension docs, `gate-validation-domain.md` and `deploy-validate-domain.md`), and s322 3 g2 p2 1 path, `server/test_live_smoke.py`, whose diff (`git diff c6ebe5c96d 9c572624c0`) changes executable test lines (`wait_until` and a `wait_for` call), not only docstrings. So 0 class (a) seats ran over a records-only delta. Every records-only gate-1 seat after a rework (s322 1 g1 p2 and p3, s322 3 g1 p4) was a class (c) remedy gate 3 asked for: a skip keyed on the delta alone would have removed all 3 and no other seat.

Verdict on the subject: NOT discharged as a mechanism, and no cost measured. Gate 1 still has no zero-seat answer after a gate-3 rework, but s321 and s322 hold no discriminating case: 0 class (a) seats over a records-only delta. That zero is a missing input, not an acquittal.

Owed: after a gate-3 rework, gate 1 is opened through the same `--gate1-rerun` answer gate 2 already uses, so a records-only delta records GATE1-SKIP and dispatches no gate-1 seat, EXCEPT a gate-1 re-review that remedies a gate-3 finding against the gate-1 review record (class (c)), which always runs; and gate 2 re-validates through the record-only route when every open finding is `Class: EVIDENCE|BOOKKEEPING`.

Baseline: s320
Step: implementation
Expected movement: class (a) seats after a gate-3 rework over a records-only delta (classes (b) and (c) excluded) stay 0, recorded as GATE1-SKIP, on the next measured sprint with a gate-3 rework, by the same script. On s321 and s322 that population was 0 seats, so no fall is measured or claimed; the (a) seats over code deltas (7) are out of it.

Step choice: the seats this entry concerns are gate-1 and gate-2 dispatches, which `core/skills/ai-dlc/steps/implementation.md` makes through `gate-transition.sh` (9 citations there), so they are charged to `implementation`, the step BL-844 and BL-809 declare. The Baseline is s320, BL-844's before sprint. s320 has no gate plans, so this join cannot read it; the first readings are s321 and s322 above.

Decision rule: the step-value ruling, "A step with no measured value goes" (`.claude/rules/operator-rulings.md`).

Receipt: `verify: manual`, the form BL-1004 and BL-1005 use. The close is a measurement on a future sprint, so no tree receipt exists. It closes on an `Outcome measured:` line from a graph sprint with a gate-3 rework that names that sprint, `derive-g3.sh`, the class (a), (b) and (c) seats per gate-3 rework with their dollars and hours, and the GATE1-SKIP records. The expected owner of the fix is `gate-transition.sh`'s Rule A block at `:393`-`:397`, which asks `--gate1-rerun` only on a gate-2 pass M >= 2. A tree receipt for the mechanism, once built, is a `core/fixtures/gate-transition` cell asserting GATE1-SKIP on a gate-1 open after a gate-3 rework with a records-only delta, and a seat on a class (c) remedy; that is new test code and is not written here. What else would satisfy a weaker receipt: a `has` on `implementation.md` for a gate-3 rework sentence, which a prose edit satisfies without any behaviour.

Provenance: batch 257, release cluster F3, from BL-844's close verifier; amended in the same release on the delta adversary's tie finding; id reserved by the lead.

**PARTIAL (v0.1137.0) — the gate-1 mechanism shipped; the gate-2 record-only route after a gate-3 rework, the step prose and the Outcome measured: line are OPEN.** `core/scripts/gate-transition.sh` gate 1, pass M >= 2, now runs `g1_after_g3` before `--route`: when the story's newest `<idx>-g3-p<K>.plan` names a sha the pass M-1 reviewed-sha is an ancestor of or equal to, and the frozen sha is not that g3 sha (class (c), the remedy gate 3 asked for, always runs), and the pass M-1 review reads APPROVED through `merge-review-shards.sh --read-verdict` (class (b) always runs), it asks `partition-review-diff.sh --gate1-rerun` from that reviewed-sha. `GATE1-SKIP` writes a `gate: skip` plan, `manifest: none`, `route: skip GATE1-SKIP: ...`, no shard line, a `rule-a-base: gate1` line and a `next:` gate-2 command; the dispatch guard's ROLE TO GATE default arm already denies every seat on a `skip` plan. Any other answer is a `gate-log: Rule A at gate 1:` line and the pass goes on as before. Tree receipt: `core/fixtures/gate-transition` arms G3K (a records-only delta after gate 3 judged G1: `gate: skip`) and its control G3C (the same world with gate 3 having judged the frozen sha: `gate: 1`, the remedy line). Hand-run once: the base `gate-transition.sh` fails G3K (it dispatches a serial seat), and a mutant without the class (c) branch fails G3C (it writes `gate: skip` over the remedy). Remainder: (1) gate 2 after a gate-3 rework still reads `--route` from the QA pass M-1 file, whose findings are QA's, not gate 3's, so a records-only gate-3 rework still dispatches a full QA fan-out; keying the record-only route on gate 3's open findings needs a class field the adjudicator verdict does not carry. (2) `core/skills/ai-dlc/steps/implementation.md` names no `gate: skip` plan; filed as BL-1131, its owner. (3) The Outcome measured: line this entry's Receipt names.

**Progress (batch 257, W12-13; the entry stays PARTIAL, `verify: manual` unchanged, no LANDED line).** Remainder (1), gate 2's record-only route keyed on gate 3's open findings, is not built in this release, and it stays OPEN as a DEFECT: the full QA fan-out it removes is reachable by mechanism and unobserved on record. Reachable: at gate 2 pass M >= 2 after a gate-3 rework, QA pass M-1 is APPROVED with no Critical or Important finding, so `--route` answers FULL, and `gate-transition.sh:480` then calls `map "$SD" "$BASE"`, whose `partition-review-diff.sh --map` call (`gate-transition.sh:266`) partitions the WHOLE story range from the story's two-dot base, not the rework delta. The partition threshold defaults to 8 reviewable files (`partition-review-diff.sh:101`), so a records-only gate-3 rework on a story with 8 or more reviewable files reaches a full QA fan-out. Unobserved: `docs/analysis/bl844-gate2-route-seats/out-g3.txt` (derive-g3.sh at graph c8ab65397, s321 and s322, 9 gate-3 reworks) holds exactly 3 gate-2 passes after a gate-3 rework, each one seat, all over small stories: s322 3 g2 p2 `serial(4_reviewable)` over 1 non-record path (`out-g3.txt:25`), s322 4 g2 p3 `serial(5_reviewable)` over 4 (`:32`), and s322 4 g2 p4 already record-only over 0 (`:33`; story 4's review files carry the `1-4` index form). The file's own total reads 0 class (a) seats over a records-only delta (`:42`). Control in the same file: 8 gate-1 plan lines (`:18`, `:20`, `:23`, `:24`, `:27`, `:30`, `:31`, `:39`), and 3 reworks followed by no gate-1 or gate-2 plan (`:40`). So the zero is a missing input, not an acquittal: no records-only rework on a story of 8 or more reviewable files occurs in s321 or s322. A false-positive census of the proposed route would also be vacuous today: the verdict items in `core/schemas/gate-adjudication-verdict.json` are `check_id`, `verdict`, `waiver_id` and `evidence`, with no class, so no verdict written to that schema could route. The build it needs: an optional `class` item field in that schema, joined to I129's finding-class vocabulary; a `--classes` reader of a verdict's FAIL rows; a `partition-review-diff.sh` mode sharing `--route`'s class loop and delta guard from the g3 sha; and a `g2_after_g3` step in `core/scripts/gate-transition.sh` mirroring `g1_after_g3`. Remainder (2) is closed: BL-1131 reads `**LANDED (v0.1139.0).**` in `docs/backlog.archive.md`. Remainder (3), the `Outcome measured:` line, still waits on a graph sprint with a gate-3 rework.
