## BL-1074 — `backlog-receipt-binding` reads red under concurrency: its re-entry control and one mutant flip when five other fixtures run beside it

**WITHDRAWN (56877eb4d).** Its subject is gone: `core/fixtures/backlog-receipt-binding` was deleted in 0.1130.0 (56877eb4d). `git ls-tree -d 9ee073be0 core/fixtures/` lists it 0 times and the sibling `owed-derivations` once; at `56877eb4d^` it lists it once.

**DEFECT.** OPEN. Filed 2026-11-14. Owner: `core/fixtures/backlog-receipt-binding/` (G1, ai-dlc-98, holds its rework).

Measured instance: on e4e961799 (base 4c3a82362), run as the hook runs it (`AI_DLC_FIXTURE_JOBS=9 bash core/fixtures/backlog-receipt-binding/run.sh </dev/null` from the repository root) as one of six concurrent hook-driving fixtures, it exited 1 with `FAIL  c5r-open-control -- an OPEN receipt re-entering the validator through pre-push exited rc=2, row '', receipt runs 2; it must exit 0` and `FAIL  c5-m4-open SURVIVED: its deciding arm c5_open did not trip -- rc=0`. Run solo, the same command exits 0 at 4c3a82362 and at e4e961799 (`PASS  backlog-receipt-binding -- control green, every mutant caught by its own assertion`). e4e961799 changes nothing the fixture reads: its `inputs.decl` names `scripts/validate-backlog-receipts.sh`, `scripts/backlog-reverify.sh` and `scripts/backlog-receipt-lib.sh`, and the c5r probe hook (`run.sh` `c5r_seed`) runs only `scripts/validate-backlog-receipts.sh` on a seeded repository. A verdict that moves with load breaks the operator ruling "Zero tests depend on load (BL-868)".

Owed: the red run shared one working tree with five concurrent fixtures, so it does not separate CPU load from interference through shared state; the cause on the c5r re-entry path (rc 2 is a refusal) is unmeasured. Find it and fix it in the test, so the fixture reads the same verdict solo and under the pool's width.

Receipt: `verify: manual`. Reproducing needs a concurrent load, which a receipt must not generate (`.claude/rules/tool-hazards.md`, "Never brief a load generator"); the closing evidence is the fix's named condition wait, with the fixture green at the pool's width in the push that carries it.

**Progress (batch 257, G1; the entry stays OPEN, `verify: manual` unchanged, no LANDED line).** The red has two halves, and both were blind: the receipt keeps its inner (re-entering) validator run's output only in `o`, and `run.sh`'s red-path filter hid the validator's own `validate-backlog-receipts: FAIL --` refusals.

- c5-m4-open SURVIVED (rc 0, 2 receipt runs, no BL-865 row) is the shape of an inner run that never reached BL-865's receipt. Forced in a scratch copy of the validator by failing `mktree`'s worktree add only inside the inner run, it printed exactly the red's three lines (PROBE-RECORD REPLAYED, R12-POPULATION, PROSE-CLOSABLE BL-801), with BL-865 classified BOUND. Any refusal of the inner run before scoring gives the same output, so the forced run pins the shape, not the cause.
- c5r-open-control rc 2 with row '' and 2 receipt runs is not the outer `mktree`: forcing the outer base worktree to fail gives rc 2 with `FAIL: BROKEN` rows and 0 runs, and forcing the re-read worktree stays green. Its shape matches `scripts/validate-backlog-receipts.sh` "N of M dispatched receipts produced a verdict" (exit 2 before any row prints): a worker exited without writing its verdict. Ruled out: the inner run clobbering the outer's WORK (its WORK is its own `mktemp -d`). Not ruled out: a process-count spike; the per-user limit is 10666 and 676 were in use when checked, long after the red, which says nothing about the count during it.
- `mktree`'s retry (5 tries, `sleep 1`, then BROKEN) is a latent bounded wait, but it is NOT shown to cause this red: 24 concurrent `git worktree add --detach` on one repository failed 0 times, and 24 with the same basename into the same common dir (what the outer and inner runs do) failed 0 times (git numbers the admin dirs `1.a`, `1.a1`, ...). Do not build a worktree-add lock on this evidence.

Instrumented in this release: the receipt appends its inner run's exit and output to `<C5R_LOG>.inner`, and when c5r-control, c5r-open-control or c5-m4-open goes red, `c5r_outer` prints the outer run's output unfiltered (40 lines; a worker that dies on a plain `bash:` error leaves only that line, which a FAIL/refusal filter drops) and `c5r_inner` prints the inner capture. Owed: read the next red's capture and fix the cause it names.

Provenance: batch 257, G3's hook-fixture run before READY; id allocated by the lead.

verify: manual

## BL-1068 — the sandbox read-set tracer cannot trace the fixtures that rerun on almost every push, so F19 cannot narrow them

**PARTIAL (batch 257, wave 5) — measurement only; no code changed. lcc-b at push load still omits, and its own volume is the leading cause (unproven, BL-584). The owed complete trace is still open, so this entry carries no `**LANDED` line.** Traced across B11's push with `--list "layer-contract-conformance-b" --tracer sandbox` in the main checkout at d594c01f2. The emitter was clamped (`rs_qos`), the run took 24 s, and 1-minute load ran 25.6-28.2. The fixture was OMITTED with 52 drop notices. A box-wide `sender == "Sandbox"` stream ran over the same window, with seeded controls live at start and stop; BL-584 has its figures. Every drop bucket carried 2,727-3,825 of lcc-b's own reports per 100 ms, against 0 outside Sandbox reports. The clean wave-3 run peaked at 3,483 per 100 ms, so own rate alone does not decide whether a window drops. The lever is the one named below: the validator's 260 grep passes re-statting the same files (`core/scripts/validate-layer-entries.sh`, `core/skills/ai-dlc-update/reconcile/layer-drift.sh`). Neither file is in this entry's Owner, and the deriver has no measured lever left.

**PARTIAL (batch 257, wave 3) — measurement only; no code changed. The owed lcc-b trace at push load is still open, so this entry carries no `**LANDED` line.** Traced with `--list --tracer sandbox` in the main checkout at 30a78d884 (clamped plain emitter, 92 s, 1-minute load 19 at start and 28 at end). layer-contract-conformance-b traced complete: 1927 paths, 0 drop notices, 0 canary paths. ledger-status-vocabulary also traced complete: 2367 paths, 0/0. fixture-git-env-seam was omitted, with 61 notices and a 2-path canary. lcc-b had been omitted at load ~9 and at 40-106, so load alone does not predict the outcome.
- Report composition, counted from the kept windows (`$WORK/<fx>.win`). Each fixture's `.git` share is followed by its total reports and its main process:
  - fixture-git-env-seam: 257 of 114,221; bsdtar 107,071 (2 pids).
  - layer-contract-conformance-b: 0 of 78,986; grep 71,504 across 260 pids, the top ones 4,408 reports each.
  - ledger-status-vocabulary: 47 of 50,694; bsdtar 50,567 (1 pid).
  The 257 comes from the same match in the same run, so the 0 is a real zero.
- Dropped cut: stop reporting `$TREE/.git` (a trailing unreported clause; a deny-rewrite probe confirmed `.git/HEAD` goes unreported while `.gitignore` and a nested `d/.git/x` stay reported). It is map-neutral (`readset_drop_git` at `:2122` and the canary at `:2107`), but it removes at most 0.2% of the volume, so it has no measured value. Most of the volume is file-test-existence and file-read-metadata, the stat channel the map keys on, so the reported op set is no lever either.
- All 61 of env-seam's notices fall inside its own two sequential bsdtar bursts; it is filed as BL-1117. Peak rates, in reports per 100 ms, were 3,839 (4 notices) and 5,192 (57 notices) for env-seam's two tars, 2,551 for ledger's single tar (clean), and 3,483 for lcc-b's greps (clean). A peak near 3,500 per 100 ms sits on both sides of loss, so peak rate alone does not decide a window either.
- Owed: unchanged, a complete trace of layer-contract-conformance-b at the load a push runs at. Its remaining volume lever is the validator's 260 grep passes, re-statting the same files (`core/scripts/validate-layer-entries.sh` 1,016 reports, `core/skills/ai-dlc-update/reconcile/layer-drift.sh` 816). Neither file is in this entry's Owner.

**PARTIAL (v0.1132.0) — at moderate load the two owed fixtures trace completely; at the load a push runs at, layer-contract-conformance-b and layer-contract-conformance still omit, so this entry carries no `**LANDED` line.** Cause: a full-priority sweep out-competes logd for CPU, so its own reports are lost upstream of the stream. Fix: the plain emitter's fixture launch (`--list`, `--all`, `--reconcile`) runs under `taskpolicy -c background` (`rs_qos_decide`/`rs_qos` in `core/scripts/derive-fixture-readsets.sh`); every child inherits the clamp. `--local-map`, `--in-pool` and `both` stay unclamped, because a clamped run on a loaded box stretches its window, which holds the per-uid slot that long. `core/fixtures/readset-sandbox-root-clause` binds both cells: nq (the plain launch goes through `taskpolicy -c background sandbox-exec`) and nl (a `--local-map` launch reaches sandbox-exec unclamped). Each is driven from the deriver's own functions, with recorder stubs. Proved once by hand at authoring: without `rs_qos` on the nest launch, nq FAILs and nl holds; without the `-z "$LOCAL_MAP"` test, nl FAILs and nq holds.
- Measured with `--list --tracer sandbox` in standalone clones (drop notices / canary paths, unclamped then clamped). layer-contract-conformance-b: 175/0 then 0/0 at load ~15 (1849 paths, 160 s), and 249/0 then 6/0 at load 50, with the clamped run spanning load 54-135. ledger-status-vocabulary: 129/798 at load 50, then 0/0 twice (2299 and 2302 paths) across load 54-135. On a 3x-oversubscribed box the clamp does not suffice: layer-contract-conformance-b at load 64-106 ran over 25 min and dropped 74 (omitted), and hermetic-runner (a peak of 116 own reports/s) dropped 67 over a 41-min window, against 0 unclamped in 84 s at load 50. Those drops are other senders' volume, not the window's own; a 10 s box-wide `sender == "Sandbox"` sample at load 61 read 4 messages, so the burst source was not caught. Every such window is omitted (fail-closed). Trace at moderate load.
- Narrowed: 1 fixture, layer-contract-conformance-b. Its accepted trace (c75a7c323 plus this deriver, load ~15) opens nothing under `templates/`, `patterns/` or `.claude/rules/`, and those 3 lines are removed (`.githooks/` was already dropped by BL-1098); 5 directory lines become 2. `scripts/` stays, because the trace stats the directory (stat-only rows stay wide, by ruling), and `core/` stays, because the trace lists `core/fixtures`. In `docs/inputs-decl-whole-rows.tsv`, 3 rows are deleted and `core/` is reclassed `whole`. Two-tree mutation at the tip (9ee073be0 plus this unit): a copy with all 21 tracked files under the 3 removed paths overwritten, plus 1 new file in each (24 paths differ), gave byte-identical output to the unmutated copy (rc 0 both). A third copy with the still-declared `scripts/render-invariant-index.sh` broken gave rc 1 (vem-cli-twin FAIL), so the fixture does see a declared input move. ledger-status-vocabulary stays wide (it archives every tracked file; ruling).
- Traced in the main checkout at 13bb5e8c8 (`--list --tracer sandbox`, load ~9): layer-contract-conformance 3175 paths, consumer-machinery-home 2236, crosswalk-home-declaration 2228 and hermetic-consumption 1021 accepted. layer-contract-conformance-b (38 drop notices), ledger-status-vocabulary (111, a 200-path canary) and fixture-git-env-seam (100, a 3-path canary) were omitted. `.ai-dlc-fixture-readsets.tsv` maps only fixtures with no `inputs.decl` (2 before and after), so a declared fixture's trace adds no map row; there is no map commit to make. The accepted layer-contract-conformance trace reads every tracked file under `templates/` (5 of 5), `patterns/` (5 of 5), `.claude/rules/` (11 of 11) and `.githooks/` (1 of 1), so its directory lines stay.
- Owed: a complete trace of layer-contract-conformance-b at the load a push runs at (omitted at load 40-106 and again at load ~9).

**DEFECT.** OPEN. Filed 2026-11-14. Owner: `core/scripts/derive-fixture-readsets.sh`, the `--tracer sandbox` profile emitter and `sandboxed()`.

Two groups were never mapped under `--list --tracer sandbox` (F19's s4 run at 6d3248518):
(a) whole-hook drivers exited nonzero: hermetic-runner, prepush-pool-depth, consumer-suite-pool, readset-skip, readset-skip-b, readset-skip-c, census-hermetic-coverage, presuite-present-key; readset-skip-d was omitted on a loss canary.
(b) report-volume fixtures dropped reports: layer-contract-conformance 612 drop notices, layer-contract-conformance-b 517, consumer-machinery-home 313, crosswalk-home-declaration 266, ledger-status-vocabulary 115.

Causes, each probed under `sandbox-exec` in a scratch directory:
- Inside any sandbox the kernel refuses the exec of a setuid binary (`/bin/ps`, rc 71), and `log` refuses to run ("Cannot run while sandboxed"). Group (a) reads process state and opens streams.
- `sandbox_apply` is refused for a nested `sandbox-exec` when the outer profile carries `(with report)`; prepush-pool-depth nests one.
- hermetic-runner's rc 127 was F19's setup, which `git rm`ed its `inputs.decl`; its DC arm builds a copy from that file.
- Group (b) bursts reach about 30k reports a second from `grep -r`, `cp -R` and `tar` sweeps. Two `log stream` clients with disjoint predicates dropped as much as one (155 and 176 notices for one stream, 310 and 334 for two), so the loss is upstream of the stream client.

**PARTIAL (v0.1127.0) — groups (a) and three of (b) trace completely; layer-contract-conformance-b and ledger-status-vocabulary stayed OPEN.** The plain emitter (`--list`, `--all`, `--reconcile`, `both`) execs `/bin/ps`, `/usr/bin/log` and `/usr/bin/sandbox-exec` `(with no-sandbox)`, each exec reported. A nest shim first on the traced fixture's PATH inserts the report clause after the caller profile's `(version N)` form and chains to the next `sandbox-exec` on the deriver's PATH, so a nested run's reads are still reported. A window holding an escaped `sandbox-exec` exec whose pid did not first read the shim's marker is omitted as NEST BYPASS. The loss canary compares `.git/**` out of both sides; `readset_drop_git` removes it from every set, and a straggler's `.git/HEAD` read omitted readset-skip and readset-skip-d on every trace. The fixture launch sets `READSET_POOL_WIDTH=1`, which layer-contract-conformance, consumer-machinery-home and crosswalk-home-declaration read as their pool width. `--local-map` and `--in-pool` are unchanged.

Measured by one solo `--list` pass over all 14 fixtures at 933c7a520: 11 traced with 0 drop notices, 0 canary paths and 0 bypasses (all of group (a) except readset-skip-b, plus the three volume fixtures); readset-skip-b was omitted once on its forced-timing arm t4w and passes untraced at the same tree. The tracer then changed once more (the shim writes its composed profile beside itself, not under the marker directory); cells nm and nc below cover that change. Verdict lines of 5 fixtures traced at 23e5f7d (consumer-suite-pool, presuite-present-key, census-hermetic-coverage, hermetic-runner, readset-skip-d) matched their plain runs line for line. A live-stream probe of the shim and guard: bypass reads 0 through the shim and 1 for an absolute `/usr/bin/sandbox-exec`; with the shim's marker read removed it reads 2. `core/fixtures/readset-sandbox-root-clause` binds both at the gate without a stream: cells nb (the guard over two seeded windows reads 0 paired, 1 unpaired), nm (under its deny channel the shim's marker read is denied before it execs the next sandbox-exec) and nc (the composed profile is the caller's `(version N)`, the inserted clause, then the caller's rules), with mutants nest-guard, nest-nomark and nest-noins each RED on its own cell alone.

No declaration narrows under F19's rules. Group (a) declarations are already per-file plus the hooks and deriver they read. layer-contract-conformance, consumer-machinery-home and crosswalk-home-declaration list `core/` and `scripts/` (file-read-data on the directory), so their directory lines stay. ledger-status-vocabulary archives every tracked file.

Owed at that release: layer-contract-conformance-b (a sequential `grep` sweep) and ledger-status-vocabulary (one `tar` of the tree) still dropped reports at width 1: 154 and 135 notices. A complete trace needs fewer reports per sweep, not a narrower pool.

Receipt: `verify: manual`. Completeness is the deriver's own drop count and loss canary over a live `log stream`, which no predicate over the tree can test.

Provenance: batch 257, H3 (ai-dlc-9e), from F19's (ai-dlc-de) s4 traces; id allocated by the lead.

## BL-1085 — READY acceptance is checked by reading prose; no script verifies the pregate record or the member's predicted set

**WITHDRAWN (0eefdee5b).** Its subject is gone. `CLAUDE.md`'s "Build process" replaced the READY counted-seed and integrator acceptance step this entry would check, and `.githooks/pre-push`'s PREGATE_GATE span already refuses a tree with no pregate record.

**DEFECT.** OPEN. Filed 2026-11-14. Owner: the READY/bundle acceptance flow (`CLAUDE.md`'s "Before READY" sentence, `scripts/pregate-self-check.sh`, `scripts/predict-push-set.sh`).

`CLAUDE.md` now requires a release's final commit to carry `scripts/pregate-self-check.sh` green on its exact tree (`--member` when it edits `.githooks/pre-push`) and a counted `scripts/seed-verdict-store.sh` run of exactly the fixtures `scripts/predict-push-set.sh` says its own change moves; the integrator seeds nothing. The integrator accepts a READY by reading the owner's prose. Nothing checks that the pregate record exists for the tree, or that the member's predicted set was seeded, so a READY with a missing record or an unseeded moved fixture is accepted and the push gate pays for it.

Fix: a script the integrator runs per READY sha that (1) checks `<common dir>/ai-dlc-pregate-green/<tree>` exists for the sha's tree (member mode writes none, so for a release that edits `.githooks/pre-push` it checks the member-mode rc stated in READY instead), and (2) runs `scripts/predict-push-set.sh` on the member tip and requires that it names none of that member's own fixtures as still to run. Until it lands the carrier is ai-dlc-b5's acceptance check (the lead imposes it from B5 on).

Receipt: a runner-wired fixture builds a scratch repo with two READY shas, one carrying its `ai-dlc-pregate-green/<tree>` record and a seeded predicted set, one whose record is absent; the script must accept the first and refuse the second. Its mutant deletes the record-existence check (or has the predicted-set check read an empty set), and the second sha must then be accepted, failing the fixture. A third arm seeds a hook-editing release with no record and a stated member-mode rc and must accept it, so the member branch is held too.

Provenance: batch 257, ruled by lead ai-dlc-a9 on the operator's approval; id allocated by lead ai-dlc-a9.

verify: manual


## BL-1124 — the push suite is throughput-bound, not pole-bound, and nothing that reads the push says so

**DEFECT.** OPEN. Filed 2026-11-15. Owner: `.githooks/pre-push` and `core/git-hooks/pre-push` (`pole_info_step`, `.githooks/pre-push:4060`; the `POLE-BOUND` header at `:4050` and the dispatch-order header at `:3184`), `CLAUDE.md` (the "POLE-BOUND" sentence in the fixture-suite section), and `fixture_suite_step`'s work outside the pool.

B9 took 1084 s and B10's first push 1159 s, against a 15-minute target. Both pushes ran `── fixture suite (6-way)`, so the hook's default of 12 was not in force. The fixture step took 960 s and 1073 s, and the pole was 145 s and 147 s. These figures come from the gate logs and from a copy of `.git/ai-dlc-fixture-durations` taken at 08:59:23, after B10's re-push. B9 was green, so its units hold clean rows. Four of those rows are B10's re-push timings rather than B9's, and they total 32 s. B10 was red, so its units hold `@red` rows.

| push | units (dist + `@consumer`) | summed loaded cost | sum / 6 | fixture step | residual |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B9 | 124 (68 + 56) | 5289 s | 882 s | 960 s | 78 s |
| B10 | 166 (103 + 63) | 5992 s | 999 s | 1073 s | 74 s |

1. **The pool makespan is sum ÷ width.** B10's dispatch order is in its retained failure log, `ai-dlc-fixture-failures.20261115T134342Z.5180`. Simulating a 6-worker list schedule in that order over the recorded costs gives 999 s. A longest-first schedule on the actual costs also gives 999 s, so no dispatch order recovers anything. `.dur` is the worker shell's `$SECONDS` (`:3430`), which covers the whole unit. The pole is 15% of the makespan. `CLAUDE.md`'s "The suite is POLE-BOUND" and the hook headers at `:3184` and `:4050` are false for selections of this size, at width 12 as well (5992 ÷ 12 ≈ 500 s against 147 s). `pole_info_step` prints only the pole, so a reader of the push sees the wrong bound.
2. **About 75 s of each fixture step lies outside the pool, and that cost is fixed.** B9 had 78 s at 124 units and B10 had 74 s at 166, so the residual does not grow with unit count. `$SECONDS` reads whole seconds, so each unit's figure is off by less than 1 s in either direction, and that error cannot add up to a fixed residual. The rest is the work before and after the pool (the consumer-layout `install.sh` build, the read-set key selection, the trace report and the record merges), which no record times.
3. **Composition of the summed cost:**
   - `@consumer` twins: 42% (B9) and 34% (B10).
   - The `review-shard-merge` and `adversarial-shard-merge` families: 33% and 29%. Their 18 units in each push cost 37 to 147 s each.
   - Units rerun only on a legacy directory line (the log's `unattributed` rows, 15 and 13 fixtures): 10% and 7%.
4. **The steps before the suite run concurrently.** Their serial sums are 220 s (B9) and 172 s (B10). At most 120 s and 83 s of wall clock remain for them, and the longest step was backlog receipts, at 97 s and 68 s.

At width 12 the suite remains throughput-bound. Loaded unit costs rise with width, and the hook's own sweep could not separate 12 from 16, so this entry predicts no figure for width 12. The next push run at 12 (the operator's batch-257 ruling) is the measurement. No hand timing run is owed.

Owed:
- (a) `pole_info_step` prints the summed loaded cost ÷ width beside the pole and names whichever bound is larger, from `LASTRUN_OWN` in the same awk. This needs no new fork.
- (b) The two hook headers state the bound the push's own figures show. The `CLAUDE.md` sentence was corrected in this unit, at -1 byte, on the operator's word.
- (c) Time the work `fixture_suite_step` does outside the pool, then cut it.
- (d) Trace the `unattributed` fixtures so that legacy directory lines stop dispatching them.
- (e) Weigh the `@consumer` twin set and the shard-merge lettered units against what each catches (operator ruling "A step with no measured value goes").

Each lettered item expires separately.

Provenance: batch 257, wave 4 (ai-dlc-33), from the B9 and B10 gate logs and the durations record; id allocated by the lead.

**PARTIAL (v0.1136.0) — (a) and (b) shipped; (c), (d) and (e) stay owed.** `pole_info_step` (`.githooks/pre-push`) now prints a second line under the pole: `throughput <sum>s summed over <n> unit(s) / <w> worker(s) = <t>s`, followed by `THROUGHPUT-BOUND` when that figure exceeds the pole and `POLE-BOUND` otherwise. It reads `LASTRUN_OWN` in the same awk, so it adds no fork. The width is `FIXTURE_JOBS`, cut to the number of units timed. A tie names the pole, and a width that is not a count names no bound. `core/fixtures/suite-pole-info` arms 10 to 12 cover these cases, and arms 1 and 9 cover the width cut. The consumer hook has no pole step, so it is unchanged. The step label now names both bounds. For (b), the two `.githooks/pre-push` headers are rewritten. The pole step's header (`3a.`) now names both lower bounds and says that a push dispatching most of the suite is width-bound, with B9's and B10's figures. The dispatch-order header in the pool block now says that the order moves the makespan only while the pole is the larger bound, and that it wins nothing measurable here because the push is width-bound. Both edits are comment-only, so the I66 comparison, which strips comments, does not see them.

**PARTIAL (v0.1140.0) — (c)'s instrument shipped; the cut, (d) and (e) stay owed.**

Width 12, re-derived on r1135 (0.1135.0's first push, 12-way, 156 units, every unit's cost from its `@red` row). The summed loaded cost is 8355 s, and 8355 ÷ 12 = 696 s. The pole is 219 s (`adversarial-shard-merge-d@consumer`), the fixture step 779 s, and the residual 83 s. So the suite is throughput-bound at width 12 as well. On r1135b (green, 5 units summing 36 s, pole 15 s), the step was 82 s, so at least 67 s ran outside the pool.

(c): `run_fixtures` (both hooks, identical lines) now prints `..    suite phases: setup <n>s select <n>s trace-report <n>s guard <n>s order <n>s pool <n>s verdicts <n>s records <n>s after <n>s`. It prints only on the distribution hook's real entry (depth 0 and `PREPUSH_CL_ENTRY`), so no consumer push, nested hook or fixture-sourced span prints a clock. `fixture_suite_step` (`.githooks/pre-push`) prints `..    suite step: content-key <n>s consumer-layout <n>s run_fixtures <n>s content-key-after <n>s`, on a red run as well. Both read the shell's `$SECONDS` and add no fork. The push that delivers this release is the measurement; the cut is owed against it.

(d): `--list "<8>" --tracer sandbox` ran in the main checkout at 27fded102 (rc 0). It traced vocabulary-index (268 paths), hermetic-consumption (1025), finding-grammar-binding (114), procsub-staged-refusal (173), validator-path-resolution (92) and bmad-invocation-resolve (68). install-scaffold-ignored and schema-install-fallback were OMITTED on 24 and 23 stream drops. The deriver drops a declared fixture's rows from the map, so no read set was kept. Still owed: a trace that keeps each declared fixture's paths, the two OMITTED fixtures, and the `inputs.decl` narrowing, which belongs to each fixture's owner.

(e): the source is 35 retained push logs (`/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-n8-git-ai-dlc/*/scratchpad/*push*.log`).
- `@consumer`: 823 unit runs, and one catch event, pw2 (`ai-dlc-fixture-failures.20261115T000445Z.28480`). 20 `@consumer` units went red on `declared file absent: tests/fixtures/<sibling>/.dist-only` and its `run.sh`: a shipping shard driver declared a dist-only sibling's files, which an installed tree lacks. 13 of the 20 had their distribution twin green in the same push, so no distribution unit saw that defect class.
- Distribution shard-merge units: 79 runs and 1 FAIL (review-shard-merge-b, f2gate).
- On r1135 the twins cost 3620 s summed (43%), about 302 s of wall clock per push at width 12. Shard-merge cost 2427 s (29%), about 202 s.
- No dollar figure: the box's compute has no recorded rate.
- The twins are not a step with no measured value. A static join would make pw2's catch without running them: a shipping fixture's declarations name no `.dist-only` fixture's files. Owed: that join, then the twin set weighed again against what remains.

**PARTIAL (v0.1142.0) — (d)'s deriver fix and 6 of its 8 fixtures shipped; the install pair, (c)'s cut and (e) stay owed.**

(d):
- The deriver fix. `derive-fixture-readsets.sh --declared-out <file>` keeps a declared fixture's accepted set as `<fx>\t<path>` and an omitted one as `<fx>\t#omitted\t<why>`. It writes per fixture by temp + mv and exits before the map is written. It takes `--list` only, and it refuses an undeclared name before any trace. Without the flag, the deriver is unchanged.
- The trace. `--list "<the 8>" --tracer sandbox --declared-out` ran in the main checkout at b53b3d642: rc 0, 155 s. It kept 6 of the 8: vocabulary-index 270 paths, hermetic-consumption 1026, finding-grammar-binding 114, procsub-staged-refusal 173, validator-path-resolution 92, bmad-invocation-resolve 69.
- Narrowed. hermetic-consumption's `core/fixtures/` line (1615 tracked files) is now `core/fixtures/*/run.sh`, `core/fixtures/*/.dist-only` and three `lib/` files, beside the existing `*/inputs.decl` glob. Under `hermetic-run.sh` the old and new declarations give the same assertion lines, arm C reads `scanned=196` both times, and `required_missing=0`. Arm C (`scripts/validate-hermetic-consumption.sh:236`) skips a directory holding none of the globbed names.

  - vocabulary-index `scripts/`, `.githooks/`, `templates/`

- Legacy rows: 96 → 84. `scripts/invalidation-guard.sh --ratchet` is rc 0.

- Still owed:
  - install-scaffold-ignored (`core/`, `patterns/`, `templates/`) and schema-install-fallback (`core/`, `templates/`). Both OMITTED again on stream drops, solo as well (6 and 9 drops).
  - procsub-staged-refusal `core/skills/` (39 of 88 files read) and vocabulary-index `core/` (167 of 1841) stay `legacy`. Their narrowing needs subdirectory rows, which the shrink-only list refuses.

**PARTIAL (v0.1145.0) — (c)'s first cut shipped; the rest of (c) stays owed. (d)'s remainder is BL-1134 and (e)'s is BL-1133.**

(c): the instrument read `select` at 57 to 77 s on r1140 to r1143 (width 12, 4 to 51 units), the largest phase outside the pool. On r1142 that was 57 of `run_fixtures`' 78 s. Read from the code, the largest per-line fork source found was in `readset_decl_parse` (READSET_KEYROWS span, both hooks). `rec_import`, `readset_local_validate`, `readset_decl_tool_hashes` and `readset_decl_keys` were not counted. For each `tools.decl` line, it resolved the tool in a `$( )` subshell. That is 7958 lines over the 293 declared fixtures on the distribution pass, plus 5615 over the 220 shipping fixtures on `cl_select`'s consumer-layout pass. So about 13,573 forks ran on every push that missed the whole-suite key. hermetic-run.sh sources the same span, so each declared unit's run paid it again for its own tools.
- The cut: the lookup now walks the search path with builtins. The fields are those of the IFS=: split, and the first match wins.
- Proof: a hand probe sourced the old and new spans side by side. `readset_decl_parse` over all 293 declared fixtures gave byte-identical output: 16394 lines, 7958 T rows. A synthetic search-path matrix also agreed on every row. It covered an empty path, `A:`, `A::B`, `:B`, `B:A`, `A:B:`, `::`, `:` and a missing dir, with a tool in two dirs, a non-executable first hit, an absent tool, and `tmp` to make an empty field reach `/tmp`.
- Mutants, both killed: dropping the first-match `break` failed the corpus and 3 matrix rows. Appending a trailing `:` failed 3 matrix rows.
- A `VS-IDENTITY 1` row pins the new span sha in both hooks. Every parse row is unchanged, so no stored entry changes meaning.
- Still owed: the other per-fixture forks of the parse (4 to 6 awk/cat per declared fixture) and of `readset_verdicts` (per run candidate), and the consumer layout's `bash <mapper> --map` start per shipping fixture with a `core/` path. The `select` figure on the push that delivers this cut is its measurement.

**PARTIAL (v0.1157.0) — (c)'s three named fork sites are cut; `select`'s unattributed fixed cost and the per-candidate readers inside READSET_VS stay owed.**

(c), both hooks, identical lines (I66):
- `readset_decl_parse` (READSET_KEYROWS span). One awk per declaration now writes the rows, the unmapped five-field rows, the mapper input and a flags file (a `core/` line; an F row spelled `/*/`), which builtins read back. The mapper's count check moved into the join awk's END. A builtin loop prints the rows and then the absent-path refusals, in the old order, so no `cat` remains. An optional 7th argument names a PREMAP (`<mapper input>\t<mapper output>` rows). A line the premap lacks, or no premap, starts the mapper for that fixture alone. hermetic-run.sh and invalidation-guard.sh pass 6 arguments, so their path is unchanged.
- `readset_declared` (consumer layout). It builds the premap from every listed fixture's declared lines, spelled as the parse sends them, through ONE mapper start. It does so only when two or more listed fixtures carry a `core/` line, the only case in which a start is saved: a list with one such fixture still starts the mapper once, and a list with none starts it zero times, as before. A mapper refusal or a count mismatch leaves no premap, and each fixture then starts the mapper alone, so its own `maprefuse` or `mapcount` row stands.
- `readset_verdicts`. One awk filters the candidates: a declared fixture with no `.dunres` row and no `.qfx` row, in `.kdec` order, with an unopenable `.qfx` read as empty. That replaces a `grep -qxF` and two awks per candidate.

  - `readset_verdicts`: 3 fewer execs per candidate. On r1146 that is about 215 candidates (dist plus `@consumer`), and on r1148 about 19.
- Proof, by hand probes, old against new on one base:

Two rows of the distribution run differ only against the edited tree: they are `spans.decl` hashes of `.githooks/pre-push#FIXTURE_POOL`, which encloses the edited span. Against a clean tree the run is identical.
  - P2: a 29-case matrix (nomap, maprefuse, mapcount, abs, `..`, plusbang, plusdir, glob, glob matching nothing, a `+` row, CRLF, absent file and directory, no `core/` line, a premap miss), both layouts, identical with and without the premap. Mapper starts 8 → 2; the 2 are the refusal and the premap miss falling back.
  - P4: `readset_declared`'s `.dparse`, `.dfx`, `.dunres` and `.dtools`, identical in the distribution, the installed tree, and an installed tree carrying one fixture the mapper refuses (the batch fails, every fixture falls back, its `maprefuse` row stands). In trimmed installed trees, mapper starts are 0 → 0 with no `core/` fixture, 1 → 1 with one, and 2 → 1 with two.
  - P3: `.vs.want` identical, counted and uncounted, with `.qfx` absent and present.
  - Mutants, all killed: no fallback on a premap miss (refuse and pmiss read `mapcount`); no mapcount check (rows instead of `mapcount`); no premap count check, under a mapper that drops a line (wrong rows instead of `mapcount`); `.qfx` ignored (`inqfx` reaches `.vs.want`).
- A `VS-IDENTITY 1` row pins the new READSET_* span sha in both hooks. Every parse row is byte-identical, so no stored entry changes meaning.

## BL-1133 — the push runs every selected `<fx>@consumer` twin, though a static join would make the one catch the twins have on record

**DEFECT.** OPEN. Filed 2026-11-15. Owner: `.githooks/pre-push` consumer-layout selection (`fixture_suite_step`, `run_fixtures`), with `scripts/predict-push-set.sh` mirroring it.

BL-1124 (e) measured the `@consumer` twin set: 823 unit runs and one catch event (pw2), about 302 s of wall clock per push at width 12 on r1135 (BL-1124's PARTIAL text). pw2's catch was a shipping shard driver declaring a `.dist-only` fixture's files, which a static join over the declarations finds without running the twins.

Fix: add that join (a shipping fixture's declarations name no `.dist-only` fixture's files) at the selection step, then skip a `<fx>@consumer` unit whose distribution twin passed on the same key and whose read set holds no layout-sensitive input; mirror the selection in `predict-push-set.sh`. Weigh the remaining twin set again against what it catches.

Provenance: batch 257, wave 6, the BL-1124 (e) remainder named by ai-dlc-48 (W6-13); id allocated by the lead.

**PARTIAL (v0.1145.0) — the distribution-twin skip shipped; the re-weigh of the remaining twin set stays owed to the push that delivers it.** `cl_select` (both hooks, identical lines under I66) now decides a `<fx>@consumer` unit `skip` with flag `d`, instead of `run` on a changed key, when two things hold. First, its distribution twin's `.kdec` row is a `skip`, meaning a pass is recorded on this tree's distribution key. Second, no key row that moved between the unit's prospective record and its placement record is layout-sensitive. Layout-sensitive rows are these: an added or removed row; a listing, tool or span row; any row under `tests/fixtures/`; the runner or the mapper; and any row that is not a byte copy of ITS OWN distribution source. That source is the declared path the consumer-layout parse (`.cl/.dparse`) mapped the row from, and its hash in the distribution `.now` must equal the row's. So a row no declaration maps runs, which covers every row of an undeclared unit, and so does a file `install.sh` renders over another path's bytes. A unit with a `.dunres` row never takes the skip. That row means a declared input the installed tree lacks, which has no key row on either side, so it stays selected and `hermetic-run.sh` refuses it. That refusal is the join the entry asked for: pw2's shape, a shard driver declaring `core/fixtures/adversarial-shard-merge-b/run.sh`, ran under a forced all-skip distribution decision in the hand probe. Every `d` unit is named on its own gate line. It publishes no record and stays out of `.k.always`, `.rskip` and `.vs.all`. Its PENDING count is still read from its old placement record. `scripts/predict-push-set.sh` hands its distribution decisions to `census-consumer-layout.sh --predict` through `AI_DLC_CL_DIST_OUT`, so the prediction takes the same skip. The census and `--only` never take it. Byte-identical code that resolves paths differently once installed is acquitted, and I33 owns that class (lead ruling D2). Owed: weigh the twin set the delivering push still runs against what it catches.

**PARTIAL (v0.1151.0).** Batch 257, W12-01, ai-dlc-5a. What the pushes since the skip shipped ran, read from the gate logs of 0.1145.0 to 0.1149.0 (every gate exit 0):

| push | `<fx>@consumer` run | `d` skips | red `@consumer` |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0.1145.0 | 207 of 220 | 12 | 0 |
| 0.1146.0 | 45 of 220 | 0 | 0 |
| 0.1147.0 | 13 of 220 | 0 | 0 |
| 0.1148.0 | 2 of 220 | 0 | 0 |
| 0.1149.0 | 7 of 220 | 10 | 0 |

The `ok … @consumer` rows in each log equal its run count, so no unit went unreported. The consumer `verdict store:` line, which joins store hits with unconfirmed-record skips, named 1 unit at 0.1145.0 and 38 at 0.1146.0, and none at the other three pushes. In the record, the last red `@consumer` unit is in the failure record of 2026-11-15T00:04:45Z, 20 units, which predates the skip. ## BL-1141 — five s322 step parts read "no measured value", and one sprint cannot decide whether they go

**DEFECT (efficiency).** OPEN. Filed 2026-11-15. Owner: the step text under `core/skills/ai-dlc/steps/` that dispatches each part; the fix names the file and line for each one.

BL-812's W9-18 step-value paragraph reads every label that rose on s322 on subagent dollars. Five parts carry no measured value there. Figures are s322, from `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s322/out/` at `EXTRACT_WIN_END=2026-11-12T14:41:00Z`.

| Part | s322 cost | s322 reading | Failure case it exists for |
|---|---|---|---|
| carry-over-evaluation p3 (part and cross) | 7 spawns, $13.10, plus a $0.29 deferral record (`co_yield_s322.txt`) | 2 MAJOR, both deferred to requirements and shipped unfixed | a defect the p2 repair wrote into the carry-over evaluation |
| `gate-validation[implementation]` gate QA | 4 seats, $10.45 (`gate_yield_s322.txt`) | 0 C/M and no rework on stories 1, 2, 3 and 5 | a story whose acceptance criteria fail after code review APPROVED |
| `gate-validation[architecture]` adjudicator shards | 10 spawns, $3.81 (`gate_yield_s322.txt`) | 10 checks, 0 FAIL | the architecture step advancing over a failing gate check |
| `gate-validation[retro]` adjudicator shards | 9 spawns, $3.49 (`tail_yield_s322.txt`) | 9 PASS, 0 FAIL | the retro step advancing over a failing gate check |
| requirements PRD validate run | 1 spawn, $1.28 (`req_yield_s322.txt`) | grade Excellent, 0 Critical, 2 Medium, 5 Low; no committed record joins it to a fix | a PRD defect reaching architecture |

"A step with no measured value goes" (`.claude/rules/operator-rulings.md`) decides each part, but one sprint never supports removing a pass. A zero-FAIL gate is also judged on consequence, because its catch is rare.

Fix: read the same five parts on s323 once it is complete, with the s322 readers ported to s323's parts and refs.
- A part with a catch on s323 is KEEP.
- A part with no measured value on both sprints is removed or narrowed in its step file, with the projected saving stated.
- For the two adjudicator parts, the reading also weighs consequence. A part judged KEEP on consequence names the failure it guards against.

Dependencies:
- None of the five needs extract.py's classifier, because each is a subagent part partitioned from `spawns.jsonl` by description.
- The s323 extraction does need the s322/s323 sprint edge in extract.py's EDGES, which W9-12 is adding, and the same edge in `lead_class_value.py`'s copy.

Baseline: s322

Expected movement: a projection to read s323 against, not a bar. A part with no measured value on s323 either goes or is narrowed, removing up to its s322 cost: $13.10, $10.45, $3.81, $3.49 and $1.28, $32.13 in all at s322's 8 stories. A part with a catch on s323 stays at its cost.

Provenance: batch 257, wave 9, the five s322 "no measured value" parts named by ai-dlc-cb (W9-18); id allocated by the lead.

## BL-1148 — while the main checkout holds an unlanded hook-editing squash, every other session's pregate is refused

**NOTE.** OPEN. Filed 2026-11-16. Owner: `scripts/pregate-self-check.sh` (HOOK-RESOLVE), with `.githooks/pre-push` (`pregate_gate`).

`core.hooksPath` is the main checkout's `.githooks`, so a push of a release that edits `.githooks/pre-push` runs the edit only once the main checkout holds it. The lead therefore detaches the main checkout at the unlanded squash for that push. Measured from the code at 5a38afcc9:
- `pregate-self-check.sh:143-147` resolves the hook a push would run with `git rev-parse --git-path hooks/pre-push`, which is the main checkout's hook in every worktree. `:148` compares it with the tree's own `.githooks/pre-push` by `cmp -s`, unless `--member` is given.
- In any other session's tree, still at `origin/main`, the resolved hook differs and the tree hook equals `origin/main:.githooks/pre-push`. That takes `:153`, "the resolved hook moved", which sets no remedy, and the script exits 2 at `:169`.
- `--member` skips the comparison but never writes the pregate record (`:44-48`). `pregate_gate` at `.githooks/pre-push:107-109` refuses any push whose tree has no record. While the window is open, no other session can produce a pushable tree.

Cost: every builder's pregate and push stalls for the hook-editing push's whole duration, from the detach to the landing and re-detach. The refusing builder reads "the resolved hook moved" with no remedy, so the stall reads like a defect in the builder's own tree. Who notices it gone: the builders who hold pregate slots during a hook-editing push, and the lead, who schedules those slots. No mechanism proposed. The record is keyed on the tree alone, `<root commit>/<HEAD tree>` (`.githooks/pre-push:74`, `:106`; written at `pregate-self-check.sh:531-534`). So the stall comes only from the comparison at `:148`, which refuses before any step runs. That comparison is what makes a record attest a run by the hook a push runs, so relaxing it is not a simple change.

Provenance: batch 257, wave 11, W11-01 item 1 (ai-dlc-9c); id allocated by the lead.

verify: manual

## BL-1155 — inputs.decl narrowings taken from sandbox windows before the STAT LOSS guard are undecided, and schema-install-fallback has no lossless window

**DEFECT.** OPEN. Filed 2026-11-16. Owner: the `inputs.decl` of each fixture listed below; `core/scripts/derive-fixture-readsets.sh` (the traced-run launch line) for the loss half.

Before 0.1146.0, a sandbox window that lost only stat reports was ACCEPTED (BL-1144). A declaration narrowed from such a window may omit a path the fixture reads. The hermetic runner holds a fixture to its declared inputs when it runs it (`core/scripts/hermetic-run.sh` header, "THE SANDBOX"). So an omitted path either fails that run, or goes unseen when the fixture tolerates its absence; the `!` sentinel catches the second case only for the lines it marks. Whether the gate runs a pure narrowing at all is BL-1151.

The population: `git log -p` of `core/fixtures/*/inputs.decl`, 9cad095fe^..99c662068 (0.1126.0, F19's traced narrowing, to 0.1149.0).
- 387 path lines were removed. Set aside: 9 lines of a fixture's own directory; 89 in fixtures since deleted; 29 declared again on the tip; 167 `core/fixtures/lib/mutant.sh` removals from the battery deletion; 24 whose file is deleted.
- That leaves 69 lines in 22 live fixtures, by commit. "Cites a trace" means the commit's added CHANGELOG or backlog prose names one; it is a text classification and does not show which window a line came from.
  - 9cad095fe (0.1126.0), cites a trace, 11 lines: procsub-staged-refusal-b and -c (`core/hooks/`, `core/session-driver/`, `core/fixtures/procsub-staged-refusal/`); self-update-gate and self-update-gate-d (`core/git-hooks/pre-push`); emit-report-refusal, reconcile-emit-report-b and -c (`core/fixtures/reconcile-emit-report/`).
  - 56877eb4d (0.1130.0), cites no trace, 19 lines, though owed-derivations' own `inputs.decl` comment cites one: apply-restamp-worklist-c (4 files); changelog-sprint-slot (`core/skills/ai-dlc/steps/requirements.md`); check-24-adversarial-convergence, -b and -c (`core/fixtures/check-24-adversarial-convergence-d/run.sh`); consumer-machinery-home, derived-fence-binding and layer-contract-conformance-b (`.githooks/`); owed-derivations (`core/scripts/sprint-status.sh`, `core/schemas/sprint-status.json`, `core/skills/ai-dlc-update/reconcile/`); prepush-pool-depth (5 files).
  - 13bb5e8c8 (0.1132.0), cites a trace, 3 lines: layer-contract-conformance-b (`templates/`, `patterns/`, `.claude/rules/`).
  - a03785229 (0.1133.0), cites a trace, 1 line: fixture-git-env-seam (`core/fixtures/*/inputs.decl`).
  - 39184970b (0.1134.0), cites a trace, 23 lines: backlog-real-ledger (both pre-push hooks, `core/scripts/`, 20 `scripts/` files).
  - ea8c9fb0c (0.1145.0), cites a trace, 2 lines: fixture-git-env-seam (`core/fixtures/`, `VERSION`).
  - 925d02f39 (0.1146.0), cites a trace, 9 lines: derived-fence-binding (`core/`, `scripts/`, `templates/`, `patterns/`, `.claude/rules/`, `core/fixtures/*/inputs.decl`); self-update-gate, -b and -c (`core/skills/ai-dlc-update/reconcile/`). The `inputs.decl` comments of both state their files from the code (seed.sh's copy list, `sg_copy`).
  - f0856fbc6 (0.1148.0), 1 line: layer-readopt-gate (`core/skills/ai-dlc-update/reconcile/`). It was traced under the guard and accepted, so it needs no re-trace.
- 28 `inputs.decl` files were created in the range already narrow (27 in 0.1130.0, 1 in 0.1135.0), all live. They have no removed line, and 3 cite a trace in their own comment: adversarial-shard-merge-b, -c and -d.
- Windows still on disk: only the f19 slot directories (`/private/tmp/ai-dlc-readset-sandbox-501-f19-s{1,2,4,5,6}`, scratch, not committed, on 0.112x trees). Replayed through the shipping READSET_STATPAIR span, with drop notices and the canary file:
  - Accepted under the guard: procsub-staged-refusal-b and -c (s1), self-update-gate (s1), self-update-gate-d (s4), derived-fence-binding (s1, s4, s5), owed-derivations (s2), backlog-real-ledger (s4, s6).
  - Refused: consumer-machinery-home (s4: 313 drops, STAT LOSS 1,447, canary 2); layer-contract-conformance-b (s1: 488 drops, STAT LOSS 158; s4: 517 drops, STAT LOSS 127); backlog-real-ledger (s5: 17 drops); layer-readopt-gate (s4: STAT LOSS 2).
  - None for fixture-git-env-seam.
  - Whether a window on disk is the one a line was narrowed from is not established.

The loss half covers both install-heavy fixtures, schema-install-fallback and install-scaffold-ignored. Each omits on every sandbox trace on record. Each window's `STAT LOSS` rows are `git` pids, one per run of `scripts/install.sh:24`'s whole-tree dirty probe (BL-1138); the run count is reconciled in BL-1134's LANDED line.
- schema-install-fallback, in W12-16's trace at 99c662068 (0.1149.0) with the 0.1154.0 deriver: 30 drop notices and `STAT LOSS: 10`.
- install-scaffold-ignored, in W12-17's trace at acd67c1ee (0.1152.0 with the 0.1154.0 deriver): 11 drop notices and `STAT LOSS: 1876`.

Superseded by BL-1134's LANDED line: their `core/` and `templates/` rows (and install-scaffold-ignored's `patterns/` row) in `docs/inputs-decl-whole-rows.tsv` are decided as `whole` while `:24` stands. They do not wait on an accepted window.

NOTE: `templates/audit-anchors.md.template` is not reported in either window. No `.win` line and no `.statpair` row names it, across 11 `:24` runs. Yet `diff-index` statted it: a clone of the traced copy reads `preload/sum_lstat:2407` against `cache_nr:2407`. The other 4 templates are reported. This may be a reporting gap, not a random loss.

Candidate, not built: a traced-run `core.preloadIndex=false` (for example `GIT_CONFIG_*` on the launch line), which reschedules the same lstats without changing what is read. Its burst rate under the profile is unmeasured, and a launch-line knob also rides `--in-pool`, which is the push's real run.

Owed:
- A guarded `--declared-out` re-trace of each listed fixture, in a trace slot the lead grants, deciding each line: still sufficient, or a path to restore.
- A measured fix for the install-heavy fixtures' loss (schema-install-fallback, install-scaffold-ignored), then their trace.

Provenance: batch 257, wave 12, W12-16 (ai-dlc-68), split out of BL-1144 on landing; id allocated by the lead.

## BL-1152 — P2 cannot see an in-process call of an UNGUARDED subject's functions after its counted source (the B route)

**DEFECT.** OPEN. Filed 2026-11-16. Owner: `scripts/validate-fixture-invocations.sh` P2 (`FXC_P2_AWK`, the counting unit and `fxc_guarded`); `core/fixtures/lib/mutant.sh` (the comment item below).

P2 counts an UNGUARDED subject with a shebang per SOURCE. After that source, a fixture can call the subject's functions any number of times, and P2 counts none of those calls, so they reach no `inproc:` row. The header of `scripts/validate-fixture-invocations.sh` names this the B route and calls it "the LARGER open route". The 312 call sites quoted there were measured on main 62a88119c; 221 of them were in a since-deleted fixture.

Re-measured on 99c662068 (0.1149.0) with a scratch variant of the shipping validator, run from inside a clean worktree of that commit. In the variant, `fxc_guarded` prints `G` for every subject whose first line opens `#!` and logs each file it flips. `bash scripts/validate-fixture-inprocess.sh` then lists, as OWED, every call of a flipped subject's column-0 function. That is the B route as P2 would count it under the per-CALL unit.
- 147 rows over 33 sites in 5 fixtures. Every one is a call of a `core/git-hooks/pre-push` function: 36 rows in each of readset-skip, -b, -c and -d (30 sites, all in `readset-skip/run.sh`), and 3 in consumer-suite-pool (`consumer-suite-pool/run.sh`, 3 sites).
- The fixtures behind the 312 figure are all gone: backlog-receipt-efficiency, story-evidence-receipts-mutants, story-evidence-smoke-mutants and smoke-surface-ignored-mutants each list 0 times in `git ls-tree -d HEAD core/fixtures/`, against readset-skip once.
- Not separated by this measurement: activation. readset-skip's runner sources an unresolvable operand (`. "$@"`), which activates every subject of the fixture, so some of its 144 rows may be phantoms of the kind BL-982's F8 ruling names (spans and drivers, no pass of the subject).

Owed:
1. Decide the B route's unit and close it: either count an unguarded subject's in-process function calls (and give the 147 rows their reasons, as BL-982 did for P2's OWED set), or rule the route out of scope.
2. The comment pair, moved here from BL-982. `core/fixtures/lib/mutant.sh`'s `fx_inproc` comment still says one call per SOURCE, and the header of `scripts/validate-fixture-invocations.sh` calls that comment superseded. Fix both together, in the first release that edits `lib/mutant.sh` for another reason. On its own, a comment edit to `lib/mutant.sh` re-runs every fixture that declares it.

Receipt: `verify: manual`. The route is open until owed item 1 is decided. A seeded post-source call of an unguarded subject's function in a scratch fixture must then read as a P2 row, or the ruling must be cited.

Provenance: batch 257, wave 12 (W12-03, ai-dlc-b2), carried from BL-982's owed items 2 and 3 by operator ruling; id allocated by the lead.

verify: manual


## BL-1153 — world adoption is bound by no arm: fixtures build their git worlds by hand instead of through lib/world.sh

## BL-1159 — P2 scans a fixture's file-declared libs but not a directory-declared one, so a directory line acquits an unrouted `. "$1"` in a borrowed lib

**NOTE.** OPEN. Filed 2026-11-16. Owner: unassigned (`scripts/validate-fixture-inprocess.sh` P2's population).

P2 judges the in-process drives in the files a fixture declares. A lib declared as a file line is scanned; the same lib under a directory line is not. So a fixture that borrows another fixture's lib through a directory line is acquitted of every unrouted drive in it, and narrowing that directory line to its files turns the drives into NEW rows.

Evidence, `bash scripts/validate-fixture-inprocess.sh` on scratch copies of 46c004676 (0.1159.0 before this entry), differing only in `core/fixtures/content-seal/inputs.decl`:
- `core/fixtures/adversarial-shard-merge/lib.sh` as a file line: rc 1, exactly 2 non-OWED rows, `FAIL P2 content-seal:adversarial-shard-merge/lib.sh:505` (`. "$1" || exit 90` in the mx runner; its `fx_inproc` at `:509` fires only for `merge-adversarial-shards.sh`) and `:2622` (`. "$1"` in `pcn2_call`, for `partition-document.sh`).
- `core/fixtures/adversarial-shard-merge/` as a directory line: rc 0, 0 non-OWED FAIL.
- main 07b013949: rc 0, 0 non-OWED FAIL, and no OWED row names `lib.sh:505` or `:2622`, so the drives are unjudged, not owed under another key.

Not fixed here, by the lead's ruling: content-seal keeps the directory line, and pairing the two drives with `fx_inproc` stays out of scope under BL-982's ruling.

Owed: decide whether P2's population includes the files under a declared directory, and measure the row set that change adds before shipping it.

Receipt: `verify: manual`. The fix is proved when a scratch fixture borrowing a lib with an unrouted `. "$1"` through a directory line reads that drive as a P2 row.

Provenance: batch 257, wave 12 (W12-17, ai-dlc-7c), from the 0.1159.0 pregate red; id allocated by the lead.

verify: manual



## BL-1160 — validate-adversarial-convergence.sh expands an empty SORTED array under set -u when no pass file carries a pass number

**NOTE.** OPEN. Filed 2026-11-16. Owner: unassigned (`core/scripts/validate-adversarial-convergence.sh` series ordering, `:294-297` and `:679`).

When every file in a series lacks a `pass<N>`/`p<N>` token, `SORTED` stays empty. `for f in "${SORTED[@]}"` at `:679` then aborts under bash 3.2's `set -u` with `SORTED[@]: unbound variable`. Every arm after `:679` is skipped, along with the closing `N convergence violation(s)` line. A series named `...-pass-1.md`, `...-pass-2.md` reaches this, because `order_key` (`:258`) does not admit the hyphen.

Evidence, on a file:// clone of the consumer at d6bc37b5a under `/bin/bash` 3.2.57, `AI_DLC_PROJECT_ROOT` set to the clone, `--series docs/reviews/s170/adversarial-review`:
- installed engine (consumer 0.1157.0, `scripts/ai-dlc/`): rc 1, two `FAIL (ORDER)` blocks, then `SORTED[@]: unbound variable`;
- distribution engine (0.1159.0, `core/scripts/` with its siblings): the same, and a `PS4` line trace puts the abort after `:677`, at the `:679` loop;
- `--cycle-state` on the same series: rc 1, empty stdout;
- the same series on a scratch copy with only `:679` guarded (`${SORTED[@]+"${SORTED[@]}"}`): gate mode rc 1, 0 `unbound` lines, the two `ORDER` findings, then `FAIL (D -- TERMINAL): the series ends at  with no verdict at all.` with an empty path, and `FAIL: 3 convergence violation(s).`; `--cycle-state` rc 1, empty stdout.

Consequence measured, crash against guard on the same input: the exit code is the same in both modes, and `--cycle-state` stdout is empty in both, so the hooks act the same way. Gate mode loses the summary line and shows an engine abort in the consumer's log. The guard alone is not the fix: past `:679` arm D judges an empty series and prints a finding with no file.

Owed: when `SORTED` is empty, exit after the `ORDER` findings with the summary, so arm D never runs on an empty series. Then add a fixture cell with an all-unorderable series that asserts no `unbound` line, no `D -- TERMINAL` row and the summary.

Receipt: `verify: manual`. The fix is proved when the all-unorderable series above prints its `ORDER` findings and the summary line under `/bin/bash` 3.2, with no `unbound variable` on stderr.

Provenance: batch 257, wave 13 (W13-02), reported by ai-dlc-94 from the consumer's s170 review; id allocated by the lead.

verify: manual
